not tracked not covered covered 
// Package config bundles what every view needs to know: the theme and the
// user settings. All views share one Config.
//
// The settings come from ~/.config/jdiff: main.toml holds the settings and the
// name of the theme, state.toml, written by jdiff, the toggles as last set.
// Each themes/<name>.toml is a theme: style and label options as in delta's
// git config, and named colors in a [colors] table. A theme leaves unset
// options to jdiff's defaults; without a name, delta's git config is the
// theme.
package config

import (
        "bytes"
        "errors"
        "fmt"
        "io/fs"
        "os"
        "path/filepath"
        "regexp"
        "slices"
        "strings"

        "github.com/BurntSushi/toml"
        "github.com/charmbracelet/x/ansi"

        "jdiff/internal/keys"
        "jdiff/internal/settings"
        "jdiff/internal/theme"
)

// Config is shared by pointer, so a setting changed in one view applies to
// all of them.
type Config struct {
        Theme    *theme.Theme
        Keys     *keys.Map // the keys of all pages, the built-in ones with the user's
        Settings settings.Settings
        Themes   []theme.Spec // all themes found; Theme is the selected one

        warnings  []string // problems with the files
        dir       string   // the configuration directory, empty for none
        statePath string   // where SaveState writes, empty for no state file

        marks marksState // jump marks, see LoadMarks

        watchIgnore []*regexp.Regexp // watch_ignore compiled, see WatchIgnored

        // AutoReload is whether what is shown is loaded again when the repository
        // changes; R toggles it. It is not saved, so it is off at each start, as
        // it ends by itself: see Settings.AutoReloadTimeout.
        AutoReload bool

        // Search is what / searched for, which . and , search again and the
        // views highlight; it is not saved.
        Search Search
}

// Query is a search of /: plain text, or with Regex a regular expression.
type Query struct {
        Text  string
        Regex bool
}

// Search is the state of /: the query searched for last, empty for none,
// and all searched for, oldest first.
type Search struct {
        Query
        History []Query
}

// Add makes q the query searched for, last in the history, once.
func (s *Search) Add(q Query) {
        s.Query = q
        s.History = append(slices.DeleteFunc(s.History, func(h Query) bool { return h == q }), q)
}

// Default returns the default theme and settings, without files.
func Default() *Config {
        c := &Config{Theme: theme.Default(), Keys: keys.Builtin(), Settings: settings.Default()}
        c.compileWatchIgnore(DefaultWatchIgnore)
        return c
}

// Dir returns jdiff's configuration directory: $XDG_CONFIG_HOME/jdiff, by
// default ~/.config/jdiff, on all systems.
func Dir() (string, error) {
        if dir := os.Getenv("XDG_CONFIG_HOME"); filepath.IsAbs(dir) {
                return filepath.Join(dir, "jdiff"), nil
        }
        home, err := os.UserHomeDir()
        if err != nil {
                return "", err
        }
        return filepath.Join(home, ".config", "jdiff"), nil
}

// minInterval and maxInterval bound watch_interval and auto_reload_interval,
// in seconds.
const (
        minInterval = 0.1
        maxInterval = 86400
)

// mainFile is the content of main.toml. WatchIgnore is not one of the
// settings, which views compare as a whole.
type mainFile struct {
        settings.Settings
        Theme string `toml:"theme"`
        // WatchIgnore (watch_ignore) are regular expressions of paths from the
        // top of the working tree, with / and directories with a / at the end:
        // while auto reload watches the working tree, a change of a file that
        // one matches does not run git status, and a directory that one matches
        // is not watched, nor those below it. See WatchIgnored.
        WatchIgnore []string `toml:"watch_ignore"`
}

// DefaultWatchIgnore is watch_ignore by default: editors' files beside the
// one saved, Vim's swap files, backups and the file it tries writing (4913),
// Emacs' locks and autosaves.
var DefaultWatchIgnore = []string{`\.sw[a-px]$`, `~$`, `(^|/)\.#`, `(^|/)#[^/]*#$`, `(^|/)4913$`, `\.tmp$`}

// Load reads the files in dir over the default settings and selects the
// theme. The themes are jdiff's defaults ("default"), delta's git config
// gitCfg with the features env (DELTA_FEATURES) and flag (--features) select
// ("delta"), and the theme files; main.toml selects one by name, else it is
// "delta". Missing files are skipped and unknown keys reported by Warning;
// files that cannot be parsed are an error, unless they are theme files not
// selected. An empty dir reads no files.
func Load(dir string, gitCfg theme.Config, env, flag string) (*Config, error) {
        c := &Config{Keys: keys.Builtin(), Settings: settings.Default()}
        c.Themes = []theme.Spec{theme.Builtin(), theme.FromGit(gitCfg, theme.Features(gitCfg, env, flag))}
        name, ignore := "", DefaultWatchIgnore
        if dir != "" {
                c.dir = dir
                c.statePath = filepath.Join(dir, "state.toml")
                m := mainFile{Settings: c.Settings, WatchIgnore: slices.Clone(DefaultWatchIgnore)}
                if err := c.decode(filepath.Join(dir, "main.toml"), &m); err != nil {
                        return nil, err
                }
                c.Settings, ignore = m.Settings, m.WatchIgnore
                c.checkIntervals()
                // Lines are cut and wrapped by columns: the symbols take one.
                def := settings.Default()
                for _, sym := range []struct {
                        key      string
                        v        *string
                        fallback string
                }{
                        {"line_end", &c.Settings.LineEnd, def.LineEnd}, {"cr_end", &c.Settings.CREnd, def.CREnd},
                        {"extends", &c.Settings.Extends, def.Extends}, {"precedes", &c.Settings.Precedes, def.Precedes},
                } {
                        if ansi.StringWidth(*sym.v) != 1 {
                                c.warnings = append(c.warnings, fmt.Sprintf("main.toml: %s %q is not one column wide, %q is used",
                                        sym.key, *sym.v, sym.fallback))
                                *sym.v = sym.fallback
                        }
                }
                // state.toml holds only the toggles. Earlier versions kept untracked
                // there too: it is left out, without a warning.
                state := struct {
                        settings.Toggles
                        Untracked bool `toml:"untracked"`
                }{Toggles: c.Settings.Toggles}
                if err := c.decode(c.statePath, &state); err != nil {
                        return nil, err
                }
                c.Settings.Toggles = state.Toggles
                // inotify_max_dirs is a toggle: it may come from main.toml or
                // state.toml, so the warning names neither.
                if n := c.Settings.InotifyMaxDirs; n < 0 {
                        c.warnings = append(c.warnings, fmt.Sprintf("inotify_max_dirs %d is negative, it is off", n))
                        c.Settings.InotifyMaxDirs = 0
                }
                var problems []string
                if c.Keys, problems = keys.Load(dir); len(problems) > 0 {
                        c.warnings = append(c.warnings, keys.FileName+": "+strings.Join(problems, "; ")+"; the built-in keys are used")
                }
                name = m.Theme
                if err := c.readThemes(filepath.Join(dir, "themes"), name); err != nil {
                        return nil, err
                }
        }
        c.Theme = theme.Resolve(c.selectTheme(name, dir))
        c.compileWatchIgnore(ignore)
        return c, nil
}

// checkIntervals clamps watch_interval and auto_reload_interval of main.toml
// to what works, with a warning for each.
func (c *Config) checkIntervals() {
        if iv := c.Settings.WatchInterval; iv < 0 {
                c.warnings = append(c.warnings, fmt.Sprintf("main.toml: watch_interval %v is negative, files are not watched", iv))
                c.Settings.WatchInterval = 0
        }
        // Shorter checks keep a core busy; longer ones overflow the
        // durations they become.
        if iv := c.Settings.WatchInterval; iv > 0 && iv < minInterval {
                c.warnings = append(c.warnings, fmt.Sprintf("main.toml: watch_interval %v is below %v, %v is used", iv, minInterval, minInterval))
                c.Settings.WatchInterval = minInterval
        }
        if iv := c.Settings.WatchInterval; iv > maxInterval {
                c.warnings = append(c.warnings, fmt.Sprintf("main.toml: watch_interval %v is above %d, %d is used", iv, maxInterval, maxInterval))
                c.Settings.WatchInterval = maxInterval
        }
        if iv := c.Settings.AutoReloadInterval; iv > maxInterval {
                c.warnings = append(c.warnings, fmt.Sprintf("main.toml: auto_reload_interval %d is above %d, %d is used", iv, maxInterval, maxInterval))
                c.Settings.AutoReloadInterval = maxInterval
        }
}

// compileWatchIgnore compiles exprs, watch_ignore, for WatchIgnored; one that
// is no regular expression is left out with a warning.
func (c *Config) compileWatchIgnore(exprs []string) {
        c.watchIgnore = nil
        for _, expr := range exprs {
                re, err := regexp.Compile(expr)
                if err != nil {
                        c.warnings = append(c.warnings, fmt.Sprintf("main.toml: watch_ignore %q is not a regular expression, left out: %v", expr, err))
                        continue
                }
                c.watchIgnore = append(c.watchIgnore, re)
        }
}

// WatchIgnored reports whether a pattern of watch_ignore matches rel, a path
// from the top of the working tree with /, of a directory with a / at the
// end.
func (c *Config) WatchIgnored(rel string) bool {
        return slices.ContainsFunc(c.watchIgnore, func(re *regexp.Regexp) bool { return re.MatchString(rel) })
}

// readThemes adds the theme files in dir to the themes. A file that cannot
// be parsed is an error when it is the selected theme, else a warning, and
// unknown keys are reported for the selected theme only.
func (c *Config) readThemes(dir, selected string) error {
        paths, _ := filepath.Glob(filepath.Join(dir, "*.toml"))
        for _, path := range paths {
                name := strings.TrimSuffix(filepath.Base(path), ".toml")
                if c.theme(name) != nil {
                        c.warnings = append(c.warnings, fmt.Sprintf("%s: %q is a built-in theme, file ignored", filepath.Base(path), name))
                        continue
                }
                sp, unknown, err := theme.FromFile(path)
                if err != nil {
                        err = fmt.Errorf("%s: %w", path, err)
                        if name == selected {
                                return err
                        }
                        c.warnings = append(c.warnings, err.Error())
                        continue
                }
                if name == selected {
                        c.unknown(path, unknown)
                }
                c.Themes = append(c.Themes, sp)
        }
        return nil
}

// selectTheme returns the theme called name, "delta" for "", or with a
// warning "delta" when there is none.
func (c *Config) selectTheme(name, dir string) theme.Spec {
        if name == "" {
                name = "delta"
        }
        if sp := c.theme(name); sp != nil {
                return *sp
        }
        c.warnings = append(c.warnings, fmt.Sprintf("theme %q: no file %s", name, filepath.Join(dir, "themes", name+".toml")))
        return *c.theme("delta")
}

// theme returns the theme called name, or nil.
func (c *Config) theme(name string) *theme.Spec {
        for i := range c.Themes {
                if c.Themes[i].Name == name {
                        return &c.Themes[i]
                }
        }
        return nil
}

// decode reads the TOML file path into v, if it exists, and records its
// unknown keys.
func (c *Config) decode(path string, v any) error {
        md, err := toml.DecodeFile(path, v)
        switch {
        case errors.Is(err, fs.ErrNotExist):
                return nil
        case err != nil:
                return fmt.Errorf("%s: %w", path, err)
        }
        var unknown []string
        for _, k := range md.Undecoded() {
                unknown = append(unknown, k.String())
        }
        c.unknown(path, unknown)
        return nil
}

// unknown reports the unknown keys of the file path.
func (c *Config) unknown(path string, keys []string) {
        if len(keys) > 0 {
                slices.Sort(keys)
                c.warnings = append(c.warnings, fmt.Sprintf("%s: unknown %s", filepath.Base(path), strings.Join(keys, ", ")))
        }
}

// SaveState writes the toggled settings to state.toml, through a temporary
// file so that the file is never left half written.
func (c *Config) SaveState() error {
        if c.statePath == "" {
                return nil
        }
        return writeTOML(c.statePath, "# Written by jdiff: the toggles as last set, over main.toml.\n", c.Settings.Toggles)
}

// writeTOML writes header and v as TOML to path, through a temporary file
// so that the file is never left half written. Each write has a temporary
// file of its own: jdiffs running at the same time must not rename each
// other's.
func writeTOML(path, header string, v any) error {
        var b bytes.Buffer
        b.WriteString(header)
        if err := toml.NewEncoder(&b).Encode(v); err != nil {
                return err
        }
        if err := os.MkdirAll(filepath.Dir(path), 0o755); err != nil {
                return err
        }
        tmp, err := os.CreateTemp(filepath.Dir(path), filepath.Base(path)+".*.tmp")
        if err != nil {
                return err
        }
        _, err = tmp.Write(b.Bytes())
        err = errors.Join(err, tmp.Close(), os.Chmod(tmp.Name(), 0o644))
        if err == nil {
                err = os.Rename(tmp.Name(), path)
        }
        if err != nil {
                os.Remove(tmp.Name())
        }
        return err
}

// Dir returns the configuration directory the files were read from, "" for
// none.
func (c *Config) Dir() string { return c.dir }

// Warning returns the problems with the theme and the files, or "".
func (c *Config) Warning() string {
        var parts []string
        if len(c.Theme.Warnings) > 0 {
                parts = append(parts, "theme: "+strings.Join(c.Theme.Warnings, "; "))
        }
        return strings.Join(append(parts, c.warnings...), "; ")
}

package config

import (
        "cmp"
        "errors"
        "fmt"
        "io/fs"
        "path/filepath"
        "slices"

        "github.com/BurntSushi/toml"
)

// Mark is a jump mark: a line of the new side of a file.
type Mark struct {
        Path string `toml:"path"` // relative to the repository, absolute outside one
        Line int    `toml:"line"` // 1-based
        Text string `toml:"text"` // the line when the mark was set, to find it again
}

// LetterMark is a mark with its letter.
type LetterMark struct {
        Letter string
        Mark
}

// marksState holds the jump marks of marks.toml: a table per repository,
// keyed by its top directory, "" outside repositories.
type marksState struct {
        path string                     // marks.toml, empty for no file
        repo string                     // the current repository
        all  map[string]map[string]Mark // the marks of all repositories
}

// LoadMarks reads the jump marks of repository repo, its top directory or ""
// outside repositories, from marks.toml in the configuration directory. A
// file that cannot be parsed is reported by Warning and replaced by the next
// change of a mark.
func (c *Config) LoadMarks(repo string) {
        c.marks = marksState{repo: repo, all: map[string]map[string]Mark{}}
        if c.dir == "" {
                return
        }
        c.marks.path = filepath.Join(c.dir, "marks.toml")
        if _, err := toml.DecodeFile(c.marks.path, &c.marks.all); err != nil && !errors.Is(err, fs.ErrNotExist) {
                c.warnings = append(c.warnings, fmt.Sprintf("marks.toml: %v; marks are lost on the next change", err))
                c.marks.all = map[string]map[string]Mark{}
        }
}

// Repo returns the repository whose marks are used, "" outside one.
func (c *Config) Repo() string { return c.marks.repo }

// Mark returns the mark of letter in the current repository.
func (c *Config) Mark(letter string) (Mark, bool) {
        m, ok := c.marks.all[c.marks.repo][letter]
        return m, ok
}

// Marks returns the marks of the current repository, sorted by letter.
func (c *Config) Marks() []LetterMark {
        var out []LetterMark
        for letter, m := range c.marks.all[c.marks.repo] {
                out = append(out, LetterMark{letter, m})
        }
        slices.SortFunc(out, func(a, b LetterMark) int { return cmp.Compare(a.Letter, b.Letter) })
        return out
}

// SetMark sets the mark of letter in the current repository and saves the
// marks.
func (c *Config) SetMark(letter string, m Mark) error {
        c.reloadMarks()
        if c.marks.all == nil {
                c.marks.all = map[string]map[string]Mark{}
        }
        if c.marks.all[c.marks.repo] == nil {
                c.marks.all[c.marks.repo] = map[string]Mark{}
        }
        c.marks.all[c.marks.repo][letter] = m
        return c.saveMarks()
}

// DeleteMark deletes the mark of letter in the current repository and saves
// the marks.
func (c *Config) DeleteMark(letter string) error {
        c.reloadMarks()
        delete(c.marks.all[c.marks.repo], letter)
        if len(c.marks.all[c.marks.repo]) == 0 {
                delete(c.marks.all, c.marks.repo)
        }
        return c.saveMarks()
}

// reloadMarks reads the marks of all repositories again before a change, so
// that it keeps those another jdiff saved since this one read them. A file
// that cannot be parsed leaves the marks as they are, see LoadMarks.
func (c *Config) reloadMarks() {
        if c.marks.path == "" {
                return
        }
        all := map[string]map[string]Mark{}
        if _, err := toml.DecodeFile(c.marks.path, &all); err == nil || errors.Is(err, fs.ErrNotExist) {
                c.marks.all = all
        }
}

func (c *Config) saveMarks() error {
        if c.marks.path == "" {
                return nil
        }
        return writeTOML(c.marks.path, "# Written by jdiff: jump marks per repository.\n", c.marks.all)
}

// Package diff aligns two files line by line for side-by-side display.
//
// Lines are matched on their content with surrounding whitespace removed, so
// re-indented code still lines up with its original. User-chosen anchors force
// specific line pairs to line up and split the matching into independent
// sections.
package diff

import "strings"

// Kind classifies a display row.
type Kind int

const (
        Equal  Kind = iota // both lines identical
        Change             // both lines present but different
        Delete             // only the left line is present
        Insert             // only the right line is present
)

// Anchor forces line Left of the old file to line up with line Right of the
// new file. Both are 0-based.
type Anchor struct{ Left, Right int }

// Span is a byte range [Start, End) within a line.
type Span struct{ Start, End int }

// Row is one display row of the side-by-side view. The emphasized parts of
// a Change row are not part of it: Intraline computes them for the rows
// shown, as most rows of a large change never are.
type Row struct {
        Kind        Kind
        Left, Right int  // 0-based line indices, -1 when absent
        Anchored    bool // the row is a user anchor
}

// Align computes the display rows for old lines a and new lines b. anchors
// must be sorted by Left and must not cross (see Conflicts).
func Align(a, b []string, anchors []Anchor) []Row {
        al := aligner{a: a, b: b, ka: trimmed(a), kb: trimmed(b)}
        pa, pb := 0, 0
        for _, an := range anchors {
                al.section(pa, an.Left, pb, an.Right)
                al.addPair(an.Left, an.Right, true)
                pa, pb = an.Left+1, an.Right+1
        }
        al.section(pa, len(a), pb, len(b))
        return al.rows
}

// aligner accumulates the rows of one alignment.
type aligner struct {
        a, b, ka, kb []string
        rows         []Row
}

// Conflicts returns the anchors that cannot coexist with n, because they
// share a line with it or would cross it.
func Conflicts(anchors []Anchor, n Anchor) []Anchor {
        var out []Anchor
        for _, an := range anchors {
                dl, dr := an.Left-n.Left, an.Right-n.Right
                if dl == 0 || dr == 0 || (dl < 0) != (dr < 0) {
                        out = append(out, an)
                }
        }
        return out
}

// Stats counts the added and removed lines of an alignment. A changed line
// counts as both.
func Stats(rows []Row) (added, removed int) {
        for _, r := range rows {
                switch r.Kind {
                case Change:
                        added, removed = added+1, removed+1
                case Insert:
                        added++
                case Delete:
                        removed++
                }
        }
        return added, removed
}

func trimmed(lines []string) []string {
        out := make([]string, len(lines))
        for i, l := range lines {
                out[i] = strings.TrimSpace(l)
        }
        return out
}

// section appends the rows for a[a0:a1] against b[b0:b1].
func (al *aligner) section(a0, a1, b0, b1 int) {
        i, j := a0, b0
        for _, p := range patience(al.ka[a0:a1], al.kb[b0:b1]) {
                al.gap(i, a0+p.a, j, b0+p.b)
                al.addPair(a0+p.a, b0+p.b, false)
                i, j = a0+p.a+1, b0+p.b+1
        }
        al.gap(i, a1, j, b1)
}

// gap appends rows for unmatched lines. Lines are paired by position as
// changes; the remainder becomes plain deletions or insertions.
func (al *aligner) gap(i0, i1, j0, j1 int) {
        for i0 < i1 && j0 < j1 {
                al.addPair(i0, j0, false)
                i0, j0 = i0+1, j0+1
        }
        for ; i0 < i1; i0++ {
                al.rows = append(al.rows, Row{Kind: Delete, Left: i0, Right: -1})
        }
        for ; j0 < j1; j0++ {
                al.rows = append(al.rows, Row{Kind: Insert, Left: -1, Right: j0})
        }
}

// addPair appends the row of line l of a next to line r of b.
func (al *aligner) addPair(l, r int, anchored bool) {
        row := Row{Kind: Equal, Left: l, Right: r, Anchored: anchored}
        if al.a[l] != al.b[r] {
                row.Kind = Change
        }
        al.rows = append(al.rows, row)
}

package diff

import (
        "unicode"
        "unicode/utf8"
)

// maxLineDistance is the share of changed content above which two paired
// lines count as unrelated and get no emphasis (delta uses the same default).
const maxLineDistance = 0.6

// MaxEmphLine is the longest line, in bytes, whose changed words are
// emphasized: comparing the words of longer ones, as of minified code, takes
// tens of milliseconds a line.
const MaxEmphLine = 4096

type token struct {
        text       string
        start, end int
}

// Intraline returns the parts of a and b that differ, at word granularity.
// Leading whitespace is its own token, so an indentation change emphasizes
// just the indentation; with splitIndent each indentation character is a
// token, and runs of whitespace next to the same unchanged words keep what
// they have in common, so only the added or removed part is emphasized. Both
// results are nil when the lines are too different for emphasis to be
// useful, or one of them is longer than MaxEmphLine.
func Intraline(a, b string, splitIndent bool) (ea, eb []Span) {
        if len(a) > MaxEmphLine || len(b) > MaxEmphLine {
                return nil, nil
        }
        ta, tb := tokenize(a, splitIndent), tokenize(b, splitIndent)
        ma := make([]bool, len(ta))
        mb := make([]bool, len(tb))
        texts := func(ts []token) []string {
                out := make([]string, len(ts))
                for i, t := range ts {
                        out[i] = t.text
                }
                return out
        }
        pairs := myers(texts(ta), texts(tb))
        for _, p := range pairs {
                ma[p.a], mb[p.b] = true, true
        }
        // ka and kb hold the bytes at the start and the end of an unmatched token
        // that are the same as on the other side.
        ka, kb := make([]kept, len(ta)), make([]kept, len(tb))
        if splitIndent {
                prev := pair{-1, -1}
                for _, p := range append(pairs, pair{len(ta), len(tb)}) {
                        keepWhitespace(ta[prev.a+1:p.a], tb[prev.b+1:p.b], ka[prev.a+1:p.a], kb[prev.b+1:p.b])
                        prev = p
                }
        }

        changedA, totalA := contentBytes(ta, ma, ka)
        changedB, totalB := contentBytes(tb, mb, kb)
        if total := totalA + totalB; total > 0 &&
                float64(changedA+changedB)/float64(total) > maxLineDistance {
                return nil, nil
        }
        return spans(ta, ma, ka), spans(tb, mb, kb)
}

// kept is the unchanged start and end of a token, in bytes.
type kept struct{ start, end int }

// keepWhitespace finds what the unmatched tokens a and b between the same
// two matches have in common in whitespace, into ka and kb: a whitespace
// run that starts both keeps their common start, one that ends both their
// common end, as words inserted into padding take its place. A run that is
// all of one side pairs with the run of the other that has more in common.
func keepWhitespace(a, b []token, ka, kb []kept) {
        if len(a) == 0 || len(b) == 0 {
                return
        }
        common := func(x, y string, fromEnd bool) int {
                n := 0
                for n < len(x) && n < len(y) {
                        i, j := n, n
                        if fromEnd {
                                i, j = len(x)-1-n, len(y)-1-n
                        }
                        if x[i] != y[j] {
                                break
                        }
                        n++
                }
                return n
        }
        first, last := 0, 0
        if isSpace(a[0].text) && isSpace(b[0].text) {
                first = common(a[0].text, b[0].text, false)
        }
        la, lb := len(a)-1, len(b)-1
        if isSpace(a[la].text) && isSpace(b[lb].text) {
                last = common(a[la].text, b[lb].text, true)
        }
        switch {
        case la > 0 && lb > 0: // distinct first and last runs: both
                ka[0].start, kb[0].start = first, first
                ka[la].end, kb[lb].end = last, last
        case last > first:
                ka[la].end, kb[lb].end = last, last
        default:
                ka[0].start, kb[0].start = first, first
        }
}

// contentBytes counts the changed and total bytes, leaving out indentation so
// that re-indented lines do not look unrelated. keep is the unchanged start
// and end of each token, see keepWhitespace.
func contentBytes(ts []token, matched []bool, keep []kept) (changed, total int) {
        indent := true
        for i, t := range ts {
                if indent = indent && isSpace(t.text); indent {
                        continue
                }
                total += len(t.text)
                if !matched[i] {
                        changed += max(len(t.text)-keep[i].start-keep[i].end, 0)
                }
        }
        return changed, total
}

// spans returns the parts of the tokens ts that changed: the unmatched ones
// without their unchanged start and end keep.
func spans(ts []token, matched []bool, keep []kept) []Span {
        var out []Span
        for i, t := range ts {
                start, end := t.start+keep[i].start, t.end-keep[i].end
                if matched[i] || start >= end {
                        continue
                }
                if n := len(out); n > 0 && out[n-1].End == start {
                        out[n-1].End = end
                } else {
                        out = append(out, Span{start, end})
                }
        }
        return out
}

// tokenize splits s into runs of word characters, runs of whitespace and
// single other characters. With splitIndent, each character of the leading
// whitespace is a token of its own.
func tokenize(s string, splitIndent bool) []token {
        var out []token
        indent := splitIndent
        for i := 0; i < len(s); {
                r, n := utf8.DecodeRuneInString(s[i:])
                j := i + n
                class := runeClass(r)
                if indent = indent && class == classSpace; indent {
                        out = append(out, token{s[i:j], i, j})
                        i = j
                        continue
                }
                if class != classSingle {
                        for j < len(s) {
                                r2, n2 := utf8.DecodeRuneInString(s[j:])
                                if runeClass(r2) != class {
                                        break
                                }
                                j += n2
                        }
                }
                out = append(out, token{s[i:j], i, j})
                i = j
        }
        return out
}

// class is the kind of character a token is made of.
type class int

const (
        classSingle class = iota // characters that form a token on their own
        classWord
        classSpace
)

func runeClass(r rune) class {
        switch {
        case r == '_' || unicode.IsLetter(r) || unicode.IsDigit(r):
                return classWord
        case unicode.IsSpace(r):
                return classSpace
        }
        return classSingle
}

func isSpace(s string) bool {
        r, _ := utf8.DecodeRuneInString(s)
        return runeClass(r) == classSpace
}

package diff

// pair is a matched pair of indices into the two compared sequences.
type pair struct{ a, b int }

// maxEditDistance bounds the Myers search. Beyond it the inputs are treated
// as unrelated, which keeps memory at O(D²) for pathological inputs.
const maxEditDistance = 2000

// patience matches a against b using the patience algorithm: lines that are
// unique in both inputs become fixed points, the gaps between them are
// matched recursively, and gaps without unique lines fall back to Myers.
func patience(a, b []string) []pair {
        var out []pair
        patienceRec(a, b, 0, 0, &out)
        return out
}

func patienceRec(a, b []string, ao, bo int, out *[]pair) {
        for len(a) > 0 && len(b) > 0 && a[0] == b[0] {
                *out = append(*out, pair{ao, bo})
                a, b = a[1:], b[1:]
                ao, bo = ao+1, bo+1
        }
        suffix := 0
        for suffix < len(a) && suffix < len(b) && a[len(a)-1-suffix] == b[len(b)-1-suffix] {
                suffix++
        }
        a, b = a[:len(a)-suffix], b[:len(b)-suffix]

        if len(a) > 0 && len(b) > 0 {
                fixed := uniqueLIS(a, b)
                if len(fixed) == 0 {
                        for _, p := range myers(a, b) {
                                *out = append(*out, pair{ao + p.a, bo + p.b})
                        }
                } else {
                        pa, pb := 0, 0
                        for _, p := range fixed {
                                patienceRec(a[pa:p.a], b[pb:p.b], ao+pa, bo+pb, out)
                                *out = append(*out, pair{ao + p.a, bo + p.b})
                                pa, pb = p.a+1, p.b+1
                        }
                        patienceRec(a[pa:], b[pb:], ao+pa, bo+pb, out)
                }
        }

        for i := range suffix {
                *out = append(*out, pair{ao + len(a) + i, bo + len(b) + i})
        }
}

// uniqueLIS returns the longest sequence of lines that occur exactly once in
// both a and b and appear in the same order in both.
func uniqueLIS(a, b []string) []pair {
        type count struct{ a, b, bi int }
        counts := make(map[string]*count)
        for _, s := range a {
                c := counts[s]
                if c == nil {
                        c = &count{}
                        counts[s] = c
                }
                c.a++
        }
        for i, s := range b {
                if c := counts[s]; c != nil {
                        c.b++
                        c.bi = i
                }
        }
        var cand []pair
        for i, s := range a {
                if c := counts[s]; c.a == 1 && c.b == 1 {
                        cand = append(cand, pair{i, c.bi})
                }
        }
        if len(cand) == 0 {
                return nil
        }

        // Patience sorting: tails[k] is the index into cand of the smallest b
        // ending an increasing run of length k+1.
        var tails []int
        prev := make([]int, len(cand))
        for i, p := range cand {
                lo, hi := 0, len(tails)
                for lo < hi {
                        mid := (lo + hi) / 2
                        if cand[tails[mid]].b < p.b {
                                lo = mid + 1
                        } else {
                                hi = mid
                        }
                }
                prev[i] = -1
                if lo > 0 {
                        prev[i] = tails[lo-1]
                }
                if lo == len(tails) {
                        tails = append(tails, i)
                } else {
                        tails[lo] = i
                }
        }
        lis := make([]pair, len(tails))
        for i, k := len(tails)-1, tails[len(tails)-1]; i >= 0; i, k = i-1, prev[k] {
                lis[i] = cand[k]
        }
        return lis
}

// myers returns a longest common subsequence of a and b as matched index
// pairs, or nil when the edit distance exceeds maxEditDistance.
func myers[T comparable](a, b []T) []pair {
        n, m := len(a), len(b)
        if n == 0 || m == 0 {
                return nil
        }
        limit := min(n+m, maxEditDistance)
        off := limit + 1
        v := make([]int, 2*limit+3)
        // trace[d] holds v[-d..d] as it was before step d.
        var trace [][]int
        for d := 0; d <= limit; d++ {
                trace = append(trace, append([]int(nil), v[off-d:off+d+1]...))
                for k := -d; k <= d; k += 2 {
                        var x int
                        if k == -d || (k != d && v[off+k-1] < v[off+k+1]) {
                                x = v[off+k+1]
                        } else {
                                x = v[off+k-1] + 1
                        }
                        y := x - k
                        for x < n && y < m && a[x] == b[y] {
                                x, y = x+1, y+1
                        }
                        v[off+k] = x
                        if x >= n && y >= m {
                                return myersBacktrack(trace, n, m)
                        }
                }
        }
        return nil
}

func myersBacktrack(trace [][]int, n, m int) []pair {
        var out []pair
        x, y := n, m
        for d := len(trace) - 1; d > 0; d-- {
                v := trace[d]
                at := func(k int) int { return v[k+d] }
                k := x - y
                var pk int
                if k == -d || (k != d && at(k-1) < at(k+1)) {
                        pk = k + 1
                } else {
                        pk = k - 1
                }
                px := at(pk)
                py := px - pk
                for x > px && y > py {
                        x, y = x-1, y-1
                        out = append(out, pair{x, y})
                }
                x, y = px, py
        }
        for x > 0 && y > 0 {
                x, y = x-1, y-1
                out = append(out, pair{x, y})
        }
        for i, j := 0, len(out)-1; i < j; i, j = i+1, j-1 {
                out[i], out[j] = out[j], out[i]
        }
        return out
}

package diff

import (
        "strings"
        "unicode"
)

// MoveInput is one file of the files searched for moved code, such as the
// files of one commit: its old and new lines and their alignment.
type MoveInput struct {
        Old, New []string
        Rows     []Row
}

// Ref is a line of one of the files: the index of the file and of the line.
type Ref struct{ File, Line int }

// Moves are the moved lines of the files, per file: From maps removed lines
// (indices into Old) to the lines they moved to, To maps added lines
// (indices into New) to the lines they came from.
type Moves struct {
        From, To []map[int]Ref
}

// minMovedAlnum is the number of letters and digits a block needs to count
// as moved, as in git, so that lone braces and the like are not marked.
const minMovedAlnum = 20

// maxCommonStarts is how many places a block may start at among the added
// lines when its first two lines have fewer than minMovedAlnum letters and
// digits, as a closing brace and a blank line: found in thousands of places
// in a large change, trying them all from each removed pair took seconds.
const maxCommonStarts = 64

// FindMoves finds blocks of consecutive removed lines that were added again
// as consecutive lines, in the same file or another one. Lines are compared
// without surrounding whitespace, as Align matches them. Lines of an Equal
// row are neither removed nor added, and the two lines of a Change row are a
// change in place, not a move. Blocks are taken in file and line order, the
// longest at each line first; each line belongs to at most one block. It is
// one function on purpose: its closures share the index of the added lines
// and the lines already in a block, so it reads top to bottom; should it
// grow, a struct holding that state is the way to split it.
func FindMoves(files []MoveInput) Moves {
        type side struct {
                row  map[int]int // line -> row, for removed or added lines only
                used map[int]bool
        }
        removed := make([]side, len(files))
        added := make([]side, len(files))
        candidates := map[string][]Ref{} // added lines by their key
        // Added lines followed by another added line, by the keys of both: the
        // starts of blocks of two lines or more, so that a line found in many
        // places, like a blank one, is not tried against all of them.
        pairs := map[string][]Ref{}
        pairKey := func(lines []string, line int) string {
                return strings.TrimSpace(lines[line]) + "\n" + strings.TrimSpace(lines[line+1])
        }
        for i, f := range files {
                removed[i] = side{row: map[int]int{}, used: map[int]bool{}}
                added[i] = side{row: map[int]int{}, used: map[int]bool{}}
                for r, row := range f.Rows {
                        if row.Kind == Equal {
                                continue
                        }
                        if row.Left >= 0 {
                                removed[i].row[row.Left] = r
                        }
                        if row.Right >= 0 {
                                added[i].row[row.Right] = r
                        }
                }
                for line := range f.New {
                        if _, ok := added[i].row[line]; ok {
                                key := strings.TrimSpace(f.New[line])
                                candidates[key] = append(candidates[key], Ref{i, line})
                                if _, ok := added[i].row[line+1]; ok {
                                        pk := pairKey(f.New, line)
                                        pairs[pk] = append(pairs[pk], Ref{i, line})
                                }
                        }
                }
        }

        // matches reports whether removed line l of file i can move to added
        // line to.
        matches := func(i, l int, to Ref) bool {
                rr, ok := removed[i].row[l]
                if !ok || removed[i].used[l] {
                        return false
                }
                ar, ok := added[to.File].row[to.Line]
                if !ok || added[to.File].used[to.Line] || (to.File == i && ar == rr) {
                        return false
                }
                return strings.TrimSpace(files[i].Old[l]) == strings.TrimSpace(files[to.File].New[to.Line])
        }

        moves := Moves{From: make([]map[int]Ref, len(files)), To: make([]map[int]Ref, len(files))}
        for i := range files {
                moves.From[i], moves.To[i] = map[int]Ref{}, map[int]Ref{}
        }
        // longest returns the longest block starting at removed line l of file
        // i among the added lines cands, the first one on a tie, and its length.
        // With limit above 0 it tries only the first limit of them still free.
        longest := func(i, l int, cands []Ref, limit int) (best Ref, n int) {
                tried := 0
                for _, c := range cands {
                        if limit > 0 {
                                if added[c.File].used[c.Line] {
                                        continue
                                }
                                if tried++; tried > limit {
                                        break
                                }
                        }
                        k := 0
                        for matches(i, l+k, Ref{c.File, c.Line + k}) {
                                k++
                        }
                        if k > n {
                                best, n = c, k
                        }
                }
                return best, n
        }
        for i, f := range files {
                // The letters and digits of each line, counted once: a block is
                // counted again for each line it may start at.
                counted := make([]int, len(f.Old))
                for l := range counted {
                        counted[l] = -1
                }
                letters := func(from, to int) int {
                        n := 0
                        for l := from; l < to; l++ {
                                if counted[l] < 0 {
                                        counted[l] = alnum(f.Old[l : l+1])
                                }
                                n += counted[l]
                        }
                        return n
                }
                for l := 0; l < len(f.Old); l++ {
                        // Only a removed line not in a block yet starts one, see
                        // matches.
                        if _, ok := removed[i].row[l]; !ok || removed[i].used[l] {
                                continue
                        }
                        // Blocks of two lines or more start among the pairs, in the
                        // order of the candidates. A single line counts only with
                        // enough letters and digits of its own, rarely found in many
                        // places: only then are all candidates tried.
                        var best Ref
                        n := 0
                        if l+1 < len(f.Old) {
                                limit := 0
                                if letters(l, l+2) < minMovedAlnum {
                                        limit = maxCommonStarts
                                }
                                best, n = longest(i, l, pairs[pairKey(f.Old, l)], limit)
                        }
                        if n < 2 && letters(l, l+1) >= minMovedAlnum {
                                best, n = longest(i, l, candidates[strings.TrimSpace(f.Old[l])], 0)
                        }
                        if n == 0 || letters(l, l+n) < minMovedAlnum {
                                continue
                        }
                        for k := range n {
                                to := Ref{best.File, best.Line + k}
                                removed[i].used[l+k], added[to.File].used[to.Line] = true, true
                                moves.From[i][l+k] = to
                                moves.To[to.File][to.Line] = Ref{i, l + k}
                        }
                        l += n - 1
                }
        }
        return moves
}

// alnum counts the letters and digits in lines.
func alnum(lines []string) int {
        n := 0
        for _, l := range lines {
                for _, r := range l {
                        if unicode.IsLetter(r) || unicode.IsDigit(r) {
                                n++
                        }
                }
        }
        return n
}

// Package gitsrc reads commits and their changed files from git.
package gitsrc

import (
        "bufio"
        "bytes"
        "context"
        "errors"
        "fmt"
        "io"
        "io/fs"
        "os"
        "os/exec"
        "path"
        "path/filepath"
        "slices"
        "strconv"
        "strings"
        "sync"
        "time"

        "jdiff/internal/source"
)

// emptyTree is the id of git's empty tree, the base of root commits.
const emptyTree = "4b825dc642cb6eb9a060e54bf8d69288fbee4904"

// Commit is one commit of the log.
type Commit struct {
        Hash, Short    string
        Author, Date   string // Date: of the author, local, as 2006-01-02 15:04
        Subject        string
        Body           string // the message after the subject, trimmed
        Parent         string // first parent, empty for a root commit
        Added, Removed int    // line counts, which Log leaves 0: see Counts
}

// LineCounts are the lines a commit added and removed.
type LineCounts struct{ Added, Removed int }

// Repo runs git in a directory; the empty Dir means the current directory.
type Repo struct {
        Dir string
        ctx context.Context // ends the git commands, see WithContext
}

// WithContext returns r running its git commands with ctx: when it is done,
// they are killed and the methods return its error. Without, they run to
// their end.
func (r Repo) WithContext(ctx context.Context) Repo {
        r.ctx = ctx
        return r
}

// running counts the git commands that run, see Wait.
var running sync.WaitGroup

// Wait waits until the git commands that run have ended, at most d, and
// reports whether they have. Killing the commands of a cancelled context
// takes a moment, which a program that ends must wait for: else they run
// on without it.
func Wait(d time.Duration) bool {
        done := make(chan struct{})
        go func() {
                running.Wait()
                close(done)
        }()
        select {
        case <-done:
                return true
        case <-time.After(d):
                return false
        }
}

// command returns the command running git with args in r.Dir.
func (r Repo) command(args ...string) *exec.Cmd {
        ctx := r.ctx
        if ctx == nil {
                ctx = context.Background()
        }
        cmd := exec.CommandContext(ctx, "git", args...)
        cmd.Dir = r.Dir
        return cmd
}

func (r Repo) git(args ...string) ([]byte, error) { return r.run(nil, nil, args...) }

// run runs git with env added to its environment and stdin as its input.
func (r Repo) run(env []string, stdin []byte, args ...string) ([]byte, error) {
        cmd := r.command(args...)
        cmd.Env = append(cmd.Environ(), env...)
        if stdin != nil {
                cmd.Stdin = bytes.NewReader(stdin)
        }
        var stderr bytes.Buffer
        cmd.Stderr = &stderr
        running.Add(1)
        out, err := cmd.Output()
        running.Done()
        if err != nil {
                return nil, r.gitError(args[0], &stderr, err)
        }
        return out, nil
}

// gitError returns the error of git name that failed with err: the context's
// error when it was cancelled, as what git says then is of no interest, else
// what git wrote to stderr, else err.
func (r Repo) gitError(name string, stderr *bytes.Buffer, err error) error {
        if r.ctx != nil && r.ctx.Err() != nil {
                return r.ctx.Err()
        }
        if msg := strings.TrimSpace(stderr.String()); msg != "" {
                return fmt.Errorf("git %s: %s", name, msg)
        }
        return fmt.Errorf("git %s: %w", name, err)
}

// Reversed reports whether git log lists the commits of args oldest first:
// each --reverse before a -- flips the order.
func Reversed(args []string) bool {
        rev := false
        for _, a := range args {
                if a == "--" {
                        break
                }
                if a == "--reverse" {
                        rev = !rev
                }
        }
        return rev
}

// logFormatFlags and diffFormatFlags are the flags of git log and git diff
// that change the output jdiff reads, alone or with =value.
var (
        logFormatFlags  = []string{"--oneline", "--pretty", "--format", "--graph", "--show-signature"}
        diffFormatFlags = []string{"-p", "-u", "--patch", "-s", "--no-patch", "--stat", "--numstat", "--shortstat",
                "--dirstat", "--name-only", "--name-status", "--summary", "--compact-summary", "--check",
                "--patch-with-raw", "--patch-with-stat", "--output"}
)

// checkFormat returns an error naming the first argument before a -- that
// is one of flags: they change the output of git command, which jdiff reads.
func checkFormat(command string, args, flags []string) error {
        for _, a := range args {
                if a == "--" {
                        break
                }
                name, _, _ := strings.Cut(a, "=")
                if slices.Contains(flags, name) {
                        return fmt.Errorf("git %s: %s changes the output jdiff reads, leave it out", command, name)
                }
        }
        return nil
}

// Log returns the commits git log selects with args, in its order, without
// their line counts: counting diffs every commit, which takes seconds in a
// long history, so Counts does it for the commits shown. Records start with
// RS, fields are separated by US and the body, which may span lines, ends
// with GS. Signatures are not shown, even with log.showSignature set: their
// output would come between the records.
func (r Repo) Log(args []string) ([]Commit, error) {
        if err := checkFormat("log", args, logFormatFlags); err != nil {
                return nil, err
        }
        out, err := r.git(append([]string{"log", "--no-show-signature",
                "--format=%x1e%H%x1f%h%x1f%an%x1f%ad%x1f%P%x1f%s%x1f%b%x1d",
                "--date=format-local:%Y-%m-%d %H:%M"}, args...)...)
        if err != nil {
                return nil, err
        }
        var commits []Commit
        for rec := range strings.SplitSeq(string(out), "\x1e") {
                if rec == "" {
                        continue
                }
                header, _, _ := strings.Cut(rec, "\x1d")
                fields := strings.Split(header, "\x1f")
                if len(fields) != 7 {
                        return nil, fmt.Errorf("git log: unexpected record %q", header)
                }
                c := Commit{Hash: fields[0], Short: fields[1], Author: fields[2], Date: fields[3],
                        Subject: fields[5], Body: strings.TrimSpace(fields[6])}
                c.Parent, _, _ = strings.Cut(fields[4], " ")
                commits = append(commits, c)
        }
        return commits, nil
}

// Counts returns the lines the commits hashes added and removed, by hash.
// Merges are counted against their first parent, as Files compares them,
// root commits against the empty tree; binary files are not counted.
func (r Repo) Counts(hashes []string) (map[string]LineCounts, error) {
        counts := map[string]LineCounts{}
        if len(hashes) == 0 {
                return counts, nil
        }
        out, err := r.git(append([]string{"log", "--no-walk=unsorted", "--no-show-signature", "--format=%x1e%H", "--numstat", "--no-relative",
                "--diff-merges=first-parent", "--end-of-options"}, hashes...)...)
        if err != nil {
                return nil, err
        }
        for rec := range strings.SplitSeq(string(out), "\x1e") {
                hash, stat, _ := strings.Cut(rec, "\n")
                if hash == "" {
                        continue
                }
                var c LineCounts
                for line := range strings.SplitSeq(stat, "\n") {
                        // "added<TAB>removed<TAB>path"; binary files show "-".
                        parts := strings.SplitN(line, "\t", 3)
                        if len(parts) < 3 {
                                continue
                        }
                        added, err1 := strconv.Atoi(parts[0])
                        removed, err2 := strconv.Atoi(parts[1])
                        if err1 == nil && err2 == nil {
                                c.Added += added
                                c.Removed += removed
                        }
                }
                counts[hash] = c
        }
        return counts, nil
}

// TopLevel returns the top directory of the repository, or an error outside
// one. git writes it with slashes also on Windows; it is returned in the
// system's form, so that it compares with other paths.
func (r Repo) TopLevel() (string, error) {
        out, err := r.git("rev-parse", "--show-toplevel")
        if err != nil {
                return "", err
        }
        return filepath.Clean(strings.TrimSpace(string(out))), nil
}

// Branch returns the name of the checked out branch, or "detached HEAD".
func (r Repo) Branch() string {
        out, err := r.git("symbolic-ref", "--short", "-q", "HEAD")
        if err != nil {
                return "detached HEAD"
        }
        return strings.TrimSpace(string(out))
}

// WatchPaths returns the files that change when the repository does: HEAD, the
// index, the packed refs and the ref of the checked out branch. They are where
// git has them, also in a worktree.
func (r Repo) WatchPaths() ([]string, error) {
        names := []string{"HEAD", "index", "packed-refs"}
        if ref, err := r.git("symbolic-ref", "-q", "HEAD"); err == nil {
                names = append(names, strings.TrimSpace(string(ref)))
        }
        return r.gitPaths(names...)
}

// gitPaths returns where git keeps the files names, also in a worktree:
// absolute and cleaned, as git gives them relative to where it ran.
func (r Repo) gitPaths(names ...string) ([]string, error) {
        args := []string{"rev-parse"}
        for _, n := range names {
                args = append(args, "--git-path", n)
        }
        out, err := r.git(args...)
        if err != nil {
                return nil, err
        }
        paths := strings.Split(strings.TrimSpace(string(out)), "\n")
        for i, p := range paths {
                if !filepath.IsAbs(p) {
                        p = filepath.Join(r.Dir, p) // relative to where git ran
                }
                paths[i] = filepath.Clean(p) // git writes / also on Windows
        }
        return paths, nil
}

// vcsDirs are where version control systems keep their own data, which is
// not worked on: WorkDirs leaves them out, and what is below them. Fossil
// keeps its data in files.
var vcsDirs = []string{".git", ".svn", ".hg", ".bzr", "CVS", "_darcs", ".jj", ".pijul", ".sl"}

// WorkDirs returns the directories of the working tree that hold its files,
// tracked or untracked and not ignored, with the time each was modified, as
// when a file there is created, deleted or renamed, as editors save; the top
// level always. A directory takes one lstat, not each of its files, so that
// this stays cheap in large repositories. A submodule and a nested
// repository count as entries of the directory they are in, which is not
// entered. Left out are the directories of vcsDirs, and those for which
// ignore holds of their path from the top with / and a / at the end, with
// the directories below them.
func (r Repo) WorkDirs(ignore func(rel string) bool) (map[string]time.Time, error) {
        top, err := r.TopLevel()
        if err != nil {
                return nil, err
        }
        // All files, also from a subdirectory, by their paths from the top.
        out, err := r.git("ls-files", "-z", "--cached", "--others", "--exclude-standard", "--full-name", "--", ":/")
        if err != nil {
                return nil, err
        }
        dirs := map[string]time.Time{top: {}}
        left := map[string]bool{} // directories from the top, whether left out
        var leftOut func(rel string) bool
        leftOut = func(rel string) bool {
                if rel == "." {
                        return false
                }
                if l, ok := left[rel]; ok {
                        return l
                }
                l := slices.Contains(vcsDirs, path.Base(rel)) || ignore(rel+"/") || leftOut(path.Dir(rel))
                left[rel] = l
                return l
        }
        for _, rel := range strings.Split(string(out), "\x00") {
                // A nested repository comes as its directory with a /.
                rel = strings.TrimSuffix(rel, "/")
                if rel == "" || leftOut(path.Dir(rel)) {
                        continue
                }
                dirs[filepath.Dir(filepath.Join(top, filepath.FromSlash(rel)))] = time.Time{}
        }
        for dir := range dirs {
                if info, err := os.Lstat(dir); err == nil {
                        dirs[dir] = info.ModTime()
                }
        }
        return dirs, nil
}

// Status returns the state of the repository: the commit of HEAD, the branch,
// staged, changed and untracked files. It does not write the index.
func (r Repo) Status() (string, error) {
        out, err := r.run([]string{"GIT_OPTIONAL_LOCKS=0"}, nil, "status", "--porcelain=v2", "--branch", "--untracked-files=all")
        return string(out), err
}

// StatusPaths returns the paths of the files that the lines of status, from
// Status, name and those of old do not: changed, renamed (the new path),
// unmerged, untracked or ignored. They are relative to where git ran. Paths
// git quotes, with special characters, are left out.
func StatusPaths(status, old string) []string {
        had := map[string]bool{}
        for _, line := range strings.Split(old, "\n") {
                had[line] = true
        }
        var paths []string
        for _, line := range strings.Split(status, "\n") {
                if had[line] || len(line) < 2 {
                        continue
                }
                // The fields before the path, which may hold spaces: see git status
                // --porcelain=v2.
                var p string
                switch line[0] {
                case '1':
                        p = field(line, 8)
                case '2':
                        p, _, _ = strings.Cut(field(line, 9), "\t")
                case 'u':
                        p = field(line, 10)
                case '?', '!':
                        p = line[2:]
                }
                if p != "" && !strings.HasPrefix(p, `"`) {
                        paths = append(paths, filepath.FromSlash(p))
                }
        }
        return paths
}

// field returns the rest of line after n fields separated by single spaces,
// "" when it has fewer.
func field(line string, n int) string {
        parts := strings.SplitN(line, " ", n+1)
        if len(parts) <= n {
                return ""
        }
        return parts[n]
}

// Files returns the files c changed against its first parent, or against
// the empty tree for a root commit, with their contents: what Diff returns
// for the two commits, but without asking for the top of the working tree,
// as two commits have no side in it and a bare repository has none.
func (r Repo) Files(c Commit) ([]source.File, error) {
        base := c.Parent
        if base == "" {
                base = emptyTree
        }
        return r.diffFiles(nil, "", []string{base, c.Hash})
}

// rawEntry is a file in the output of git diff --raw -z, without its content,
// and the ids of its sides.
type rawEntry struct {
        file         source.File
        oldID, newID string
}

// parseRaw parses the output of git diff --raw -z. Unmerged files are left
// out.
func parseRaw(out []byte) ([]rawEntry, error) {
        var entries []rawEntry
        tokens := strings.Split(strings.TrimSuffix(string(out), "\x00"), "\x00")
        for i := 0; i+1 < len(tokens); {
                // ":oldmode newmode oldid newid status", then the path or paths.
                meta := strings.Fields(strings.TrimPrefix(tokens[i], ":"))
                if len(meta) != 5 {
                        return nil, fmt.Errorf("git diff: unexpected entry %q", tokens[i])
                }
                f := source.File{Path: tokens[i+1], InOld: true, InNew: true}
                i += 2
                switch meta[4][0] {
                case 'A':
                        f.InOld = false
                case 'D':
                        f.InNew = false
                case 'R', 'C':
                        if i >= len(tokens) {
                                return nil, fmt.Errorf("git diff: truncated rename of %s", f.Path)
                        }
                        f.OldPath, f.Path = f.Path, tokens[i]
                        i++
                case 'U':
                        continue
                }
                entries = append(entries, rawEntry{f, meta[2], meta[3]})
        }
        return entries, nil
}

// Diff returns the files git diff compares with args, the arguments of git
// diff: the working tree against the index without any, --cached for the
// index against HEAD, commits and paths. Sides in the working tree, which
// git names by the zero id, are read from disk (see readWorking) and, but
// symlinks, set OldFile or NewFile, so that they can be watched. Unmerged
// files are left out.
func (r Repo) Diff(args []string) ([]source.File, error) { return r.diff(nil, args) }

// DiffUntracked returns what Diff does, and the untracked files as added
// where git diff compares with the working tree. They are marked with git add
// -N in a copy of the index, which is gone on return: the real one stays as
// it is. Directories, as nested repositories, and files that cannot be read,
// as broken symlinks, are left out.
func (r Repo) DiffUntracked(args []string) ([]source.File, error) {
        top, err := r.TopLevel()
        if err != nil {
                return nil, err
        }
        paths, err := Repo{Dir: top, ctx: r.ctx}.untracked()
        if err != nil {
                return nil, err
        }
        if len(paths) == 0 {
                return r.Diff(args)
        }
        dir, err := os.MkdirTemp("", "jdiff-")
        if err != nil {
                return nil, err
        }
        defer os.RemoveAll(dir)
        index, err := r.copyIndex(dir)
        if err != nil {
                return nil, err
        }
        env := []string{"GIT_INDEX_FILE=" + index}
        // One file may be named like a pathspec, say with a * in it.
        list := []byte(strings.Join(paths, "\x00"))
        if _, err := (Repo{Dir: top, ctx: r.ctx}).run(append(env, "GIT_LITERAL_PATHSPECS=1"), list,
                "add", "-N", "--pathspec-from-file=-", "--pathspec-file-nul"); err != nil {
                return nil, err
        }
        return r.diff(env, args)
}

// untracked returns the paths of the untracked files that are not ignored,
// relative to r.Dir, which must be the top of the working tree.
func (r Repo) untracked() ([]string, error) {
        out, err := r.git("ls-files", "--others", "--exclude-standard", "-z")
        if err != nil {
                return nil, err
        }
        var paths []string
        for _, p := range strings.Split(strings.TrimSuffix(string(out), "\x00"), "\x00") {
                if p != "" && readableFile(filepath.Join(r.Dir, filepath.FromSlash(p))) {
                        paths = append(paths, p)
                }
        }
        return paths, nil
}

// readableFile reports whether path is, or links to, a regular file that can
// be opened.
func readableFile(path string) bool {
        info, err := os.Stat(path)
        if err != nil || !info.Mode().IsRegular() {
                return false
        }
        f, err := os.Open(path)
        if err != nil {
                return false
        }
        f.Close()
        return true
}

// copyIndex copies the index into dir and returns the path of the copy. A
// repository without an index yet leaves nothing to copy.
func (r Repo) copyIndex(dir string) (string, error) {
        paths, err := r.gitPaths("index")
        if err != nil {
                return "", err
        }
        from := paths[0]
        to := filepath.Join(dir, "index")
        data, err := os.ReadFile(from)
        if errors.Is(err, fs.ErrNotExist) {
                return to, nil
        }
        if err != nil {
                return "", err
        }
        return to, os.WriteFile(to, data, 0o600)
}

// diff is Diff with env added to the environment of git diff.
func (r Repo) diff(env, args []string) ([]source.File, error) {
        if err := checkFormat("diff", args, diffFormatFlags); err != nil {
                return nil, err
        }
        top, err := r.TopLevel()
        if err != nil {
                return nil, err
        }
        return r.diffFiles(env, top, args)
}

// diffFiles returns the files git diff compares with args, with env added to
// its environment. Sides git names by the zero id are read from the working
// tree at top; Files passes none, as two commits have no such sides. Blobs
// are requested by object id, not by "rev:path": git cat-file reads one
// request per line, and paths may hold newlines.
func (r Repo) diffFiles(env []string, top string, args []string) ([]source.File, error) {
        // Paths relative to the top, also with diff.relative set.
        out, err := r.run(env, nil, append([]string{"diff", "--no-ext-diff", "--no-relative", "--raw", "-z", "--no-abbrev", "-M"},
                args...)...)
        if err != nil {
                return nil, err
        }

        entries, err := parseRaw(out)
        if err != nil {
                return nil, err
        }

        // A side of a file, in the order of the files and old before new: a
        // blob, or the working copy for the zero id.
        type fileSide struct {
                blob int // index into specs, -1 for the working copy
                path string
        }
        var files []source.File
        var sides []fileSide
        var specs []string
        for _, e := range entries {
                f := e.file
                files = append(files, f)
                for _, want := range []struct {
                        in   bool
                        id   string
                        path string
                }{{f.InOld, e.oldID, f.PathInOld()}, {f.InNew, e.newID, f.Path}} {
                        if !want.in {
                                continue
                        }
                        s := fileSide{blob: -1, path: want.path}
                        if strings.Trim(want.id, "0") != "" {
                                s.blob = len(specs)
                                specs = append(specs, want.id)
                        }
                        sides = append(sides, s)
                }
        }

        blobs, err := r.catFiles(specs)
        if err != nil {
                return nil, err
        }
        n := 0
        for i := range files {
                f := &files[i]
                for _, old := range []bool{true, false} {
                        if (old && !f.InOld) || (!old && !f.InNew) {
                                continue
                        }
                        side := sides[n]
                        n++
                        var content []string
                        binary := false
                        if side.blob >= 0 {
                                content, binary = lines(blobs[side.blob])
                        } else {
                                path := filepath.Join(top, filepath.FromSlash(side.path))
                                w := readWorking(path)
                                content, binary = w.lines, w.binary
                                if w.watch {
                                        if old {
                                                f.OldFile, f.OldStamp = path, w.stamp
                                        } else {
                                                f.NewFile, f.NewStamp = path, w.stamp
                                        }
                                }
                        }
                        f.Binary = f.Binary || binary
                        if old {
                                f.Old = content
                        } else {
                                f.New = content
                        }
                }
        }
        return files, nil
}

// workingSide is a side of a file read from the working tree, see
// readWorking.
type workingSide struct {
        lines  []string
        stamp  source.Stamp
        binary bool
        watch  bool // reloaded when it changes on disk
}

// readWorking reads the side of a file in the working tree at path as git
// compares it, so that one file cannot fail the whole list: a symlink by
// the path it points to, as git stores it, also one to a directory, and not
// watched, since watching reads what it points to; a directory, as of a
// submodule, and a file that cannot be read as binary, not watched; binary
// content as binary, watched; a file gone since git listed it as empty,
// watched so that it shows when it is back.
func readWorking(path string) workingSide {
        info, err := os.Lstat(path)
        switch {
        case errors.Is(err, fs.ErrNotExist):
                return workingSide{watch: true}
        case err != nil:
                return workingSide{binary: true}
        case info.Mode()&fs.ModeSymlink != 0:
                target, err := os.Readlink(path)
                if err != nil {
                        return workingSide{binary: true}
                }
                return workingSide{lines: []string{target}}
        }
        l, stamp, err := source.Read(path)
        switch {
        case errors.Is(err, source.ErrBinary):
                return workingSide{stamp: stamp, binary: true, watch: true}
        case err != nil:
                return workingSide{binary: true}
        }
        return workingSide{lines: l, stamp: stamp, watch: true}
}

// lines splits a blob, reporting missing blobs (e.g. submodules) and
// binary content as binary.
func lines(b blob) ([]string, bool) {
        if b.missing {
                return nil, true
        }
        l, err := source.SplitLines(b.data)
        return l, errors.Is(err, source.ErrBinary)
}

type blob struct {
        data    []byte
        missing bool
}

// catFiles reads the objects named by specs, their ids, with a single git
// cat-file --batch process.
func (r Repo) catFiles(specs []string) ([]blob, error) {
        if len(specs) == 0 {
                return nil, nil
        }
        cmd := r.command("cat-file", "--batch")
        var stderr bytes.Buffer
        cmd.Stderr = &stderr
        stdin, err := cmd.StdinPipe()
        if err != nil {
                return nil, err
        }
        stdout, err := cmd.StdoutPipe()
        if err != nil {
                return nil, err
        }
        if err := cmd.Start(); err != nil {
                return nil, err
        }
        running.Add(1)
        defer running.Done()
        go func() {
                w := bufio.NewWriter(stdin)
                for _, s := range specs {
                        fmt.Fprintln(w, s)
                }
                w.Flush()
                stdin.Close()
        }()

        // fail stops git, which may be blocked writing output no longer read.
        // When the context was cancelled, git was killed and err is only the
        // end of its output: the context's error is returned, as by run.
        fail := func(err error) ([]blob, error) {
                cmd.Process.Kill()
                cmd.Wait()
                if r.ctx != nil && r.ctx.Err() != nil {
                        return nil, r.ctx.Err()
                }
                return nil, err
        }

        rd := bufio.NewReader(stdout)
        blobs := make([]blob, len(specs))
        for i := range specs {
                header, err := rd.ReadString('\n')
                if err != nil {
                        return fail(fmt.Errorf("git cat-file: %w", err))
                }
                // "<oid> <type> <size>" or "<object> missing"
                fields := strings.Fields(header)
                if len(fields) != 3 {
                        blobs[i].missing = true
                        continue
                }
                size, err := strconv.Atoi(fields[2])
                if err != nil {
                        return fail(fmt.Errorf("git cat-file: bad header %q", header))
                }
                data := make([]byte, size+1) // content and trailing newline
                if _, err := io.ReadFull(rd, data); err != nil {
                        return fail(fmt.Errorf("git cat-file: %w", err))
                }
                blobs[i] = blob{data: data[:size], missing: fields[1] != "blob"}
        }
        if err := cmd.Wait(); err != nil {
                return nil, r.gitError("cat-file", &stderr, err)
        }
        return blobs, nil
}

// Package keys holds the keymap of jdiff: keybindings.toml, which binds the
// keys of all pages to actions, gives their words and decides the order of
// the settings, with the keys of the user's keybindings.toml over it. A Map
// is built once, with the configuration, and not changed after.
package keys

import (
        _ "embed"
        "errors"
        "io/fs"
        "os"
        "path/filepath"
        "regexp"
        "slices"
        "strconv"
        "strings"

        "github.com/BurntSushi/toml"
)

// BuiltinText is the built-in keybindings.toml.
//
//go:embed keybindings.toml
var BuiltinText string

// Row is a row of keybindings.toml.
type Row struct {
        Action  string
        Keys    []string
        Label   []string
        Help    string
        Setting string
}

// HelpRow is a line of the help popup in keybindings.toml.
type HelpRow struct{ Heading, Note, Action string }

// Map holds keybindings.toml: the rows of each page and the help's lines.
type Map struct {
        CommitList []Row     `toml:"commit_list"`
        FileList   []Row     `toml:"file_list"`
        DiffView   []Row     `toml:"diff_view"`
        FlowView   []Row     `toml:"flow_view"`
        Everywhere []Row     `toml:"everywhere"`
        DocView    []Row     `toml:"doc_view"`
        Help       []HelpRow `toml:"help"`
}

// page is a page of keybindings.toml: its name and its rows.
type page struct {
        name string
        rows *[]Row
}

// pages returns the pages of km in the order of keybindings.toml, which
// decides which page's row Row and RowsOf return.
func (km *Map) pages() []page {
        return []page{
                {"everywhere", &km.Everywhere}, {"diff_view", &km.DiffView}, {"flow_view", &km.FlowView},
                {"commit_list", &km.CommitList}, {"file_list", &km.FileList}, {"doc_view", &km.DocView},
        }
}

// Parse returns the Map of text, a keybindings.toml.
func Parse(text string) (Map, error) {
        var p Map
        _, err := toml.Decode(text, &p)
        return p, err
}

// builtin is BuiltinText, read. It is part of the program, so that failing to
// read it is a bug, which TestKeybindingsFile finds.
var builtin = func() Map {
        p, err := Parse(BuiltinText)
        if err != nil {
                panic(err)
        }
        return p
}()

// Builtin returns the built-in keys. They are shared: do not change them.
func Builtin() *Map { return &builtin }

// Row returns the row that gives the keys and words of action; see
// keybindings.toml for the order. Without one, it has no keys.
func (km Map) Row(action string) Row {
        if rows := km.RowsOf(action); len(rows) > 0 {
                return rows[0]
        }
        return Row{}
}

// RowsOf returns the rows of action in the first page that has it, in the
// order of Row.
func (km Map) RowsOf(action string) []Row {
        for _, pg := range km.pages() {
                var of []Row
                for _, r := range *pg.rows {
                        if r.Action == action {
                                of = append(of, r)
                        }
                }
                if len(of) > 0 {
                        return of
                }
        }
        return nil
}

// placeholder matches {action} and {action:n} in the words of keybindings.toml.
var placeholder = regexp.MustCompile(`\{([a-z_]+)(?::([0-9]+))?\}`)

// Text returns s with each placeholder replaced by the keys of its row, ""
// for a row that does not exist.
func (km Map) Text(s string) string {
        return placeholder.ReplaceAllStringFunc(s, func(m string) string {
                sub := placeholder.FindStringSubmatch(m)
                n, _ := strconv.Atoi(sub[2])
                if rows := km.RowsOf(sub[1]); n < len(rows) {
                        return strings.Join(rows[n].Keys, " ")
                }
                return ""
        })
}

// twoWay lists the actions whose rows take two keys, one for each way.
var twoWay = []string{"move", "page", "half_page", "ends", "scroll", "change", "next_file", "history", "match", "link", "line_link", "next_page"}

// userRow is a row of the user's keybindings.toml, which changes only keys.
type userRow struct {
        Action string
        Keys   []string
}

// ApplyUserKeys returns km with the keys of text, the user's
// keybindings.toml, applied over them, and what is wrong with it. The rows of
// an action on a page replace all its rows there, keeping their words; a file
// with a problem changes nothing, so that a mistake cannot take away the keys
// to quit or get help.
func (km Map) ApplyUserKeys(text string) (Map, []string) {
        var u struct {
                CommitList []userRow `toml:"commit_list"`
                FileList   []userRow `toml:"file_list"`
                DiffView   []userRow `toml:"diff_view"`
                FlowView   []userRow `toml:"flow_view"`
                Everywhere []userRow `toml:"everywhere"`
                DocView    []userRow `toml:"doc_view"`
        }
        md, err := toml.Decode(text, &u)
        if err != nil {
                return km, []string{err.Error()}
        }
        var problems []string
        for _, k := range md.Undecoded() {
                problems = append(problems, "unknown "+k.String())
        }
        merged := km
        fromFile := map[string][]userRow{
                "everywhere": u.Everywhere, "diff_view": u.DiffView, "flow_view": u.FlowView,
