        add := func(k flowKind, part, file, line int) {
                f.rows = append(f.rows, flowRow{k, part, file, line, 0})
        }
        for p, part := range f.parts {
                if part.diff == nil && part.commit != nil {
                        part.diff = part.commit.diff // loaded for the list meanwhile
                }
                for i := range f.commitLines(part) {
                        add(flowCommit, p, -1, i)
                }
                if part.diff == nil || len(part.diff.files) == 0 {
                        add(flowNote, p, -1, 0) // loading, why it cannot, or no files
                }
                if part.diff == nil {
                        continue
                }
                for i, e := range part.diff.files {
                        add(flowFile, p, i, 0)
                        if e.Binary {
                                add(flowNote, p, i, 0)
                                continue
                        }
                        screen, _ := part.diff.diffOf(e).screenLines(f.built)
                        for _, s := range screen {
                                f.rows = append(f.rows, flowRow{flowDiff, p, i, s.v, s.sub})
                        }
                }
        }
        f.rowAt, f.stale = make(map[flowRow]int, len(f.rows)), false
        for i, r := range slices.Backward(f.rows) {
                f.rowAt[r] = i // the first of equal rows, as a search finds
        }
        f.cursor = max(min(f.cursor, len(f.rows)-1), 0)
}

// refresh runs reload, which may reload files, calling its argument after
// each reload of a diff, and rebuilds the rows. The cursor stays on its
// screen row and, in a reloaded file, on its line.
func (f *Flow) refresh(reload func(after func(*fileAlign))) {
        var cur flowRow
        var a *fileAlign
        var row diff.Row
        reloads, lost := 0, false
        if f.cursor < len(f.rows) && f.rows[f.cursor].kind == flowDiff {
                cur = f.rows[f.cursor]
                a = f.parts[cur.part].diff.files[cur.file].align
                row, reloads = a.rows[a.view[cur.line].row], a.reloads
        }
        f.keepScreenRow(func() {
                // The cursor's row is followed through each reload: a diff may
                // reload both sides, and each reload maps only its own side.
                reload(func(rd *fileAlign) {
                        if rd != a || lost {
                                return
                        }
                        if i := a.rowAfterReload(row); i >= 0 {
                                row = a.rows[i]
                        } else {
                                lost = true
                        }
                })
                if a != nil {
                        // The files may have been listed again, in another order.
                        cur.file = slices.IndexFunc(f.parts[cur.part].diff.files, func(e *fileEntry) bool { return e.align == a })
                }
                f.relocate()
                if a == nil || cur.file < 0 || a.reloads == reloads || lost {
                        return
                }
                if v := a.follow(row, false); v >= 0 {
                        if i := f.findDiff(cur.part, cur.file, v, cur.sub); i >= 0 {
                                f.cursor = i
                        }
                }
        })
}

// followFiles keeps the cursor on its file of part when the files were
// listed again, from before to after: on the file's row, or for a file that is
// gone on the bar of the next file left, else of the last. The rows are
// rebuilt next.
func (f *Flow) followFiles(part int, before, after []*fileEntry) {
        if f.cursor >= len(f.rows) {
                return
        }
        r := &f.rows[f.cursor]
        if r.part != part || r.file < 0 {
                return
        }
        f.stale = true
        for i := r.file; i < len(before); i++ {
                if j := slices.Index(after, before[i]); j >= 0 {
                        if i == r.file {
                                r.file = j
                        } else {
                                *r = flowRow{flowFile, part, j, 0, 0}
                        }
                        return
                }
        }
        *r = flowRow{flowFile, part, len(after) - 1, 0, 0}
}

// followParts keeps the cursor on its commit when the commits were listed
// again, from the parts old to f.parts, which still hold it. The rows are
// rebuilt next.
func (f *Flow) followParts(old []*flowPart) {
        if f.cursor < len(f.rows) {
                r := &f.rows[f.cursor]
                r.part = slices.Index(f.parts, old[r.part])
                f.stale = true
        }
}

// relocate rebuilds the rows, keeping the cursor on its row: with wrapped
// lines on the same piece of the same line, else on the first line of its
// diff row when the layout changed.
func (f *Flow) relocate() {
        var cur flowRow
        ok := f.cursor < len(f.rows)
        if ok {
                cur = f.rows[f.cursor]
        }
        from := f.built
        f.build()
        i := f.find(cur)
        if i < 0 && cur.kind == flowCommit {
                // A message line no longer shown: on to the subject.
                i = f.find(flowRow{flowCommit, cur.part, -1, 2, 0})
        }
        if cur.kind == flowDiff {
                sub := cur.sub
                if a := f.parts[cur.part].diff.files[cur.file].align; cur.line < len(a.view) {
                        sub = a.convertSub(cur.line, cur.sub, from, f.built, f.active)
                }
                i = f.findDiff(cur.part, cur.file, cur.line, sub)
        }
        if ok && i >= 0 {
                f.cursor = i
        }
}

// findDiff returns the index of line sub of diff row line, or of its first
// line when it has no line sub, or -1.
func (f *Flow) findDiff(part, file, line, sub int) int {
        if i := f.find(flowRow{flowDiff, part, file, line, sub}); i >= 0 {
                return i
        }
        return f.find(flowRow{flowDiff, part, file, line, 0})
}

// find returns the index of row r, or -1. The rows are indexed by build: a
// long log has many, and each move looks for rows. After a row changed in
// place, as followFiles and followParts do before the rows are rebuilt, they
// are searched.
func (f *Flow) find(r flowRow) int {
        if !f.stale {
                if i, ok := f.rowAt[r]; ok {
                        return i
                }
                return -1
        }
        for i, x := range f.rows {
                if x == r {
                        return i
                }
        }
        return -1
}

// inLog reports whether f shows the commits of the log, rather than the
// files outside it.
func (f *Flow) inLog() bool { return f.load != nil }

// unloaded reports whether the files of part are not loaded yet, neither
// for the flow nor for the list.
func (f *Flow) unloaded(part int) bool {
        p := f.parts[part]
        return p.diff == nil && p.err == "" && (p.commit == nil || p.commit.diff == nil)
}

// nextLoad returns the command loading the next commit, nil while one loads
// or none is to: the one a search waits for, else the first on the screen
// not loaded yet, else the first within a screen from the cursor on the way
// of the last move, so that it is there when the cursor comes.
func (f *Flow) nextLoad() tea.Cmd {
        if !f.inLog() || f.loading != nil {
                return nil
        }
        p := -1
        if w := f.waiting; w != nil {
                p = slices.Index(f.parts, w.part)
        }
        h := f.bodyHeight()
        first := func(from, to int) {
                for i := max(from, 0); p < 0 && i < min(to, len(f.rows)); i++ {
                        if q := f.rows[i].part; f.unloaded(q) {
                                p = q
                        }
                }
        }
        first(f.top, f.top+h)
        switch {
        case f.ahead > 0:
                first(f.cursor, f.cursor+h+1)
        case f.ahead < 0:
                first(f.cursor-h, f.cursor+1)
        }
        if p < 0 || !f.unloaded(p) {
                return nil
        }
        f.loading = f.parts[p]
        return f.load(p)
}

// partLoaded takes the files of the commit of part, which the log loaded,
// or why they could not be. The cursor stays on its row and screen row, or
// after end on the last row; a search waiting for the commit goes on. It
// returns the command loading the next commit.
func (f *Flow) partLoaded(part *flowPart, err error) tea.Cmd {
        if f.loading == part {
                f.loading = nil
        }
        if err != nil {
                part.err = firstLine(err)
        }
        f.keepScreenRow(f.relocate) // build takes the files from the commit
        if f.atEnd {
                _, f.cursor = f.bounds()
                f.scrollToCursor()
        }
        if w := f.waiting; w != nil && !f.unloaded(slices.Index(f.parts, w.part)) {
                f.waiting = nil
                w.redo()
        }
        return f.nextLoad()
}

// await makes the search redo wait for commit p to load, saying so.
func (f *Flow) await(p int, redo func()) {
        f.waiting = &flowWait{f.parts[p], redo}
        f.msg = "loading " + f.parts[p].commit.Short + "…"
}

// moveCursor moves the cursor by delta rows, over the rows there are: the
// commits on the way load as they come on the screen. For far, end, the
// cursor stays on the last row as they load, until another key.
func (f *Flow) moveCursor(delta int) {
        f.cursor = f.confine(max(min(f.cursor+delta, len(f.rows)-1), 0))
        f.atEnd, f.ahead = delta >= far, direction(delta > 0)
        f.scrollToCursor()
}

// bounds returns the first and last row the cursor may go to: those of its
// commit while stay_in_commit is set, else all.
func (f *Flow) bounds() (lo, hi int) {
        lo, hi = 0, len(f.rows)-1
        if !f.inLog() || !f.cfg.Settings.StayInCommit || len(f.rows) == 0 {
                return lo, hi
        }
        p := f.rows[f.cursor].part
        for lo = f.cursor; lo > 0 && f.rows[lo-1].part == p; lo-- {
        }
        for hi = f.cursor; hi < len(f.rows)-1 && f.rows[hi+1].part == p; hi++ {
        }
        return lo, hi
}

// confine returns row i, or the nearest row within the bounds with a
// warning that the cursor stays in its commit.
func (f *Flow) confine(i int) int {
        lo, hi := f.bounds()
        if i >= lo && i <= hi {
                return i
        }
        f.msg = "stays in commit " + f.parts[f.rows[f.cursor].part].commit.Short + " (C)"
        return max(min(i, hi), lo)
}

// scroll moves the window by delta rows, as far as it can, and the cursor
// along, so that the cursor keeps its screen row.
func (f *Flow) scroll(delta int) {
        f.ahead = direction(delta > 0)
        top := f.top
        f.top = max(min(f.top+delta, len(f.rows)-f.bodyHeight()), 0)
        f.cursor = f.confine(max(min(f.cursor+f.top-top, len(f.rows)-1), 0))
        f.scrollToCursor()
}

// searchResult is how a search ended.
type searchResult int

const (
        notFound searchResult = iota
        found
        unloadedOnTheWay // a commit on the way is not loaded yet, see search
)

// search moves the cursor to the next row in direction dir for which ok
// holds. With stay set it does not leave the bounds; reaching them sets a
// warning. With wait, a commit on the way that is not loaded yet ends it,
// at the first of its rows that are there that ok does not take: its part
// is returned, for the caller to wait for it (see await), else -1.
func (f *Flow) search(dir int, ok func(int) bool, stay, wait bool) (searchResult, int) {
        for i := f.cursor + dir; i >= 0 && i < len(f.rows); i += dir {
                if lo, hi := f.bounds(); stay && (i < lo || i > hi) {
                        f.confine(i)
                        return notFound, -1
                }
                if ok(i) {
                        f.cursor = i
                        f.scrollToCursor()
                        return found, -1
                }
                if p := f.rows[i].part; wait && f.inLog() && f.unloaded(p) {
                        return unloadedOnTheWay, p
                }
        }
        return notFound, -1
}

// nextChange moves the cursor to the start of the next (dir > 0) or previous
// block of changed rows, waiting for the commits on the way to load.
func (f *Flow) nextChange(dir int) {
        switch r, p := f.search(dir, f.changeStart, true, true); {
        case r == unloadedOnTheWay:
                f.await(p, func() { f.nextChange(dir) })
        case r == notFound && f.msg == "":
                f.msg = "no more changes"
        }
}

// nextFile moves the cursor to the bar of the next (dir > 0) or previous
// file, at the top of the screen, waiting for the commits on the way to
// load.
func (f *Flow) nextFile(dir int) {
        switch r, p := f.search(dir, f.isKind(flowFile, 0), true, true); {
        case r == found:
                f.barToTop()
        case r == unloadedOnTheWay:
                f.await(p, func() { f.nextFile(dir) })
        case f.msg == "":
                f.msg = ends(dir > 0, "last file", "first file")
        }
}

// barToTop scrolls the bar under the cursor to the top of the screen, so
// that what it starts is visible.
func (f *Flow) barToTop() {
        f.top = f.cursor
        f.scrollToCursor()
}

func (f *Flow) isKind(k flowKind, line int) func(int) bool {
        return func(i int) bool { return f.rows[i].kind == k && f.rows[i].line == line }
}

// changeStart reports whether row i starts a block of changed rows.
func (f *Flow) changeStart(i int) bool {
        changed := func(i int) bool {
                r := f.rows[i]
                return r.kind == flowDiff && f.parts[r.part].diff.files[r.file].align.changed(r.line)
        }
        return changed(i) && (i == 0 || !changed(i-1))
}

// show puts the cursor on line sub of diff row line of file in part, on
// the file bar for line -1 and on the commit bar for file -1, at screen row
// screenRow.
func (f *Flow) show(part, file, line, sub, screenRow int) {
        f.ahead, f.atEnd, f.waiting = 0, false, nil
        // While hidden, diff views may have changed the diffs or the layout.
        f.build()
        f.adopt(part, file)
        i := f.find(flowRow{flowCommit, part, -1, 0, 0})
        switch {
        case f.parts[part].diff == nil:
        case file >= 0 && line >= 0:
                i = f.findDiff(part, file, line, sub)
        case file >= 0:
                i = f.find(flowRow{flowFile, part, file, 0, 0})
        }
        f.cursor = max(i, 0)
        f.top = f.cursor - screenRow
        f.scrollToCursor()
}

// showFrom shows file of part where its diff view from leaves it: on the
// cursor's line at its screen row, in its pane. For from nil it shows the
// file's bar, or the commit's bar for file -1. FileDiff.takeOver is the way
// back.
func (f *Flow) showFrom(part, file int, from *FileDiff) {
        if from == nil {
                f.show(part, file, -1, 0, 0)
                return
        }
        from.syncLayout()
        f.show(part, file, from.cursor, from.sub, from.pos()-from.top)
        f.active = from.active
}

// adopt takes over the pending mark and replace prompt that diff views of
// the files of part left, so that esc and enter treat them as the flow's
// own. Of several pending marks the one of file is kept, else the first;
// the others are dropped, as the flow keeps one.
func (f *Flow) adopt(part, file int) {
        f.marked, f.replacing = nil, nil
        d := f.parts[part].diff
        if d == nil {
                return
        }
        var marked []*fileAlign
        for i, e := range d.files {
                a := e.align
                if a == nil {
                        continue
                }
                if a.pending != nil {
                        if i == file {
                                marked = append([]*fileAlign{a}, marked...)
                        } else {
                                marked = append(marked, a)
                        }
                }
                if a.replace != nil {
                        f.replacing = a
                }
        }
        if len(marked) == 0 {
                return
        }
        for _, a := range marked[1:] {
                f.marked = a
                f.dropMark()
        }
        f.marked = marked[0]
        if len(marked) > 1 {
                f.build() // the dropped marks may have folded rows
        }
}

// position returns the part of the cursor, its file (-1 on rows of the
// commit) and the line in the file's diff view (-1 on other rows).
func (f *Flow) position() (part, file, line int) {
        if len(f.rows) == 0 {
                return 0, -1, -1
        }
        r := f.rows[f.cursor]
        line = -1
        if r.kind == flowDiff {
                line = r.line
        }
        return r.part, r.file, line
}

// headerLines is the number of lines above the rows: the commit in the log,
// and the file names.
func (f *Flow) headerLines() int {
        if f.inLog() {
                return 2
        }
        return 1
}

// cursorSub returns the line of the cursor within its diff row.
func (f *Flow) cursorSub() int {
        if len(f.rows) == 0 {
                return 0
        }
        return f.rows[f.cursor].sub
}

func (f *Flow) bodyHeight() int { return max(f.height-1-f.headerLines(), 1) }

func (f *Flow) keepScreenRow(fn func()) {
        offset := f.cursor - f.top
        fn()
        f.top = f.cursor - offset
        f.scrollToCursor()
}

func (f *Flow) scrollToCursor() {
        f.top = scrollWindow(f.cursor, f.top, f.bodyHeight(), len(f.rows))
}

// help returns the keys of the status bar.
func (f *Flow) help() string {
        return statusKeys(f.cfg, barKeys{enter: "anchor", back: "to_files", commits: f.inLog(), flow: true})
}

func (f *Flow) View() tea.View { return tea.NewView(f.frame()) }

func (f *Flow) frame() string {
        if f.width == 0 {
                return ""
        }
        var b strings.Builder
        b.WriteString(f.header())
        for i := f.top; i < f.top+f.bodyHeight(); i++ {
                if i < len(f.rows) {
                        b.WriteString(f.row(i))
                }
                b.WriteString("\n")
        }
        b.WriteString(f.status())
        return b.String()
}

// header renders the lines above the rows: in the log the commit of the
// cursor, then the names of its file as in the single file view.
func (f *Flow) header() string {
        t := f.cfg.Theme
        p, file, _ := f.position()
        var b strings.Builder
        if f.inLog() && p < len(f.parts) {
                c := f.parts[p].commit
                line := " " + t.CommitHash.Render(c.Short) + " " + t.Title.Render(c.Subject)
                b.WriteString(ansi.Truncate(line, f.width, "…") + "\n")
        }
        if file >= 0 {
                b.WriteString(paneTitles(t, f.parts[p].diff.files[file].paneNames(), f.active, f.cfg.Settings.SideBySide, f.width))
        }
        return b.String() + "\n"
}

func (f *Flow) status() string {
        p, file, _ := f.position()
        var where []string
        if f.inLog() {
                c := fmt.Sprintf("commit %d/%d", p+1, len(f.parts))
                if f.cfg.Settings.StayInCommit {
                        c += " (C)"
                }
                where = append(where, c)
        }
        if file >= 0 {
                files := f.parts[p].diff.files
                where = append(where, fmt.Sprintf("file %d/%d", file+1, len(files)))
                if a := files[file].align; a != nil {
                        where = append(where, fmt.Sprintf("anchors %d", len(a.anchors)))
                }
        }
        msg := f.msg
        if msg == "" && f.marked != nil {
                msg = f.marked.markPrompt()
        }
        left := pageStatus(f.cfg, msg, f.help())
        return statusLine(f.cfg.Theme, f.width, left, " "+strings.Join(where, " · "))
}

// row renders row i. Diff rows are drawn by the file's diff with the flow's
// pane, scroll offset and width; other rows show the cursor in the first
// column and in their text, in the cursor style.
func (f *Flow) row(i int) string {
        t := f.cfg.Theme
        r := f.rows[i]
        part := f.parts[r.part]
        cursor := i == f.cursor
        mark := " "
        if cursor {
                mark = t.Cursor.Render(" ")
        }
        w := max(f.width-1, 0)
        // bar renders s across the row in st.
        bar := func(s string, st lipgloss.Style) string {
                s = ansi.Truncate(s, w, "…")
                if cursor {
                        st = t.Cursor.Inherit(st)
                }
                return st.Render(s + strings.Repeat(" ", max(w-ansi.StringWidth(s), 0)))
        }
        // text renders s in st, on the cursor row past its indentation.
        text := func(s string, st lipgloss.Style) string {
                s = ansi.Truncate(s, w, "…")
                if !cursor {
                        return st.Render(s)
                }
                body := strings.TrimLeft(s, " ")
                return st.Render(s[:len(s)-len(body)]) + t.Cursor.Inherit(st).Render(body)
        }

        switch r.kind {
        case flowCommit:
                line := f.commitLines(part)[r.line]
                switch r.line {
                case 0:
                        return mark + bar(" "+line, t.CommitBar)
                case 2:
                        return mark + text(line, t.Heading.Inherit(t.Zero))
                }
                return mark + text(line, t.Zero)
        case flowFile:
                e := part.diff.files[r.file]
                line := " "
                if lbl := e.label(t); lbl != "" {
                        line += lbl + " "
                }
                line += e.name()
                if e.Binary {
                        line += "   binary"
                } else if e.added > 0 || e.removed > 0 {
                        line += fmt.Sprintf("   +%d -%d", e.added, e.removed)
                }
                return mark + bar(line, t.FileBar)
        case flowNote:
                note := "no changed files"
                switch {
                case r.file >= 0:
                        note = "binary file"
                case part.err != "":
                        note = printable(part.err)
                case part.diff == nil:
                        note = "loading…"
                }
                return mark + text("  "+note, t.Hint)
        }
        a := part.diff.files[r.file].align
        return a.render(sline{r.line, r.sub}, i == f.cursor, layout{f.width, f.xoff, f.active, f.built})
}

package ui

import (
        "fmt"
        "strings"

        tea "charm.land/bubbletea/v2"
        "charm.land/lipgloss/v2"
        "github.com/charmbracelet/x/ansi"

        "jdiff/internal/config"
        "jdiff/internal/keys"
)

// binding is a key and what it does; a binding without key is a heading.
type binding struct{ key, what string }

// helpBindings returns the lines of the help popup of km, in the order of its
// help; helpLines puts the settings in their order.
func helpBindings(km *keys.Map) []binding {
        var bs []binding
        for _, h := range km.Help {
                switch {
                case h.Heading != "":
                        bs = append(bs, binding{"", h.Heading})
                case h.Note != "":
                        bs = append(bs, binding{"", "  " + km.Text(h.Note)})
                default:
                        r := km.Row(h.Action)
                        bs = append(bs, binding{strings.Join(r.Keys, " "), km.Text(r.Help)})
                }
        }
        return bs
}

// Help wraps the top-level model and shows the key bindings of all pages in
// a popup over it on ?.
type Help struct {
        tea.Model
        cfg           *config.Config
        lines         []binding // of cfg.Keys, which do not change
        rows          []string  // the lines drawn, wrapped to the width while open
        boxWidth      int
        open          bool
        top           int // first shown row of the popup
        width, height int
}

// WithHelp returns m with the help popup.
func WithHelp(cfg *config.Config, m tea.Model) *Help {
        return &Help{Model: m, cfg: cfg, lines: helpLines(cfg.Keys)}
}

func (h *Help) Update(msg tea.Msg) (tea.Model, tea.Cmd) {
        switch msg := msg.(type) {
        case tea.WindowSizeMsg:
                h.width, h.height = msg.Width, msg.Height
                if h.open { // else wrapped when opened: resizing stays cheap
                        h.wrap()
                        h.scroll(0)
                }
        case tea.KeyPressMsg:
                if cmd, ok := popupKeys(h.cfg.Keys, msg, h.open, "help", h.key); ok {
                        return h, cmd
                }
        }
        _, cmd := h.Model.Update(msg)
        return h, cmd
}

// popupKeys passes the key press msg to key, when the popup is open or msg
// opens it, being a key of action opener, and reports whether it did; else
// msg is for the page below.
func popupKeys(km *keys.Map, msg tea.KeyPressMsg, open bool, opener string, key func(string) tea.Cmd) (tea.Cmd, bool) {
        k := msg.String()
        if !open && !isAction(km, k, opener) {
                return nil, false
        }
        return key(k), true
}

// key handles k in the popup, or opens it on ?.
func (h *Help) key(k string) tea.Cmd {
        if !h.open {
                h.open, h.top = true, 0
                h.wrap()
                return nil
        }
        cmd, _ := popupNav(h.cfg.Keys, k, "help", &h.open, h.scroll)
        return cmd
}

// popupNav handles the keys that all popups share, so that they close and
// move alike: a key of opener, esc and q close the popup, ctrl+c quits, down
// and j move by one, up and k back. It reports whether k was one of them;
// the popup handles the others.
func popupNav(km *keys.Map, k, opener string, open *bool, move func(delta int)) (tea.Cmd, bool) {
        if isAction(km, k, opener) {
                *open = false
                return nil, true
        }
        switch k {
        case "esc", "q":
                *open = false
        case "ctrl+c":
                return tea.Quit, true
        case "down", "j":
                move(1)
        case "up", "k":
                move(-1)
        default:
                return nil, false
        }
        return nil, true
}

// boxHeight is the height of the popup.
func (h *Help) boxHeight() int { return popupHeight(len(h.rows), h.height) }

// scroll moves the shown lines by delta; from the top, it moves on by two
// at once, as the window never starts at the second line.
func (h *Help) scroll(delta int) {
        top := h.top + delta
        if top == 1 && delta > 0 {
                top = 2
        }
        h.top = windowAt(top, h.boxHeight()-2, len(h.rows)).first
}

// wrap draws the lines into rows as wide as the screen allows: what does not
// fit goes on in the rows below, under its first word, instead of being cut.
// The popup is as wide as its widest row.
func (h *Help) wrap() {
        t := h.cfg.Theme
        keyW := 0
        for _, b := range h.lines {
                keyW = max(keyW, ansi.StringWidth(b.key))
        }
        inner := h.width - 4 // border and padding
        h.rows = h.rows[:0]
        for _, b := range h.lines {
                switch {
                case b.key == "" && !strings.HasPrefix(b.what, " "):
                        h.rows = append(h.rows, wrapRows("", 0, b.what, inner, t.Branch)...)
                case b.key == "":
                        h.rows = append(h.rows, wrapRows(strings.Repeat(" ", keyW+2), keyW+2, strings.TrimSpace(b.what), inner, t.Hint)...)
                default:
                        key := t.Key.Render(fmt.Sprintf("  %-*s", keyW, b.key)) + "  "
                        h.rows = append(h.rows, wrapRows(key, keyW+4, b.what, inner, lipgloss.NewStyle())...)
                }
        }
        rowW := 0
        for _, r := range h.rows {
                rowW = max(rowW, ansi.StringWidth(r))
        }
        h.boxWidth = min(rowW+4, h.width)
}

// wrapRows returns the rows of text in style after prefix, which is indent
// columns wide, wrapped to width; the rows after the first are indented as
// far. Without room after the indent, text stays in one row.
func wrapRows(prefix string, indent int, text string, width int, style lipgloss.Style) []string {
        if width-indent < 1 {
                return []string{prefix + style.Render(text)}
        }
        parts := strings.Split(ansi.Wrap(text, width-indent, ""), "\n")
        rows := make([]string, len(parts))
        for i, p := range parts {
                if i > 0 {
                        prefix = strings.Repeat(" ", indent)
                }
                rows[i] = prefix + style.Render(strings.TrimRight(p, " "))
        }
        return rows
}

func (h *Help) View() tea.View { return tea.NewView(h.frame()) }

func (h *Help) frame() string {
        base := h.Model.View().Content
        if !h.open || h.width == 0 {
                return base
        }
        t := h.cfg.Theme
        lines := windowAt(h.top, h.boxHeight()-2, len(h.rows)).rows(t, len(h.rows), func(i int) string { return h.rows[i] })
        title := t.CommitHash.Render(" keys ")
        if h.boxHeight()-2 < len(h.rows) {
                title += t.Hint.Render(fmt.Sprintf(" %d/%d ", h.top+1, len(h.rows)))
        }
        return overlay(base, box(t, h.boxWidth, h.boxHeight(), title, lines), h.boxWidth, h.width)
}

// overlay draws the popup pop, w columns wide, centered over base, which
// is width columns wide.
func overlay(base, pop string, w, width int) string {
        y := max((strings.Count(base, "\n")+1-strings.Count(strings.TrimSuffix(pop, "\n"), "\n")-1)/2, 0)
        return overlayAt(base, pop, max((width-w)/2, 0), y, w, width)
}

// overlayAt draws the popup pop, w columns wide, over base, which is width
// columns wide, from column x of row y on.
func overlayAt(base, pop string, x, y, w, width int) string {
        rows := strings.Split(base, "\n")
        popRows := strings.Split(strings.TrimSuffix(pop, "\n"), "\n")
        for i, p := range popRows {
                if y+i >= len(rows) {
                        break
                }
                row := rows[y+i]
                left := ansi.Truncate(row, x, "")
                left += strings.Repeat(" ", x-ansi.StringWidth(left))
                rows[y+i] = left + p + ansi.Cut(row, x+w, width)
        }
        return strings.Join(rows, "\n")
}

package ui

import (
        "context"
        "fmt"
        "slices"
        "strings"

        tea "charm.land/bubbletea/v2"
        "charm.land/lipgloss/v2"
        "github.com/charmbracelet/x/ansi"

        "jdiff/internal/config"
        "jdiff/internal/gitsrc"
        "jdiff/internal/keys"
        "jdiff/internal/source"
)

// LoadFunc returns the files a commit changed. It runs in the background,
// and ends when ctx is done.
type LoadFunc func(ctx context.Context, c gitsrc.Commit) ([]source.File, error)

// CountFunc returns the line counts of the commits with the given hashes. It
// runs in the background, and ends when ctx is done.
type CountFunc func(ctx context.Context, hashes []string) (map[string]gitsrc.LineCounts, error)

// countsMsg brings the line counts of the commits hashes, or why they could
// not be counted.
type countsMsg struct {
        hashes []string
        counts map[string]gitsrc.LineCounts
        err    error
}

// countWidth is the width the count columns keep while counts arrive, so
// that they do not move with each batch.
const countWidth = 4

// commitEntry is one commit in the log. Its file list is created on
// first open and kept, so anchors survive going back to the list.
type commitEntry struct {
        gitsrc.Commit
        counted bool // Added and Removed are known
        diff    *Diff
}

// Log is the Bubble Tea model of the commit list (keys of commit_list).
// Enter shows the files of a commit, a Diff; N/P step through commits from
// the deeper views.
type Log struct {
        cfg    *config.Config
        branch string // shown above the commit list
        load   LoadFunc
        // counts counts the lines of the commits shown, in the background; nil
        // when the commits came counted or counting failed.
        counts    CountFunc
        relist    CommitsFunc             // lists the commits again, nil when they cannot be
        counting  bool                    // a count is running
        byHash    map[string]*commitEntry // the commits by hash
        minCount  int                     // the least width of the count columns
        cols      logColumns              // see measure
        loadGen   int                     // counts the requests of requestCommit
        pending   *commitRequest          // the commit loading, nil for none
        relisting relistState             // of the commits, see relistCommits
        // ctx ends the git commands of the log, cancel ends ctx, see stop.
        ctx    context.Context
        cancel context.CancelFunc

        commits []*commitEntry
        order   historyOrder // of commits
        list
        open     *commitEntry // commit whose files are shown, nil for the list
        fileList bool         // in flow mode: the file list of open, not the flow view
        flow     *Flow        // all commits one after the other
        msg      string
        prefix   string // the action, "jump", while waiting for the letter of a mark
}

// NewLog returns the commit list for commits of branch, in the order of git
// log: newest first, or with oldestFirst (git log --reverse) oldest. With
// counts, their line counts are counted once they are shown; without, they
// come with the commits.
func NewLog(cfg *config.Config, branch string, commits []gitsrc.Commit, oldestFirst bool,
        load LoadFunc, counts CountFunc) *Log {
        l := &Log{cfg: cfg, branch: branch, load: load, counts: counts, byHash: map[string]*commitEntry{}, minCount: 1,
                order: orderOf(oldestFirst)}
        l.ctx, l.cancel = context.WithCancel(context.Background())
        if counts != nil {
                l.minCount = countWidth
        }
        var parts []*flowPart
        for _, c := range commits {
                e := newCommitEntry(c, counts == nil)
                l.commits = append(l.commits, e)
                l.byHash[c.Hash] = e
                parts = append(parts, newCommitPart(e))
        }
        l.list = list{n: len(l.commits), preview: &cfg.Settings.Preview}
        l.measure()
        l.flow = newFlow(cfg, parts, func(p int) tea.Cmd {
                part := l.flow.parts[p]
                return l.loadCmd(l.ctx, part.commit, 0, part)
        })
        l.flow.order = l.order
        if len(commits) == 0 {
                l.msg = "no commits"
        }
        if w := cfg.Warning(); w != "" {
                l.msg = w
        }
        return l
}

// newCommitEntry returns the entry of c, counted when its line counts are
// known.
func newCommitEntry(c gitsrc.Commit, counted bool) *commitEntry {
        // Commits are only shown: their text is made printable once.
        c.Author, c.Subject = printable(c.Author), printable(c.Subject)
        body := strings.Split(c.Body, "\n")
        for i, line := range body {
                body[i] = printable(line)
        }
        c.Body = strings.Join(body, "\n")
        return &commitEntry{Commit: c, counted: counted}
}

// CommitsFunc lists the commits of the log again, with the branch. It runs
// in the background, and ends when ctx is done.
type CommitsFunc func(ctx context.Context) (branch string, commits []gitsrc.Commit, err error)

// WithRelist sets how r lists the commits again and returns l.
func (l *Log) WithRelist(list CommitsFunc) *Log {
        l.relist = list
        return l
}

func (l *Log) Init() tea.Cmd { return nil }

func (l *Log) Update(msg tea.Msg) (tea.Model, tea.Cmd) {
        if m, ok := msg.(countsMsg); ok {
                l.addCounts(m)
        } else if cmd := l.update(msg); cmd != nil {
                return l, cmd
        }
        return l, l.countShown()
}

// countShown returns the command counting the lines of the commits on the
// list's screen and a screen beyond that are not counted yet, or nil when
// they are, a count is running or the list is not shown.
func (l *Log) countShown() tea.Cmd {
        if l.counts == nil || l.counting || l.open != nil || l.height == 0 {
                return nil
        }
        var hashes []string
        for i := l.top; i < min(l.top+2*l.listHeight(), len(l.commits)); i++ {
                if e := l.commits[i]; !e.counted {
                        hashes = append(hashes, e.Hash)
                }
        }
        if len(hashes) == 0 {
                return nil
        }
        l.counting = true
        ctx, counts := l.ctx, l.counts
        return func() tea.Msg {
                c, err := counts(ctx, hashes)
                return countsMsg{hashes, c, err}
        }
}

// addCounts takes the line counts of m. After an error the counts are not
// tried again: the list stays without them.
func (l *Log) addCounts(m countsMsg) {
        l.counting = false
        if m.err != nil {
                l.counts = nil
                l.setMsg("line counts: " + firstLine(m.err))
                return
        }
        for _, h := range m.hashes {
                if e := l.byHash[h]; e != nil {
                        c := m.counts[h]
                        e.Added, e.Removed, e.counted = c.Added, c.Removed, true
                }
        }
        l.measure()
}

func (l *Log) update(msg tea.Msg) tea.Cmd {
        switch msg := msg.(type) {
        case tea.WindowSizeMsg:
                l.width, l.height = msg.Width, msg.Height
                l.scrollToCursor()
                if l.flowShown() {
                        _, cmd := l.flow.Update(msg)
                        return cmd
                }
        case settingChangedMsg:
                l.scrollToCursor() // the message box takes room from the list
                if msg.problem != "" && l.open == nil {
                        l.msg = msg.problem
                }
        case backMsg:
                if l.flowShown() {
                        return l.filesOfFlow()
                }
                l.open, l.fileList = nil, false
                return nil
        case flowAtMsg:
                return l.showFlow(int(msg), nil)
        case switchCommitMsg:
                return l.switchCommit(int(msg))
        case commitLoadedMsg:
                return l.commitLoaded(msg)
        case refreshMsg:
                return l.relistCommits(msg.quiet)
        case commitsListedMsg:
                if msg.log == l {
                        return l.commitsListed(msg)
                }
                return nil
        case showMsg:
                l.setMsg(string(msg))
                return nil
        case toggleUntrackedMsg:
                l.setMsg(untrackedOnlyInDiff)
                return nil
        case toggleFlowMsg:
                cmd := l.toggleFlow()
                if w := saveState(l.cfg); w != "" {
                        l.setMsg(w)
                }
                return cmd
        }
        if l.flowShown() {
                _, cmd := l.flow.Update(msg)
                l.cursor, _, _ = l.flow.position()
                l.scrollToCursor()
                return cmd
        }
        if l.open != nil {
                _, cmd := l.open.diff.Update(msg)
                return cmd
        }
        if k, ok := msg.(tea.KeyPressMsg); ok {
                return l.key(k.String())
        }
        return nil
}

func (l *Log) key(k string) tea.Cmd {
        l.msg = ""
        if l.prefix != "" {
                // Marks are lines of files: the list has none to jump to.
                l.prefix = ""
                if isMarkLetter(k) {
                        l.msg = "mark " + k + ": open a commit to jump to it"
                }
                return nil
        }
        h := l.cfg.Keys.Key(keys.CommitList, k)
        page := max(l.listHeight()-1, 1)
        if delta, _, ok := motion(h, page, len(l.commits)); ok {
                l.moveCursor(delta)
                return nil
        }
        switch h.Action {
        case "quit":
                return tea.Quit
        case "open":
                if l.cfg.Settings.Flow {
                        return l.showFlow(-1, nil)
                }
                return l.requestCommit(l.cursor, "")
        case "flow":
                return toggleFlow
        case "history":
                // The next or previous commit in history, as N and P go from its files.
                dir := historyDir(h)
                if i := l.cursor + l.order.step(dir); i >= 0 && i < len(l.commits) {
                        l.moveCursor(i - l.cursor)
                } else {
                        l.msg = ends(dir > 0, "last commit", "first commit")
                }
        case "jump":
                l.prefix, l.msg = h.Action, jumpPrompt
        default:
                return pageKey(l.cfg.Keys, h, false)
        }
        return nil
}

// flowShown reports whether the flow view is shown.
func (l *Log) flowShown() bool { return l.open != nil && l.cfg.Settings.Flow && !l.fileList }

// showFlow shows the flow view on file of the commit under the cursor,
// where its diff view from leaves it, or on its bar for from nil and on the
// commit's bar for file -1 (see Flow.showFrom), and returns the command
// loading the commits on the screen. A list that loading again emptied has
// no commit to show.
func (l *Log) showFlow(file int, from *FileDiff) tea.Cmd {
        if len(l.commits) == 0 {
                l.msg = "no commits"
                return nil
        }
        l.open, l.fileList = l.commits[l.cursor], false
        l.flow.width, l.flow.height = l.width, l.height
        l.flow.showFrom(l.cursor, file, from)
        return l.flow.nextLoad()
}

// toggleFlow switches between the flow view and the files of the commit
// under the cursor, keeping the cursor on its line and screen row, and
// returns the command loading the commits on the screen of the flow view.
func (l *Log) toggleFlow() tea.Cmd {
        st := &l.cfg.Settings
        if !st.Flow {
                st.Flow = true
                switch {
                case l.open == nil:
                        return l.showFlow(-1, nil)
                case l.open.diff.open == nil:
                        return l.showFlow(l.open.diff.cursor, nil)
                default:
                        return l.showFlow(l.open.diff.cursor, l.open.diff.open.view)
                }
        }
        st.Flow = false
        if l.open == nil {
                return nil
        }
        if l.fileList { // the file list stays
                l.fileList = false
                return nil
        }
        p, file, line := l.flow.position()
        screenRow := l.flow.cursor - l.flow.top
        path := ""
        if e := l.commits[p]; e.diff != nil && file >= 0 {
                path = e.diff.files[file].Path
        }
        l.open = nil
        if l.commits[p].diff == nil { // still loading: its files when they come
                return l.requestCommit(p, "")
        }
        if !l.openCommit(p, path) {
                return nil
        }
        d := l.open.diff
        if d.open == nil && file >= 0 {
                d.cursor = file
                d.scrollToCursor()
        }
        if d.open != nil && d.open.Path == path {
                d.open.view.takeOver(l.flow, line, screenRow)
        }
        return nil
}

// diff returns the file list of commit i, loading its files on first
// use.
func (l *Log) diff(i int) (*Diff, error) {
        e := l.commits[i]
        if e.diff == nil {
                files, err := l.load(l.ctx, e.Commit)
                if err != nil {
                        return nil, err
                }
                e.diff = newDiff(l.cfg, files, &e.Commit)
        }
        return e.diff, nil
}

// openCommit shows the files of commit i, opening path directly when the
// commit changed it.
func (l *Log) openCommit(i int, path string) bool {
        if i < 0 || i >= len(l.commits) {
                return false
        }
        e := l.commits[i]
        if _, err := l.diff(i); err != nil {
                l.msg = firstLine(err)
                return false
        }
        e.diff.Update(tea.WindowSizeMsg{Width: l.width, Height: l.height})
        if path == "" || !e.diff.openPath(path) {
                e.diff.open = nil
        }
        l.open = e
        l.cursor = i
        l.scrollToCursor()
        return true
}

// switchCommit shows the next (dir > 0) or previous commit in history,
// keeping the open file when the other commit changed it too, and returns
// the command loading it, see requestCommit.
func (l *Log) switchCommit(dir int) tea.Cmd {
        if l.open == nil {
                return nil
        }
        d := l.open.diff
        path := ""
        if d.open != nil {
                path = d.open.Path
        }
        i := l.cursor + l.order.step(dir)
        if i < 0 || i >= len(l.commits) {
                d.setMsg(ends(dir > 0, "last commit", "first commit"))
                return nil
        }
        if d.open != nil {
                d.open.updateStats()
        }
        return l.requestCommit(i, path)
}

// commitLoadedMsg brings the files of the commit with hash, or why they
// could not be loaded: for the request number gen of requestCommit or
// filesOfFlow, 0 for none, and for part of the flow view, nil for none.
type commitLoadedMsg struct {
        gen   int
        hash  string
        files []source.File
        err   error
        part  *flowPart
}

// loadCmd returns the command loading the files of e in the background, for
// request gen and the flow's part, see commitLoadedMsg; ctx ends it. Only git
// runs there: the views are made when the files come, on the keys' side,
// which owns the settings.
func (l *Log) loadCmd(ctx context.Context, e *commitEntry, gen int, part *flowPart) tea.Cmd {
        c, load := e.Commit, l.load
        return func() tea.Msg {
                files, err := load(ctx, c)
                return commitLoadedMsg{gen, c.Hash, files, err, part}
        }
}

// commitRequest is the commit requestCommit loads: shown with path open
// when it arrives, if the view is still from, which it was asked from; with
// files, as filesOfFlow asks, its file list when the flow view still shows
// it. msg says that it loads; cancel ends its git commands.
type commitRequest struct {
        gen       int
        path, msg string
        from      *commitEntry
        files     bool
        cancel    context.CancelFunc
}

// request starts loading the files of e for req, ending any request before
// and its git commands, and returns the command; the status bar says that
// it loads.
func (l *Log) request(e *commitEntry, req commitRequest) tea.Cmd {
        if l.pending != nil {
                l.pending.cancel()
        }
        l.loadGen++
        req.gen, req.msg = l.loadGen, "loading "+e.Short+"…"
        ctx, cancel := context.WithCancel(l.ctx)
        req.cancel = cancel
        l.pending = &req
        l.setMsg(req.msg)
        return l.loadCmd(ctx, e, req.gen, nil)
}

// stop ends the git commands the log runs in the background: the view is
// replaced, or the program ends.
func (l *Log) stop() { l.cancel() }

// requestCommit shows commit i as openCommit does, at once when its files
// are loaded, else it returns the command loading them, which runs git
// without blocking the keys. The status bar says what loads meanwhile, and
// the view stays usable; moving to another view, or another request, drops
// the files when they come.
func (l *Log) requestCommit(i int, path string) tea.Cmd {
        if i < 0 || i >= len(l.commits) {
                return nil
        }
        e := l.commits[i]
        if e.diff != nil {
                if !l.openCommit(i, path) && l.open != nil {
                        l.open.diff.setMsg(l.msg)
                }
                return nil
        }
        return l.request(e, commitRequest{path: path, from: l.open})
}

// commitLoaded takes the files msg brings: they become the commit's, unless
// it has some already, loaded for the list or the flow view meanwhile. The
// flow view, when it loaded them, shows them; a request shows the commit,
// when it is still the one asked for last and the view is the one it was
// asked from. It returns the command loading the next commit of the flow
// view.
func (l *Log) commitLoaded(msg commitLoadedMsg) tea.Cmd {
        e := l.byHash[msg.hash]
        if e != nil && e.diff == nil && msg.err == nil {
                e.diff = newDiff(l.cfg, msg.files, &e.Commit)
        }
        var cmd tea.Cmd
        if msg.part != nil {
                cmd = l.flow.partLoaded(msg.part, msg.err)
        }
        req := l.pending
        if req == nil || msg.gen != req.gen {
                return cmd
        }
        l.pending = nil
        req.cancel()
        l.dropMsg(req.msg)
        switch {
        case e == nil:
        case req.files:
                if p, _, _ := l.flow.position(); l.flowShown() && l.commits[p] == e {
                        cmd = batch(cmd, l.filesOfFlow())
                }
        case l.open != req.from || l.flowShown():
        case msg.err != nil:
                l.setMsg(firstLine(msg.err))
        default:
                l.openCommit(slices.Index(l.commits, e), req.path)
        }
        return cmd
}

// commitsListedMsg brings the commits log listed again, with the branch, or
// why it could not.
type commitsListedMsg struct {
        log     *Log
        branch  string
        commits []gitsrc.Commit
        err     error
        quiet   bool
}

// relistCommits returns the command listing the commits again (r) in the
// background, as Diff.relistFiles does the files: the commits stay usable
// meanwhile, and a listing asked for while one runs runs after it.
func (l *Log) relistCommits(quiet bool) tea.Cmd {
        if l.relist == nil {
                l.setMsg(nothingToReload)
                return nil
        }
        if !l.relisting.start(quiet) {
                return nil
        }
        if !quiet {
                l.setMsg(reloading)
        }
        ctx, list := l.ctx, l.relist
        return func() tea.Msg {
                branch, commits, err := list(ctx)
                return commitsListedMsg{l, branch, commits, err, quiet}
        }
}

// commitsListed takes the commits listed again. New commits are added and
// gone ones dropped; a commit still there keeps its entry, and so its files
// with their anchors and folds: its content cannot change. The cursor stays
// on its commit; when the commit shown is gone, the list is shown. It returns
// the command listing them once more when that was asked for meanwhile.
func (l *Log) commitsListed(res commitsListedMsg) tea.Cmd {
        var next tea.Cmd
        if again, quiet := l.relisting.done(); again {
                next = l.relistCommits(quiet)
        }
        branch, commits, quiet := res.branch, res.commits, res.quiet
        if res.err != nil {
                l.setMsg(firstLine(res.err))
                return next
        }
        parts := map[*commitEntry]*flowPart{}
        for _, p := range l.flow.parts {
                parts[p.commit] = p
        }
        var cur *commitEntry
        if l.cursor < len(l.commits) {
                cur = l.commits[l.cursor]
        }
        kept, oldParts, shown, open := l.byHash, l.flow.parts, l.flowShown(), l.open
        if shown {
                p, _, _ := l.flow.position()
                open = oldParts[p].commit
        }
        l.branch, l.commits, l.flow.parts, l.byHash = branch, nil, nil, map[string]*commitEntry{}
        added := 0
        for _, c := range commits {
                e, p := kept[c.Hash], (*flowPart)(nil)
                if e != nil {
                        delete(kept, c.Hash)
                        p = parts[e]
                } else {
                        e = newCommitEntry(c, l.counts == nil)
                        p = newCommitPart(e)
                        added++
                }
                l.commits = append(l.commits, e)
                l.byHash[c.Hash] = e
                l.flow.parts = append(l.flow.parts, p)
        }
        l.list.n = len(l.commits)
        l.measure()
        if i := slices.Index(l.commits, cur); i >= 0 {
                l.cursor = i
        } else {
                l.cursor = max(min(l.cursor, len(l.commits)-1), 0)
        }
        msg := refreshed(added, len(kept), 0, 0)
        switch {
        case open != nil && kept[open.Hash] == open:
                msg = "commit " + open.Short + " is gone; " + msg
                l.open = nil
        case shown:
                l.flow.followParts(oldParts)
                l.flow.keepScreenRow(l.flow.relocate)
                next = batch(next, l.flow.nextLoad())
                l.cursor, _, _ = l.flow.position()
                l.open = l.commits[l.cursor]
        case open != nil:
                l.cursor = slices.Index(l.commits, open)
        }
        l.scrollToCursor()
        if !quiet || msg != nothingChanged {
                l.setMsg(msg)
        }
        return next
}

// filesOfFlow shows the file list of the commit under the flow view's
// cursor, on its file, as backspace does from a diff view. A commit that
// cannot be loaded has no list to show: the commits are shown, on it. A
// commit still loading shows its list when its files come; it returns the
// command loading them.
func (l *Log) filesOfFlow() tea.Cmd {
        p, file, _ := l.flow.position()
        l.cursor = p
        l.scrollToCursor()
        e := l.commits[p]
        if e.diff == nil && l.flow.parts[p].err == "" { // its list when it comes
                return l.request(e, commitRequest{files: true})
        }
        if e.diff == nil {
                l.open, l.fileList = nil, false
                return nil
        }
        d := e.diff
        d.Update(tea.WindowSizeMsg{Width: l.width, Height: l.height})
        d.open = nil
        if file >= 0 {
                d.cursor = file
                d.scrollToCursor()
        }
        l.open, l.fileList = l.commits[p], true
        return nil
}

// dropMsg clears msg where setMsg may have put it, unless another message
// took its place.
func (l *Log) dropMsg(msg string) {
        if l.msg == msg {
                l.msg = ""
        }
        if l.flow.msg == msg {
                l.flow.msg = ""
        }
        for _, e := range l.commits {
                if d := e.diff; d != nil {
                        if d.msg == msg {
                                d.msg = ""
                        }
                        if d.open != nil && d.open.view != nil && d.open.view.msg == msg {
                                d.open.view.msg = ""
                        }
                }
        }
}

// setMsg shows msg in the status bar of whatever is displayed.
func (l *Log) setMsg(msg string) {
        switch {
        case l.flowShown():
                l.flow.msg = msg
        case l.open != nil:
                l.open.diff.setMsg(msg)
        default:
                l.msg = msg
        }
}

// maxAuthorWidth caps the author column.
const maxAuthorWidth = 16

// logColumns are the widths the columns of the commit list need: the digits
// of the counts, the author, the subject, and hash and date together.
type logColumns struct{ added, removed, author, subject, fixed int }

// measure computes the widths of the columns, after the commits or their
// counts changed: measuring them on each frame takes too long in a long
// history.
func (l *Log) measure() {
        c := logColumns{added: l.minCount, removed: l.minCount}
        for _, e := range l.commits {
                c.added = max(c.added, len(fmt.Sprint(e.Added)))
                c.removed = max(c.removed, len(fmt.Sprint(e.Removed)))
                c.author = max(c.author, min(ansi.StringWidth(e.Author), maxAuthorWidth))
                c.subject = max(c.subject, ansi.StringWidth(e.Subject))
                c.fixed = max(c.fixed, ansi.StringWidth(e.Short)+ansi.StringWidth(e.Date))
        }
        l.cols = c
}

func (l *Log) View() tea.View { return tea.NewView(l.frame()) }

func (l *Log) frame() string {
        if l.flowShown() {
                return l.flow.frame()
        }
        if l.open != nil {
                return l.open.diff.frame()
        }
        if l.width == 0 {
                return ""
        }
        t := l.cfg.Theme
        addW, remW, authW := l.cols.added, l.cols.removed, l.cols.author
        // The subject gets the room left by the other columns, so that author
        // and date stay aligned and visible.
        fixedW := l.cols.fixed + 1 + 2 + (addW + 1) + 1 + (remW + 1) + 2 + 2 + authW + 2
        subjW := max(min(l.cols.subject, l.width-fixedW), 1)

        count := fmt.Sprintf("(%d commits)", len(l.commits))
        if len(l.commits) == 1 {
                count = "(1 commit)"
        }
        head := " " + t.Branch.Render(l.branch) + " " + t.Heading.Render(count)
        item := func(i int, st func(lipgloss.Style) lipgloss.Style) string {
                c := l.commits[i]
                author := pad(ansi.Truncate(c.Author, maxAuthorWidth, "…"), authW, lipgloss.NewStyle())
                subject := ansi.Truncate(c.Subject, subjW, "…")
                subject += strings.Repeat(" ", subjW-ansi.StringWidth(subject))
                return " " + st(t.CommitHash).Render(c.Short) + "  " + counts(t, st, c.Added, c.Removed, addW, remW) + "  " +
                        st(lipgloss.NewStyle()).Render(subject) + "  " + st(t.Decoration).Render(author) + "  " +
                        st(t.Decoration).Render(c.Date)
        }
        hints := statusKeys(l.cfg, barKeys{enter: "open", flow: true})
        return l.render(t, head, item, func(h int) string { return l.messageBox(l.commits[l.cursor].Commit, h) }, pageStatus(l.cfg, l.msg, hints))
}

// messageBox renders the full message of c in a box height lines high,
// with the hash in the top border. A last … line marks a cut message.
func (l *Log) messageBox(c gitsrc.Commit, height int) string {
        t := l.cfg.Theme
        lines := []string{t.Heading.Inherit(t.Zero).Render(c.Subject)}
        if c.Body != "" {
                lines = append(lines, "")
                for line := range strings.SplitSeq(c.Body, "\n") {
                        lines = append(lines, t.Zero.Render(line))
                }
        }
        if n := height - 2; len(lines) > n {
                lines = lines[:n]
                if n > 1 {
                        lines[n-1] = t.Hint.Render("…")
                }
        }
        return box(t, l.width, height, t.CommitHash.Render(" "+c.Hash+" "), lines)
}

package ui

import (
        "fmt"
        "strings"
        "unicode"

        "github.com/charmbracelet/x/ansi"
        "github.com/yuin/goldmark"
        "github.com/yuin/goldmark/ast"
        "github.com/yuin/goldmark/extension"
        east "github.com/yuin/goldmark/extension/ast"
        "github.com/yuin/goldmark/text"
)

// docStyle is what a piece of a page is, which decides its style in the
// theme when it is drawn.
type docStyle int

const (
        docPlain docStyle = iota
        docBold
        docItalic
        docCode
        docHeading
        docLink
        docDecoration // bullets, rules, the lines of tables and quotes
        docOverflow   // » at the end of a cut line
)

// docSeg is a piece of a line of a page in one style: printable text, part
// of link number link of the page, -1 for none.
type docSeg struct {
        text  string
        style docStyle
        link  int
}

// docLine is a line of a page; anchor is the name of the heading it starts,
// as links to it write it after #.
type docLine struct {
        segs   []docSeg
        anchor string
}

// plain returns the text of l, without styles.
func (l docLine) plain() string {
        var b strings.Builder
        for _, s := range l.segs {
                b.WriteString(s.text)
        }
        return b.String()
}

// docPage is a markdown page rendered for a width: its lines, none wider,
// and the targets of its links with the line each starts on.
type docPage struct {
        lines   []docLine
        links   []pageLink
        anchors map[string]int // the line of each heading
}

// pageLink is a link of a page: where it goes, as written, and its first
// line; broken when it leads nowhere, which Readme decides.
type pageLink struct {
        target string
        line   int
        broken bool
}

var markdown = goldmark.New(goldmark.WithExtensions(extension.Table))

// renderMarkdown renders src for a screen width columns wide. Images are left
// out, so a line of badges is gone; a line still too wide, of code or a table,
// is cut with ».
func renderMarkdown(src []byte, width int) docPage {
        width = max(width, 1)
        r := &mdRenderer{src: src}
        lines := r.blocks(markdown.Parser().Parse(text.NewReader(src)), width, false)
        page := docPage{anchors: map[string]int{}}
        for i := range r.links {
                page.links = append(page.links, pageLink{target: r.links[i], line: -1})
        }
        for i, l := range lines {
                l.segs = cutSegs(l.segs, width)
                lines[i] = l
                if l.anchor != "" {
                        page.anchors[l.anchor] = i
                }
                for _, s := range l.segs {
                        if s.link >= 0 && page.links[s.link].line < 0 {
                                page.links[s.link].line = i
                        }
                }
        }
        page.lines = lines
        return page
}

// renderText renders a file that is no markdown for a screen width columns
// wide: its lines as code, the long ones wrapped at the last space that
// fits, past their indentation, else where the line ends; the further pieces
// indented.
func renderText(src []byte, width int) docPage {
        width = max(width, 1)
        page := docPage{anchors: map[string]int{}}
        text := strings.TrimSuffix(strings.ReplaceAll(string(src), "\r\n", "\n"), "\n")
        for _, line := range strings.Split(text, "\n") {
                rest, lead, w := printable(line), "", width
                add := func(s string) {
                        page.lines = append(page.lines, docLine{segs: trimSegs([]docSeg{{lead, docPlain, -1}, {s, docCode, -1}})})
                }
                for ansi.StringWidth(rest) > w {
                        piece := ansi.Truncate(rest, w, "")
                        if i := strings.LastIndex(piece, " "); i > 0 && strings.TrimSpace(piece[:i]) != "" {
                                piece = piece[:i+1]
                        }
                        add(piece)
                        rest = rest[len(piece):]
                        if lead == "" && width > 2 {
                                lead, w = "  ", width-2
                        }
                }
                add(rest)
        }
        return page
}

// mdRenderer renders the blocks of a markdown tree into lines.
type mdRenderer struct {
        src     []byte
        links   []string        // the targets of the links found so far
        anchors map[string]bool // the anchors given so far
        depth   int             // of nested lists
}

// blocks renders the children of n one below the other, with an empty line
// between them unless tight, as in the items of a tight list.
func (r *mdRenderer) blocks(n ast.Node, width int, tight bool) []docLine {
        var out []docLine
        for c := n.FirstChild(); c != nil; c = c.NextSibling() {
                lines := r.block(c, width)
                if len(lines) == 0 {
                        continue
                }
                if len(out) > 0 && !tight {
                        out = append(out, docLine{})
                }
                out = append(out, lines...)
        }
        return out
}

// block renders the block n, width columns wide but for code.
func (r *mdRenderer) block(n ast.Node, width int) []docLine {
        switch n := n.(type) {
        case *ast.Heading:
                lines := wrapSegs(r.inline(n, docHeading), width)
                if len(lines) == 0 {
                        return nil
                }
                lines[0].anchor = r.anchor(n)
                if n.Level <= 2 {
                        w := 0
                        for _, l := range lines {
                                w = max(w, segsWidth(l.segs))
                        }
                        rule := "─"
                        if n.Level == 1 {
                                rule = "═"
                        }
                        lines = append(lines, docLine{segs: []docSeg{{strings.Repeat(rule, w), docDecoration, -1}}})
                }
                return lines
        case *ast.Paragraph, *ast.TextBlock:
                return wrapSegs(r.inline(n, docPlain), width)
        case *ast.List:
                return r.list(n, width)
        case *ast.FencedCodeBlock, *ast.CodeBlock:
                var out []docLine
                lines := n.Lines()
                for i := range lines.Len() {
                        seg := lines.At(i)
                        line := printable(strings.TrimRight(string(seg.Value(r.src)), "\r\n"))
                        out = append(out, docLine{segs: trimSegs([]docSeg{{"  ", docPlain, -1}, {line, docCode, -1}})})
                }
                return out
        case *ast.Blockquote:
                inner := r.blocks(n, max(width-2, 1), false)
                for i := range inner {
                        inner[i].segs = append([]docSeg{{"│ ", docDecoration, -1}}, inner[i].segs...)
                }
                return inner
        case *ast.ThematicBreak:
                return []docLine{{segs: []docSeg{{strings.Repeat("─", width), docDecoration, -1}}}}
        case *east.Table:
                return r.table(n, width)
        }
        return nil // HTML
}

// bullets are the marks of the items of unordered lists, by depth.
var bullets = []string{"•", "◦", "▪"}

// list renders the items of n, each after its bullet or number, its further
// lines indented to its text.
func (r *mdRenderer) list(n *ast.List, width int) []docLine {
        var marks []string
        for c, i := n.FirstChild(), 0; c != nil; c, i = c.NextSibling(), i+1 {
                if n.IsOrdered() {
                        marks = append(marks, fmt.Sprintf("%d.", n.Start+i))
                } else {
                        marks = append(marks, bullets[min(r.depth, len(bullets)-1)])
                }
        }
        markW := 0
        for _, m := range marks {
                markW = max(markW, ansi.StringWidth(m))
        }
        indent := markW + 1
        r.depth++
        defer func() { r.depth-- }()
        var out []docLine
        for c, i := n.FirstChild(), 0; c != nil; c, i = c.NextSibling(), i+1 {
                if i > 0 && !n.IsTight {
                        out = append(out, docLine{})
                }
                lines := r.blocks(c, max(width-indent, 1), n.IsTight)
                if len(lines) == 0 {
                        lines = []docLine{{}}
                }
                for j, l := range lines {
                        lead := strings.Repeat(" ", indent)
                        style := docPlain
                        if j == 0 {
                                lead, style = fmt.Sprintf("%*s ", markW, marks[i]), docDecoration
                        }
                        if len(l.segs) == 0 && j > 0 {
                                out = append(out, l) // an empty line stays empty
                                continue
                        }
                        l.segs = append([]docSeg{{lead, style, -1}}, l.segs...)
                        out = append(out, l)
                }
        }
        return out
}

// table renders t with its columns aligned between bars, the header bold
// and ruled off. When it does not fit, the widest column is narrowed and its
// cells wrapped, down to tableMinColumn; beyond that the lines are cut.
func (r *mdRenderer) table(t *east.Table, width int) []docLine {
        var rows [][][]docSeg
        for row := t.FirstChild(); row != nil; row = row.NextSibling() {
                style := docPlain
                if _, ok := row.(*east.TableHeader); ok {
                        style = docBold
                }
                var cells [][]docSeg
                for cell := row.FirstChild(); cell != nil; cell = cell.NextSibling() {
                        cells = append(cells, r.inline(cell, style))
                }
                rows = append(rows, cells)
        }
        if len(rows) == 0 {
                return nil
        }
        cols := len(t.Alignments)
        widths := make([]int, cols)
        for _, cells := range rows {
                for i, c := range cells {
                        if i < cols {
                                for _, l := range wrapSegs(c, 1<<20) {
                                        widths[i] = max(widths[i], segsWidth(l.segs))
                                }
                        }
                }
        }
        const gap = 3 // " │ "
        total := func() int {
                sum := gap * (cols - 1)
                for _, w := range widths {
                        sum += w
                }
                return sum
        }
        for total() > width {
                widest := 0
                for i, w := range widths {
                        if w > widths[widest] {
                                widest = i
                        }
                }
                if widths[widest] <= tableMinColumn {
                        break
                }
                widths[widest]--
        }
        bar := docSeg{" │ ", docDecoration, -1}
        var out []docLine
        for ri, cells := range rows {
                wrapped := make([][]docLine, cols)
                height := 1
                for i := range cols {
                        if i < len(cells) {
                                wrapped[i] = wrapSegs(cells[i], widths[i])
                        }
                        height = max(height, len(wrapped[i]))
                }
                for j := range height {
                        var segs []docSeg
                        for i := range cols {
                                if i > 0 {
                                        segs = append(segs, bar)
                                }
                                var cell []docSeg
                                if j < len(wrapped[i]) {
                                        cell = wrapped[i][j].segs
                                }
                                segs = append(segs, alignSegs(cell, widths[i], t.Alignments[i])...)
                        }
                        out = append(out, docLine{segs: trimSegs(segs)})
                }
                if ri == 0 {
                        var rule []string
                        for _, w := range widths {
                                rule = append(rule, strings.Repeat("─", w))
                        }
                        out = append(out, docLine{segs: []docSeg{{strings.Join(rule, "─┼─"), docDecoration, -1}}})
                }
        }
        return out
}

// tableMinColumn is the narrowest a column of a table is wrapped to.
const tableMinColumn = 10

// alignSegs pads segs to width as align says: right, centered or left.
func alignSegs(segs []docSeg, width int, align east.Alignment) []docSeg {
        pad := max(width-segsWidth(segs), 0)
        left := 0
        switch align {
        case east.AlignRight:
                left = pad
        case east.AlignCenter:
                left = pad / 2
        }
        out := []docSeg{{strings.Repeat(" ", left), docPlain, -1}}
        out = append(out, segs...)
        return append(out, docSeg{strings.Repeat(" ", pad-left), docPlain, -1})
}

// trimSegs drops the spaces at the end of a line.
func trimSegs(segs []docSeg) []docSeg {
        for len(segs) > 0 {
                last := &segs[len(segs)-1]
                last.text = strings.TrimRight(last.text, " ")
                if last.text != "" {
                        break
                }
                segs = segs[:len(segs)-1]
        }
        return segs
}

// anchor returns the name links give heading h after #, as GitHub makes it:
// lower case, spaces as -, without punctuation, numbered when it repeats.
func (r *mdRenderer) anchor(h *ast.Heading) string {
        var b strings.Builder
        for _, s := range r.inlineQuiet(h) {
                for _, c := range strings.ToLower(s.text) {
                        switch {
                        case c == ' ':
                                b.WriteRune('-')
                        case c == '-' || c == '_' || unicode.IsLetter(c) || unicode.IsDigit(c):
                                b.WriteRune(c)
                        }
                }
        }
        name := b.String()
        if r.anchors == nil {
                r.anchors = map[string]bool{}
        }
        for i := 1; r.anchors[name]; i++ {
                name = fmt.Sprintf("%s-%d", b.String(), i)
        }
        r.anchors[name] = true
        return name
}

// inlineQuiet returns the inline pieces of n without counting its links.
func (r *mdRenderer) inlineQuiet(n ast.Node) []docSeg {
        links := len(r.links)
        segs := r.inline(n, docPlain)
        r.links = r.links[:links]
        return segs
}

// inline returns the text of the inline children of n in style, as pieces;
// emphasis and code inside it take their own style, but in a heading or a
// link. A hard line break is a piece "\n".
func (r *mdRenderer) inline(n ast.Node, style docStyle) []docSeg {
        var out []docSeg
        r.inlineInto(n, style, -1, &out)
        return out
}

func (r *mdRenderer) inlineInto(n ast.Node, style docStyle, link int, out *[]docSeg) {
        inner := func(s docStyle) docStyle {
                if style == docHeading || style == docLink {
                        return style
                }
                return s
        }
        for c := n.FirstChild(); c != nil; c = c.NextSibling() {
                switch c := c.(type) {
                case *ast.Text:
                        *out = append(*out, docSeg{printable(string(c.Segment.Value(r.src))), style, link})
                        switch {
                        case c.HardLineBreak():
                                *out = append(*out, docSeg{"\n", style, link})
                        case c.SoftLineBreak():
                                *out = append(*out, docSeg{" ", style, link})
                        }
                case *ast.String:
                        *out = append(*out, docSeg{printable(string(c.Value)), style, link})
                case *ast.CodeSpan:
                        var b strings.Builder
                        for t := c.FirstChild(); t != nil; t = t.NextSibling() {
                                if t, ok := t.(*ast.Text); ok {
                                        b.Write(t.Segment.Value(r.src))
                                }
                        }
                        *out = append(*out, docSeg{printable(b.String()), inner(docCode), link})
                case *ast.Emphasis:
                        s := docItalic
                        if c.Level >= 2 {
                                s = docBold
                        }
                        r.inlineInto(c, inner(s), link, out)
                case *ast.Link:
                        id, before := len(r.links), len(*out)
                        r.links = append(r.links, string(c.Destination))
                        r.inlineInto(c, docLink, id, out)
                        if len(*out) == before { // only an image: a badge
                                r.links = r.links[:id]
                        }
                case *ast.AutoLink:
                        id := len(r.links)
                        r.links = append(r.links, string(c.URL(r.src)))
                        *out = append(*out, docSeg{printable(string(c.Label(r.src))), docLink, id})
                case *ast.Image, *ast.RawHTML:
                default:
                        r.inlineInto(c, style, link, out)
                }
        }
}

// segsWidth returns the width of segs in columns.
func segsWidth(segs []docSeg) int {
        w := 0
        for _, s := range segs {
                w += ansi.StringWidth(s.text)
        }
        return w
}

// wordToken is a word of inline text, which may be made of pieces in several
// styles, and the space before it, in the style of the text it was in.
type wordToken struct {
        space docSeg
        parts []docSeg
        brk   bool // a hard line break
}

// wrapSegs fills lines of width columns with the words of segs, breaking
// at spaces; a word wider than a line is broken where the line ends.
func wrapSegs(segs []docSeg, width int) []docLine {
        var words []wordToken
        cur := wordToken{}
        for _, s := range segs {
                if s.text == "\n" {
                        words = append(words, cur, wordToken{brk: true})
                        cur = wordToken{}
                        continue
                }
                for i, part := range strings.Split(s.text, " ") {
                        if i > 0 {
                                words = append(words, cur)
                                cur = wordToken{space: docSeg{" ", s.style, s.link}}
                        }
                        if part != "" {
                                cur.parts = append(cur.parts, docSeg{part, s.style, s.link})
                        }
                }
        }
        words = append(words, cur)

        var out []docLine
        var line []docSeg
        lineW := 0
        flush := func() {
                out = append(out, docLine{segs: line})
                line, lineW = nil, 0
        }
        for _, w := range words {
                if w.brk {
                        flush()
                        continue
                }
                if len(w.parts) == 0 {
                        continue
                }
                ww := segsWidth(w.parts)
                switch {
                case lineW == 0:
                case lineW+1+ww <= width:
                        line = append(line, w.space)
                        lineW++
                default:
                        flush()
                }
                for ww > width-lineW {
                        // Only a word too long for a line of its own is broken.
                        if lineW > 0 {
                                flush()
                                continue
                        }
                        line = sliceSegs(w.parts, 0, width)
                        w.parts = sliceSegs(w.parts, width, ww)
                        ww -= width
                        flush()
                }
                line = append(line, w.parts...)
                lineW += ww
        }
        if len(line) > 0 {
                flush()
        }
        return out
}

// sliceSegs returns the columns from to to of segs.
func sliceSegs(segs []docSeg, from, to int) []docSeg {
        var out []docSeg
        col := 0
        for _, s := range segs {
