                a.cell(right, v, sub, w, nw, cursor, lo)
}

func (m *FileDiff) status() string {
        msg := m.msg
        if msg == "" {
                msg = m.markPrompt()
        }
        left := pageStatus(m.cfg, msg, m.help())
        row := 0
        if m.cursor < len(m.view) {
                row = m.view[m.cursor].row + 1
        }
        right := fmt.Sprintf(" row %d/%d · anchors %d", row, len(m.rows), len(m.anchors))
        return statusLine(m.cfg.Theme, m.width, left, right)
}

// fold renders displayed row v, a fold of unchanged rows, across both panes
// of a view width columns wide.
func (a *fileAlign) fold(v int, cursor bool, width int) string {
        label := fmt.Sprintf(" ⋯ %d unchanged lines ⋯ ", a.view[v].hidden)
        if cursor {
                label = a.cfg.Theme.Cursor.Render(label)
        }
        side := max(width-ansi.StringWidth(label), 0)
        line := a.cfg.Theme.Decoration.Render(strings.Repeat("─", side/2)) + label +
                a.cfg.Theme.Decoration.Render(strings.Repeat("─", side-side/2))
        return ansi.Truncate(line, width, "")
}

// cell renders piece of side s of displayed row v: marker, line number,
// separator and the line content; further pieces of a wrapped line have no
// number, and a side without that piece is empty. On the cursor row both
// sides are bold, and the line number of the active side is in the cursor
// style.
func (a *fileAlign) cell(s side, v, piece, width, numWidth int, cursor bool, lo layout) string {
        t := a.cfg.Theme
        row := a.rows[a.view[v].row]
        line := a.lineAt(s, v)
        changed := row.Kind != diff.Equal && line >= 0
        sepStyle := t.NumLeft
        if s == right {
                sepStyle = t.NumRight
        }

        contentWidth := max(width-numWidth-3, 0)
        num := strings.Repeat(" ", numWidth)
        if line >= 0 && piece >= a.pieces(s, line, lo.shape) {
                // The other side's line takes more pieces.
                return strings.Repeat(" ", 1+numWidth) + sepStyle.Render("│") + strings.Repeat(" ", 1+contentWidth)
        }
        if line >= 0 && piece == 0 {
                num = fmt.Sprintf("%*d", numWidth, line+1)
        }
        pending := piece == 0 && a.pending != nil && a.pending.side == s && a.pending.line == line && line >= 0
        _, moved := a.movedTo(s, line)
        ls := lineState{s: s, row: row, line: line, changed: changed, pending: pending, moved: moved,
                cont: piece > 0 && line >= 0, cursor: cursor, active: cursor && s == lo.active}
        gutter := a.gutter(ls, num, sepStyle)

        if line < 0 {
                return gutter + t.Filler.Render(strings.Repeat("╱", contentWidth))
        }

        return gutter + a.content(ls, contentWidth, startCol(piece, contentWidth, lo), lo.wrap == 0)
}

// lineState is what gutter and content draw a line by; cell and unifiedLine
// each work it out once.
type lineState struct {
        s       side
        row     diff.Row // the row the line is shown in
        line    int      // the line of side s, -1 for none
        changed bool     // a changed line of its side
        pending bool     // the pending end of an anchor
        moved   bool     // moved, drawn in the moved style
        cont    bool     // a further piece of a wrapped line
        cursor  bool     // on the cursor row
        active  bool     // on the cursor row and the active side
}

// gutter renders what precedes the line of ls: the marker, the line numbers
// nums and the separator in sepStyle. The marker is ↪ on further pieces of a
// wrapped line, ● on the pending end of an anchor, ◆ on anchors and - or + on
// changed lines, in the moved style for moved ones; changed lines also have
// their side's number style. On the cursor line marker and numbers are bold,
// on the active side the numbers are in the cursor style.
func (a *fileAlign) gutter(ls lineState, nums string, sepStyle lipgloss.Style) string {
        t := a.cfg.Theme
        s, changed, cursor := ls.s, ls.changed, ls.cursor
        lineStyle, _, _, _ := a.sideStyles(s)
        if ls.moved {
                lineStyle = a.movedStyle(s)
        }
        numStyle := t.NumZero
        marker := " "
        switch {
        case ls.cont && changed:
                marker = boldIf(lineStyle, cursor).Render("↪")
        case ls.cont:
                marker = boldIf(t.Decoration, cursor).Render("↪")
        case ls.pending:
                marker = t.Pending.Render("●")
        case ls.row.Anchored:
                marker = boldIf(t.Anchor, cursor).Render("◆")
        case changed && s == left:
                marker = boldIf(lineStyle, cursor).Render("-")
        case changed:
                marker = boldIf(lineStyle, cursor).Render("+")
        }
        if changed {
                numStyle = t.NumPlus
                if s == left {
                        numStyle = t.NumMinus
                }
        }
        if ls.active {
                // The cursor's line numbers stand out in the cursor style, in
                // their own color.
                numStyle = t.Cursor.Inherit(numStyle)
        } else {
                numStyle = boldIf(numStyle, cursor)
        }
        return marker + numStyle.Render(nums) + sepStyle.Render("│") + " "
}

// boldIf returns style, bold when on is set.
func boldIf(style lipgloss.Style, on bool) lipgloss.Style {
        if on {
                return style.Bold(true)
        }
        return style
}

// movedStyle returns the style of moved lines of side s.
func (a *fileAlign) movedStyle(s side) lipgloss.Style {
        if s == left {
                return a.cfg.Theme.MinusMoved
        }
        return a.cfg.Theme.PlusMoved
}

// content renders the line of ls width columns wide with changes
// emphasized, from column from on. On the cursor line unchanged text
// is bold; a moved line is drawn in the moved style, without emphasis.
// With cut, a line that goes on past the pane ends in Extends, one with
// text scrolled out to the left starts with Precedes.
func (a *fileAlign) content(ls lineState, width, from int, cut bool) string {
        t := a.cfg.Theme
        s, row, line, cursor, moved := ls.s, ls.row, ls.line, ls.cursor, ls.moved
        lineStyle, nonEmph, emph, indent := a.sideStyles(s)
        st := lineStyles{base: boldIf(t.Zero, cursor && row.Kind == diff.Equal), ws: t.Whitespace,
                showAll: a.cfg.Settings.ShowWhitespaces, plainSpace: !a.cfg.Settings.MarkWhitespace,
                trailing: t.TrailingWhitespace, showTrailing: a.cfg.Settings.ShowTrailing, lineEnd: a.cfg.Settings.LineEnd,
                cr: a.cfg.Settings.CREnd, showCR: a.cfg.Settings.ShowCR, hidden: t.HiddenChar}
        var spans []diff.Span
        switch {
        case row.Kind == diff.Equal:
        case moved:
                st.base = a.movedStyle(s)
        case row.Kind == diff.Change:
                st.base, st.emph, st.space, spans = nonEmph, emph, indent, a.emphOf(a.rowOf[s][line], s)
                if !a.cfg.Settings.BoldChanges {
                        st.emph, st.space = st.emph.UnsetBold(), st.space.UnsetBold()
                }
        default:
                st.base = lineStyle
        }
        if m := matcherOf(a.cfg.Search.Query); m != nil && (a.cfg.Settings.SearchAll || row.Kind != diff.Equal) {
                st.matches, st.match = m.spans(a.lines[s][line]), t.Search
        }
        // The pieces of a wrapped line are drawn from one highlight of it.
        key := highlightKey{line, a.aligns, cursor, moved, a.cfg.Search.Query, a.cfg.Settings, t}
        if c := &a.highlighted[s]; !c.ok || c.key != key {
                *c = highlightCache{key, highlight(a.lines[s][line], spans, st), true}
        }
        content := a.highlighted[s].out
        plain := lipgloss.NewStyle()
        if !cut { // a piece of a wrapped line
                return pad(ansi.Cut(content, from, from+width), width, plain)
        }
        // fit returns s as w columns.
        fit := func(s string, w int) string { return pad(ansi.Truncate(s, w, ""), w, plain) }
        full := ansi.StringWidth(content)
        content = fit(cutLeft(content, from), width)
        // The edges say where the line goes on.
        if width >= 2 {
                mark := a.cfg.Theme.Overflow
                if full > from+width {
                        content = fit(content, width-1) + mark.Render(a.cfg.Settings.Extends)
                }
                if from > 0 && full > 0 {
                        content = mark.Render(a.cfg.Settings.Precedes) + fit(cutLeft(content, 1), width-1)
                }
        }
        return content
}

// cutLeft returns s without its first n columns. A wide character they cut
// through leaves a space, so that what follows keeps its column.
func cutLeft(s string, n int) string {
        if n <= 0 {
                return s
        }
        r := ansi.TruncateLeft(s, n, "")
        if ansi.StringWidth(r) > ansi.StringWidth(s)-n { // kept the cut character
                r = " " + ansi.TruncateLeft(s, n+1, "")
        }
        return r
}

// highlight renders text with the given spans emphasized. Changed
// whitespace (inside spans) is drawn visibly, · for a space and → for a tab,
// when it is indentation, holds a tab or is more than one space: a single
// space between changed words is no news. With showTrailing whitespace at
// the end of the line is drawn visibly in its style, with showAll all
// whitespace, and the end of the line as lineEnd. The \r of a \r\n line
// end is drawn as cr where it changed or with showCR, else not at all. Tabs
// are expanded and hidden characters drawn as ☠ in their style.
func highlight(text string, spans []diff.Span, st lineStyles) string {
        cr, changedCR := strings.HasSuffix(text, "\r"), false
        if cr {
                text = text[:len(text)-1]
        }
        text, spans, st.matches = dropJoiners(text, spans, st.matches)
        var b strings.Builder
        col := 0
        pBase, pEmph, pSpace, pWS := newPainter(st.base), newPainter(st.emph), newPainter(st.space), newPainter(st.ws)
        pTrailing, pHidden, pMatch := newPainter(st.trailing), newPainter(st.hidden), newPainter(st.match)
        render := func(part string, p painter, visible bool) {
                if part == "" {
                        return
                }
                var e strings.Builder
                expand(&e, part, &col, visible)
                b.WriteString(p.paint(e.String()))
        }
        lead := true // in the line's indentation
        at := 0      // where the part rendered next starts in text
        trailFrom := len(strings.TrimRightFunc(text, unicode.IsSpace))
        // class is the kind of run r belongs to: hidden, whitespace or text.
        class := func(r rune) int {
                switch {
                case hiddenChar(r):
                        return 2
                case unicode.IsSpace(r):
                        return 1
                }
                return 0
        }
        // renderPart splits a part into hidden, whitespace and other runs.
        renderPart := func(part string, emph, match bool) {
                for part != "" {
                        r, _ := utf8.DecodeRuneInString(part)
                        c := class(r)
                        ws := c == 1
                        i := strings.IndexFunc(part, func(r rune) bool { return class(r) != c })
                        if i < 0 {
                                i = len(part)
                        }
                        run := part[:i]
                        if c == 2 {
                                lead = false
                                at += i
                                render(run, pHidden, false)
                                part = part[i:]
                                continue
                        }
                        mark := lead || strings.ContainsRune(run, '\t') || utf8.RuneCountInString(run) > 1
                        lead = lead && ws
                        trailing := ws && at >= trailFrom
                        at += i
                        switch {
                        case match: // found by /: over all styles, whitespace visible as around
                                render(run, pMatch, ws && (st.showAll || trailing && st.showTrailing))
                        case trailing && st.showTrailing:
                                render(run, pTrailing, true)
                        case ws && emph && !st.plainSpace && mark:
                                render(part[:i], pSpace, true)
                        case ws && st.showAll:
                                render(part[:i], pWS, true)
                        case ws:
                                render(part[:i], pBase, false)
                        case emph:
                                render(part[:i], pEmph, false)
                        default:
                                render(part[:i], pBase, false)
                        }
                        part = part[i:]
                }
        }
        // The line is drawn in parts between the edges of the spans and the
        // matches, each emphasized or found by / or not.
        cuts := []int{0, len(text)}
        for _, sp := range spans {
                changedCR = changedCR || sp.End > len(text)
                cuts = append(cuts, min(sp.Start, len(text)), min(sp.End, len(text)))
        }
        for _, sp := range st.matches {
                cuts = append(cuts, min(sp.Start, len(text)), min(sp.End, len(text)))
        }
        slices.Sort(cuts)
        cuts = slices.Compact(cuts)
        in := func(spans []diff.Span, at int) bool {
                return slices.ContainsFunc(spans, func(sp diff.Span) bool { return at >= sp.Start && at < sp.End })
        }
        for i := 0; i+1 < len(cuts); i++ {
                from, to := cuts[i], cuts[i+1]
                renderPart(text[from:to], in(spans, from), in(st.matches, from))
        }
        if cr && (changedCR || st.showCR) {
                style := st.ws
                if changedCR {
                        style = st.emph
                }
                b.WriteString(style.Render(st.cr))
        }
        if st.showAll {
                b.WriteString(st.ws.Render(st.lineEnd))
        }
        return b.String()
}

// expand writes text, which starts at column *col, to b as drawn: tabs
// expanded to the next tab stop and hidden characters as ☠; with visible
// set, spaces are drawn as · and tabs start with →. *col advances.
func expand(b *strings.Builder, text string, col *int, visible bool) {
        for _, r := range text {
                switch {
                case r == '\t':
                        n := tabWidth - *col%tabWidth
                        if visible {
                                b.WriteString("→" + strings.Repeat(" ", n-1))
                        } else {
                                b.WriteString(strings.Repeat(" ", n))
                        }
                        *col += n
                case r == ' ' && visible:
                        b.WriteRune('·')
                        *col++
                case unicode.IsSpace(r) && !hiddenChar(r) && visible:
                        // A space of another width, as the ideographic space: · in
                        // its room.
                        w := max(ansi.StringWidth(string(r)), 1)
                        b.WriteString("·" + strings.Repeat(" ", w-1))
                        *col += w
                case hiddenChar(r):
                        b.WriteString(hiddenSymbol)
                        *col++
                default:
                        b.WriteRune(r)
                        *col += ansi.StringWidth(string(r))
                }
        }
}

// printable returns line as drawn, see expand, so that its width is known:
// text from files, git or marks may hold tabs and control characters. The
// joiners of emoji are left out, see emojiJoiners.
func printable(line string) string {
        var b strings.Builder
        col := 0
        line, _, _ = dropJoiners(line, nil, nil)
        expand(&b, line, &col, false)
        return b.String()
}

package ui

import (
        "errors"
        "fmt"
        "path/filepath"
        "slices"
        "strconv"
        "time"

        tea "charm.land/bubbletea/v2"

        "jdiff/internal/config"
        "jdiff/internal/source"
        "jdiff/watch"
)

// racyWindow is how long after its modification time a file is compared by
// content: file systems keep the time in coarse steps, so a change of the
// same size right after the last read may leave the stamp as it was.
const racyWindow = 2 * time.Second

// watchTickMsg asks the top-level view to check its watched files. owner is
// the view that asked for it; another view, which took its place, drops it.
// Without owner, any view takes it.
type watchTickMsg struct{ owner tea.Model }

// changedMsg reports files on disk that changed, by their paths cleaned,
// from inotify watcher w.
type changedMsg struct {
        w     *watch.Watcher
        paths []string
}

// watchEndedMsg reports that inotify watcher w stopped reporting changes: it
// was closed, or a directory could not be watched any more. The view whose
// watcher it still is checks its files itself from now on.
type watchEndedMsg struct{ w *watch.Watcher }

// waitChanges returns the command waiting for the next changes w reports.
func waitChanges(w *watch.Watcher) tea.Cmd {
        return func() tea.Msg {
                paths, ok := <-w.Changes()
                if !ok {
                        return watchEndedMsg{w}
                }
                return changedMsg{w, paths}
        }
}

// diskWatch watches the files of a view on disk: by the watcher App
// shares, which it gives the view before Init (shareWatcher) and waits on, or
// by one of the view's own, which the view waits on and closes. The files it
// does not watch, all without a watcher, the view checks every
// watch_interval (poll), ticking while such a check is pending.
type diskWatch struct {
        w       *watch.Watcher
        own     bool
        ticking bool
}

// share makes dw watch by w, which App shares; a pending check stays.
func (dw *diskWatch) share(w *watch.Watcher) { dw.w, dw.own = w, false }

// poll returns the next check, for owner, of the files at paths, the view's
// to check, unless one is pending, auto reload is off, there is no
// watch_interval or all of them are watched.
func (dw *diskWatch) poll(cfg *config.Config, owner tea.Model, paths []string) tea.Cmd {
        if dw.ticking || !cfg.AutoReload || cfg.Settings.WatchInterval <= 0 || !dw.unwatched(paths) {
                return nil
        }
        dw.ticking = true
        return watchTick(cfg, owner)
}

// ticked reports whether owner checks its files for msg: it is owner's check
// or anyone's, which is then no longer pending, and auto reload is on.
func (dw *diskWatch) ticked(cfg *config.Config, owner tea.Model, msg watchTickMsg) bool {
        if msg.owner != nil && msg.owner != owner {
                return false
        }
        dw.ticking = false
        return cfg.AutoReload
}

// unwatched reports whether a change of a file at paths is not reported.
func (dw *diskWatch) unwatched(paths []string) bool {
        return slices.ContainsFunc(paths, func(p string) bool { return !dw.watched(p) })
}

// set watches the files at paths in place of those before, starting the
// view's own watcher when it has none, there are files and inotify_max_dirs
// allows one. It returns the command waiting for the changes of a watcher
// just started, and why the system refused, or "".
func (dw *diskWatch) set(cfg *config.Config, paths []string) (cmd tea.Cmd, msg string) {
        if cfg.Settings.WatchInterval <= 0 {
                return nil, ""
        }
        if dw.w == nil {
                if cfg.Settings.InotifyMaxDirs <= 0 || len(paths) == 0 {
                        return nil, ""
                }
                w, err := watch.New(cfg.Settings.InotifyMaxDirs)
                if err != nil {
                        return nil, refused(cfg, err)
                }
                dw.w, dw.own, cmd = w, true, waitChanges(w)
        }
        if err := dw.w.SetFiles(paths); err != nil {
                msg = refused(cfg, err)
        }
        return cmd, msg
}

// refused returns what to say when the system refused to watch, nothing
// where it cannot.
func refused(cfg *config.Config, err error) string {
        if errors.Is(err, watch.ErrUnsupported) {
                return ""
        }
        return err.Error() + "; checking files every " + strconv.FormatFloat(cfg.Settings.WatchInterval, 'g', -1, 64) + "s"
}

// next returns the command waiting for the next changes of the view's own
// watcher, nil for the shared one, which App waits on.
func (dw *diskWatch) next() tea.Cmd {
        if !dw.own || dw.w == nil {
                return nil
        }
        return waitChanges(dw.w)
}

// watched reports whether a change of the file at path is reported.
func (dw *diskWatch) watched(path string) bool { return dw.w != nil && dw.w.Watched(path) }

// touch counts the directories of the files at paths as used.
func (dw *diskWatch) touch(paths []string) {
        if dw.w != nil {
                dw.w.Touch(paths)
        }
}

// limit applies inotify_max_dirs, set in the settings popup, also to the
// shared watcher, before the view's next poll; App applies it too, see "Kept
// on purpose" in doc/development.md.
func (dw *diskWatch) limit(cfg *config.Config) {
        if dw.w != nil {
                dw.w.SetLimit(cfg.Settings.InotifyMaxDirs)
        }
}

// ended reports whether w, which stopped reporting, was the watcher, which
// is then dropped.
func (dw *diskWatch) ended(w *watch.Watcher) bool {
        if w == nil || w != dw.w {
                return false
        }
        dw.w = nil
        return true
}

// stop stops watching the files: the view's own watcher is closed, the
// shared one, which stays, watches them no more.
func (dw *diskWatch) stop() {
        switch {
        case dw.w == nil:
        case dw.own:
                dw.w.Close()
                dw.w = nil
        default:
                dw.w.SetFiles(nil)
        }
}

// diskPaths returns the paths of the sides of f on disk, cleaned as
// changedMsg reports them.
func diskPaths(f source.File) []string {
        var out []string
        for _, p := range []string{f.OldFile, f.NewFile} {
                if p != "" {
                        out = append(out, filepath.Clean(p))
                }
        }
        return out
}

// watchTick returns the command for the next check of the watched files,
// nil when watching is off.
func watchTick(cfg *config.Config, owner tea.Model) tea.Cmd {
        interval := cfg.Settings.WatchInterval
        if interval <= 0 {
                return nil
        }
        return tea.Tick(time.Duration(interval*float64(time.Second)), func(time.Time) tea.Msg { return watchTickMsg{owner} })
}

// stale reports whether the file at path may differ from what was read with
// stamp: its stamp changed or, while its modification time is recent, its
// content may have. A file that cannot be read is not.
func stale(path string, stamp source.Stamp) bool {
        st, err := source.StampOf(path)
        return err == nil && (st != stamp || time.Since(st.ModTime) < racyWindow)
}

// refreshFile reloads the sides of f that changed on disk into f and into
// its diff a, unless nil, calling after, unless nil, after each reload of a
// so that views follow their cursor line before the next one. It returns the
// message to show, "" when nothing changed. A side changed when its stamp
// did, or, while its modification time is recent, its content. A side that
// cannot be read is checked again next time; a side that became binary keeps
// its content.
func refreshFile(f *source.File, a *fileAlign, after func()) string {
        type sideFile struct {
                path  string
                stamp *source.Stamp
                lines *[]string
        }
        sides := [2]sideFile{{f.OldFile, &f.OldStamp, &f.Old}, {f.NewFile, &f.NewStamp, &f.New}}
        reloaded, binary, dropped := false, false, 0
        for s, sf := range sides {
                if sf.path == "" {
                        continue
                }
                if !stale(sf.path, *sf.stamp) {
                        continue
                }
                lines, st, err := source.Read(sf.path)
                if errors.Is(err, source.ErrBinary) {
                        *sf.stamp, binary = st, true
                        continue
                }
                if err != nil {
                        continue
                }
                if slices.Equal(lines, *sf.lines) {
                        *sf.stamp = st
                        continue
                }
                *sf.stamp, *sf.lines, reloaded = st, lines, true
                if a != nil {
                        dropped += a.reload(side(s), lines)
                        if after != nil {
                                after()
                        }
                }
        }
        switch {
        case binary:
                return f.Path + " is binary now, not reloaded"
        case !reloaded:
                return ""
        case dropped == 1:
                return f.Path + " reloaded, 1 anchor dropped"
        case dropped > 1:
                return fmt.Sprintf("%s reloaded, %d anchors dropped", f.Path, dropped)
        }
        return f.Path + " reloaded"
}

package ui

import (
        "fmt"
        "strings"

        tea "charm.land/bubbletea/v2"
        "charm.land/lipgloss/v2"
        "github.com/charmbracelet/x/ansi"

        "jdiff/internal/config"
        "jdiff/internal/keys"
        "jdiff/internal/theme"
)

// paneTitles renders names above the panes of a view width columns wide.
// Side by side the name of the active pane is bold, the other one faint; in
// the normal layout, one column of both, both are bold.
func paneTitles(t *theme.Theme, names [2]string, active side, sideBySide bool, width int) string {
        w := (width - 1) / 2
        title := func(s side) string {
                style := t.Heading
                if sideBySide && s != active {
                        style = t.InactiveHeading
                }
                return pad(style.Render(ansi.Truncate(" "+printable(names[s]), w, "…")), w, lipgloss.NewStyle())
        }
        return title(left) + t.Decoration.Render("│") + title(right)
}

// statusLine renders the status bar with left, already styled, and right,
// in the hint style, aligned to its edges; left is shortened when both do
// not fit.
func statusLine(t *theme.Theme, width int, left, right string) string {
        left = ansi.Truncate(left, max(width-ansi.StringWidth(right), 0), "…")
        gap := max(width-ansi.StringWidth(left)-ansi.StringWidth(right), 0)
        return ansi.Truncate(left+t.Hint.Render(strings.Repeat(" ", gap)+right), width, "")
}

// popupTitle returns the title of a popup: its name, then msg or the keys
// of hints, as statusText renders them.
func popupTitle(t *theme.Theme, name, msg, hints string) string {
        return t.CommitHash.Render(" "+name+" ") + t.Hint.Render(" ") + statusText(t, msg, hints) + t.Hint.Render(" ")
}

// statusText returns the left part of a status bar: msg, or without one
// the hints, each a key and what it does, separated by " · ", in the hint
// style. msg may name files or quote git, so it is drawn printable.
func statusText(t *theme.Theme, msg, hints string) string {
        if msg != "" {
                return t.Hint.Render(printable(msg))
        }
        return t.Hint.Render(hints)
}

// pageStatus returns the left part of the status bar of a page, see
// statusText; while auto reload is on, its name comes first.
func pageStatus(cfg *config.Config, msg, hints string) string {
        if cfg.AutoReload {
                return popupTitle(cfg.Theme, "auto reload", msg, hints)
        }
        return statusText(cfg.Theme, msg, hints)
}

// barKeys says what differs between the status bars of the pages.
type barKeys struct {
        enter   string // the action of enter
        back    string // the action of backspace, "" when it does nothing
        commits bool   // N and P switch commits
        flow    bool   // f switches between the flow and the single file view
        text    bool   // a page of text, --readme: no layout, git diff or settings
}

// barState returns the index of an action's label while it is on or off.
func barState(on bool) int {
        if on {
                return 0
        }
        return 1
}

// statusKeys returns the keys of the status bar of a page, the same on all of
// them, in one order, so that a key that switches what is shown changes only
// its own label: f and s name what they switch to. All other keys are in the
// help. keybindings.toml gives the keys and the words.
func statusKeys(cfg *config.Config, k barKeys) string {
        var shown []string
        add := func(row keys.Row, keyNames string, state int) {
                if l := row.LabelIn(state); l != "" {
                        shown = append(shown, keyNames+" "+l)
                }
        }
        first := func(action string, state int) {
                if r := cfg.Keys.Row(action); len(r.Keys) > 0 {
                        add(r, r.Keys[0], state)
                }
        }
        first(k.enter, 0)
        if k.back != "" {
                first(k.back, 0)
        }
        if k.commits {
                r := cfg.Keys.Row("history")
                add(r, strings.Join(r.Keys, "/"), 0)
        }
        if k.flow {
                first("flow", barState(cfg.Settings.Flow))
        }
        actions := []string{"git_diff", "settings", "help", "quit"}
        if k.text {
                actions = actions[2:]
        } else {
                first("layout", barState(cfg.Settings.SideBySide))
        }
        for _, action := range actions {
                first(action, 0)
        }
        return strings.Join(shown, " · ")
}

// minBoxHeight fits the borders and one line.
const minBoxHeight = 3

// splitList divides a screen height high between a heading, an empty line,
// a list of n items, the box below it when shown and the status bar. The
// list takes at most half of the room and the box, after an empty line, the
// rest; box is 0 when hidden, for an empty list or when the screen is too
// small for it.
func splitList(height, n int, showBox bool) (list, box int) {
        room := max(height-3, 1)
        if !showBox || n == 0 {
                return room, 0
        }
        list = max(min(n, room/2), 1)
        box = room - list - 1
        if box < minBoxHeight {
                return room, 0
        }
        return list, box
}

// box renders lines in a rounded box width columns wide and height lines
// high, with title in the top border. Lines that do not fit are cut.
func box(t *theme.Theme, width, height int, title string, lines []string) string {
        border := t.Decoration
        inner := max(width-4, 0) // border and a space on each side
        title = ansi.Truncate(title, inner, "")
        var b strings.Builder
        b.WriteString(border.Render("╭─") + title +
                border.Render(strings.Repeat("─", max(width-3-ansi.StringWidth(title), 0))+"╮") + "\n")
        for i := range height - 2 {
                line := ""
                if i < len(lines) {
                        line = ansi.Truncate(lines[i], inner, "…")
                }
                b.WriteString(border.Render("│") + " " + pad(line, inner, lipgloss.NewStyle()) + " " + border.Render("│") + "\n")
        }
        b.WriteString(border.Render("╰"+strings.Repeat("─", max(width-2, 0))+"╯") + "\n")
        return b.String()
}

// popupHeight is the height of a popup of n lines, at least one, on a screen
// height lines high: all lines when they fit, else the screen but a line above
// and below.
func popupHeight(n, height int) int { return min(max(n, 1)+2, max(height-2, minBoxHeight)) }

// emptyListWidth is the least width of the line of a listPopup without items.
const emptyListWidth = 40

// listPopup is the state of a popup that lists items with a cursor, so that
// such popups move and look the same.
type listPopup struct {
        cursor, top   int // the selected item, the first one shown
        width, height int
}

// moveIn moves the cursor by delta among n items.
func (p *listPopup) moveIn(delta, n int) {
        p.cursor = max(min(p.cursor+delta, n-1), 0)
        p.top = windowAround(p.cursor, p.top, popupHeight(n, p.height)-2, n).first
}

// draw draws the popup over base: the items all in a box, the selected one in
// the cursor style, or empty as a hint when there are none. It is as wide as
// the items and title need, up to the width of the screen.
func (p *listPopup) draw(t *theme.Theme, base, title string, all []string, empty string) string {
        n := len(all)
        height := popupHeight(n, p.height)
        lines := windowAround(p.cursor, p.top, height-2, n).rows(t, n, func(i int) string {
                if i == p.cursor {
                        return t.Cursor.Render(all[i])
                }
                return all[i]
        })
        widest := 0
        for _, item := range all {
                widest = max(widest, ansi.StringWidth(item))
        }
        if n == 0 {
                lines, widest = []string{t.Hint.Render(empty)}, emptyListWidth
        }
        width := min(max(widest+4, ansi.StringWidth(title)+4), p.width)
        return overlay(base, box(t, width, height, title, lines), width, p.width)
}

// scrollWindow returns the first shown item of a window h items high over n
// items: top, moved as little as needed to show cursor.
func scrollWindow(cursor, top, h, n int) int {
        if cursor < top {
                top = cursor
        } else if cursor >= top+h {
                top = cursor - h + 1
        }
        return max(min(top, n-h), 0)
}

// jumpTop returns the first shown item after the cursor jumped from item
// from to item to, with the window h items high over n items at top: it
// stays when to is in it, moves as little as needed when from and to fit in
// it together, so that both stay in view, and else keeps the cursor's
// screen row, so that the eye stays where it was.
func jumpTop(from, to, top, h, n int) int {
        if d := to - from; d < h && -d < h {
                return scrollWindow(to, top, h, n)
        }
        return scrollWindow(to, top+to-from, h, n)
}

// list is the state of the commit and the file list: n items below a
// heading and an empty line, the box below them while preview is set, and the
// status bar.
type list struct {
        cursor, top   int
        n             int
        preview       *bool // the setting that shows the box
        maxRows       int   // the most rows of the list while the box shows, 0 for no limit
        width, height int
}

// rows returns how many rows the list would take with the box: all items, up
// to maxRows.
func (li *list) rows() int {
        if li.maxRows > 0 {
                return min(li.n, li.maxRows)
        }
        return li.n
}

// listHeight is the height of the list below the heading and an empty line.
func (li *list) listHeight() int {
        h, _ := splitList(li.height, li.rows(), *li.preview)
        return h
}

// boxHeight is the height of the box below the list, 0 when it is hidden.
func (li *list) boxHeight() int {
        _, h := splitList(li.height, li.rows(), *li.preview)
        return h
}

func (li *list) moveCursor(delta int) {
        li.cursor = max(min(li.cursor+delta, li.n-1), 0)
        li.scrollToCursor()
}

func (li *list) scrollToCursor() {
        li.top = windowAround(li.cursor, li.top, li.listHeight(), li.n).first
}

// window is the part of n lines that a view h rows high shows: the lines
// from first to end, after a row with an arrow for the lines hidden above
// when up, and before one for those hidden below when down. Arrows take
// rows when the lines do not fit in three rows or more; an arrow hides at
// least two lines, a single one is shown instead.
type window struct {
        first, end int
        up, down   bool
}

// windowAt returns the window of h rows over n lines from top, moved into
// the lines. It never starts at 1, which would show the lines it shows from
// 0, with an arrow in place of the first.
func windowAt(top, h, n int) window {
        if n <= h || h < 3 {
                top = max(min(top, n-h), 0)
                return window{top, min(top+h, n), false, false}
        }
        // Up to the end of the lines, the window holds an arrow and h-1 lines.
        top = max(min(top, n-h+1), 0)
        if top == 1 {
                top = 0
        }
        end := shownEnd(top, h, n)
        return window{top, end, top > 0, end < n}
}

// windowAround returns the window of h rows over n lines from top, moved as
// little as needed to show the line cursor.
func windowAround(cursor, top, h, n int) window {
        if n <= h || h < 3 {
                return windowAt(scrollWindow(cursor, top, h, n), h, n)
        }
        w := windowAt(min(top, cursor), h, n)
        for cursor >= w.end {
                next := w.first + 1
                if next == 1 {
                        next = 2
                }
                w = windowAt(next, h, n)
        }
        return w
}

// shownEnd returns the end of the lines shown from top in a window h rows
// high over n lines, after the rows of the arrows.
func shownEnd(top, h, n int) int {
        end := top + h
        if top > 0 {
                end-- // the arrow above
        }
        if end < n {
                end-- // the arrow below
        }
        return min(end, n)
}

// rows returns the rows of w over n lines: line(i) for the lines shown and
// the arrows with the number of lines they hide, drawn by moreRow.
func (w window) rows(t *theme.Theme, n int, line func(i int) string) []string {
        var out []string
        if w.up {
                out = append(out, moreRow(t, "↑", w.first))
        }
        for i := w.first; i < w.end; i++ {
                out = append(out, line(i))
        }
        if w.down {
                out = append(out, moreRow(t, "↓", n-w.end))
        }
        return out
}

// moreRow renders the row of an arrow for hidden lines of a list or popup.
func moreRow(t *theme.Theme, arrow string, hidden int) string {
        return t.More.Render(fmt.Sprintf("  %s %d more", arrow, hidden))
}

// render renders the list: head, the shown items as drawn by item, which
// renders the selected one bold through st, the box as drawn by box when it
// is shown, and status, styled, see statusText.
func (li *list) render(t *theme.Theme, head string, item func(i int, st func(lipgloss.Style) lipgloss.Style) string,
        box func(height int) string, status string) string {
        var b strings.Builder
        b.WriteString(ansi.Truncate(head, li.width, "…") + "\n\n")
        h := li.listHeight()
        w := windowAround(li.cursor, li.top, h, li.n)
        rows := w.rows(t, li.n, func(i int) string {
                return item(i, func(s lipgloss.Style) lipgloss.Style { return s.Bold(i == li.cursor) })
        })
        for i := range h {
                if i < len(rows) {
                        b.WriteString(ansi.Truncate(rows[i], li.width, "…"))
                }
                b.WriteString("\n")
        }
        if h := li.boxHeight(); h > 0 && li.cursor < li.n {
                b.WriteString("\n" + box(h))
        }
        b.WriteString(ansi.Truncate(status, li.width, ""))
        return b.String()
}

// minInputWidth is the least width of the line in the popups of inputBox.
const minInputWidth = 40

// inputBox renders a popup holding a line typed: title, text with the
// cursor at its end, centered between empty lines with centered, and err
// below when set. It is at least minInputWidth wide and grows with them up
// to width, beyond which the start of text is cut, so that its end stays
// in view. It returns the popup and its width.
func inputBox(t *theme.Theme, width int, title, text, err string, centered bool) (string, int) {
        text += "█"
        inner := max(minInputWidth, ansi.StringWidth(title), ansi.StringWidth(text), ansi.StringWidth(err))
        inner = min(inner, width-4) // borders and a space on each side
        if over := ansi.StringWidth(text) - inner; over > 0 {
                text = cutLeft(text, over)
        }
        lines := []string{text}
        if centered {
                lines = []string{"", strings.Repeat(" ", (inner-ansi.StringWidth(text))/2) + text, ""}
        }
        if err != "" {
                lines = append(lines, t.Minus.Render(err))
        }
        return box(t, inner+4, len(lines)+2, title, lines), inner + 4
}

// lineInput is a line of text being typed, with the cursor at its end.
type lineInput struct{ text string }

// cancels reports whether k closes the input without using it: esc, or
// backspace on nothing typed.
func (in *lineInput) cancels(k tea.KeyPressMsg) bool {
        return k.String() == "esc" || k.String() == "backspace" && in.text == ""
}

// paste adds pasted text, which comes as a tea.PasteMsg: on one line, line
// breaks and tabs as spaces, without characters that are not visible, which
// could reach the terminal.
func (in *lineInput) paste(text string) {
        in.text += strings.Map(func(r rune) rune {
                switch {
                case r == '\n' || r == '\r' || r == '\t':
                        return ' '
                case hiddenChar(r):
                        return -1
                }
                return r
        }, text)
}

// edit applies k to the text: typed text is added, backspace deletes a
// character, ctrl+w a word and ctrl+u the line. Pasted text comes as a
// tea.PasteMsg, which the owner adds with paste.
func (in *lineInput) edit(k tea.KeyPressMsg) {
        switch k.String() {
        case "backspace":
                if r := []rune(in.text); len(r) > 0 {
                        in.text = string(r[:len(r)-1])
                }
        case "ctrl+w":
                in.text = strings.TrimRight(in.text, " ")
                in.text = in.text[:strings.LastIndex(in.text, " ")+1]
        case "ctrl+u":
                in.text = ""
        default:
                in.text += k.Text
        }
}

// pad fills s with spaces in style up to width columns.
func pad(s string, width int, style lipgloss.Style) string {
        if n := width - ansi.StringWidth(s); n > 0 {
                return s + style.Render(strings.Repeat(" ", n))
        }
        return s
}

// count formats n with sign right aligned to width, or blanks when n is 0.
func count(sign string, n, width int) string {
        if n == 0 {
                return strings.Repeat(" ", width)
        }
        return fmt.Sprintf("%*s", width, sign+fmt.Sprint(n))
}

// counts renders the added and removed line counts in columns addW and remW
// digits wide, in st of the plus and minus styles.
func counts(t *theme.Theme, st func(lipgloss.Style) lipgloss.Style, added, removed, addW, remW int) string {
        return st(t.Plus).Render(count("+", added, addW+1)) + " " + st(t.Minus).Render(count("-", removed, remW+1))
}

// minWidth and minHeight are the smallest terminal the views are drawn in.
// Side by side the gutters of both panes take 7 columns and twice the digits
// of the line numbers, 19 for files of up to 999999 lines; the lists need a
// heading, an empty line, an item and the status bar.
const minWidth, minHeight = 20, 4

// SizeGuard wraps the top-level model and puts it on the alternate screen.
// In a terminal smaller than minWidth by minHeight it shows that in a
// centered box instead of the page and ignores keys but ctrl+c, until the
// terminal is large enough again; the page still learns the size and gets
// all other messages.
type SizeGuard struct {
        tea.Model
        cfg           *config.Config
        width, height int
}

// WithSizeGuard returns m guarded against too small terminals.
func WithSizeGuard(cfg *config.Config, m tea.Model) *SizeGuard { return &SizeGuard{Model: m, cfg: cfg} }

// Stop ends the git commands that run in the background, and the watching
// of files, once the program ended: its git commands would else run on.
func (g *SizeGuard) Stop() {
        if m, ok := g.Model.(interface{ stop() }); ok {
                m.stop()
        }
}

func (g *SizeGuard) tooSmall() bool { return g.width < minWidth || g.height < minHeight }

func (g *SizeGuard) Update(msg tea.Msg) (tea.Model, tea.Cmd) {
        switch msg := msg.(type) {
        case tea.WindowSizeMsg:
                g.width, g.height = msg.Width, msg.Height
        case tea.KeyPressMsg:
                if g.tooSmall() {
                        if msg.String() == "ctrl+c" {
                                return g, tea.Quit
                        }
                        return g, nil
                }
        }
        _, cmd := g.Model.Update(msg)
        return g, cmd
}

func (g *SizeGuard) View() tea.View {
        v := tea.NewView(g.frame())
        v.AltScreen = true
        return v
}

func (g *SizeGuard) frame() string {
        if g.width == 0 || !g.tooSmall() {
                return g.Model.View().Content
        }
        first := "terminal too small"
        if ansi.StringWidth(first) > g.width {
                first = "too small"
        }
        lines := []string{first, fmt.Sprintf("%dx%d, need %dx%d", g.width, g.height, minWidth, minHeight)}
        w := 0
        for _, l := range lines {
                w = max(w, ansi.StringWidth(l)+4) // border and a space on each side
        }
        notice := strings.Join(lines, "\n")
        if w <= g.width && len(lines)+2 <= g.height {
                notice = strings.TrimSuffix(box(g.cfg.Theme, w, len(lines)+2, "", lines), "\n")
        }
        // Without room for all of it, it is cut.
        rows := strings.Split(lipgloss.Place(g.width, g.height, lipgloss.Center, lipgloss.Center, notice), "\n")
        rows = rows[:min(len(rows), g.height)]
        for i, r := range rows {
                rows[i] = ansi.Truncate(r, g.width, "")
        }
        return strings.Join(rows, "\n")
}

// Command jdiff is an interactive side-by-side diff viewer with manual line
// alignment.
package main

import (
        "bytes"
        "context"
        "embed"
        "fmt"
        "io"
        "os"
        "path/filepath"
        "runtime/debug"
        "strings"
        "time"

        tea "charm.land/bubbletea/v2"
        "github.com/charmbracelet/colorprofile"
        "github.com/charmbracelet/x/term"
        "github.com/spf13/cobra"

        "jdiff/internal/config"
        "jdiff/internal/gitsrc"
        "jdiff/internal/source"
        "jdiff/internal/theme"
        "jdiff/internal/ui"
)

//go:embed LICENSE
var license string

// thirdParty holds the licenses of the modules linked into jdiff, which
// they require a binary to carry; make licenses writes it.
//
//go:embed LICENSE-THIRD-PARTY
var thirdParty string

// docs holds the documentation that --readme shows: the packages install only
// the binary, so it is the only copy at hand. doc/config.md links to the
// built-in keybindings.toml.
//
//go:embed README.md doc/*.md internal/keys/keybindings.toml
var docs embed.FS

// docPages are the pages of docs in the order of n and p; --readme starts
// with the first.
var docPages = []string{"README.md", "doc/usage.md", "doc/features.md", "doc/install.md", "doc/config.md",
        "doc/keybindings.md", "doc/development.md"}

func main() {
        cmd, err := newRoot(start).ExecuteC()
        if err == nil {
                return
        }
        // The errors of running a view end in fatal; those left are of the
        // command line, of the command cmd.
        fmt.Fprintf(os.Stderr, "jdiff: %v\n\n%s", err, cmd.UsageString())
        os.Exit(2)
}

// options are the flags of the command line.
type options struct {
        version, license, readme, untracked bool
        features                            string
}

// newRoot returns the command line: jdiff OLD NEW, jdiff log and jdiff diff,
// whose arguments go to git as they are. jdiff's flags come before log and
// diff. It calls start with what to show, see start.
func newRoot(start func(o options, mode string, args []string)) *cobra.Command {
        var o options
        cobra.EnableCommandSorting = false // log, diff, completion
        root := &cobra.Command{
                Use:   "jdiff [flags] OLD NEW",
                Short: "An interactive diff viewer for the terminal, with manual line alignment, that follows your edits",
                Example: "  jdiff old.txt new.txt     compare two files, or two directories\n" +
                        "  jdiff log -20             browse the last 20 commits\n" +
                        "  jdiff -u diff HEAD        what git diff HEAD shows, and the untracked files\n" +
                        "  jdiff -- log log1         compare files named like a command",
                Args: func(cmd *cobra.Command, args []string) error {
                        for _, f := range []struct {
                                on   bool
                                name string
                        }{{o.version, "version"}, {o.license, "license"}, {o.readme, "readme"}} {
                                if f.on && len(args) > 0 {
                                        return fmt.Errorf("--%s takes no arguments, not %q", f.name, args[0])
                                }
                                if f.on {
                                        return nil
                                }
                        }
                        return cobra.ExactArgs(2)(cmd, args)
                },
                RunE: func(cmd *cobra.Command, args []string) error {
                        mode := ""
                        switch {
                        case o.version:
                                fmt.Println(resolveVersion())
                                return nil
                        case o.license:
                                printLicense()
                                return nil
                        case o.readme:
                                mode = "readme"
                        }
                        start(o, mode, args)
                        return nil
                },
                SilenceErrors:    true,
                SilenceUsage:     true,
                TraverseChildren: true, // the flags before log and diff
        }
        f := root.Flags()
        f.BoolVar(&o.version, "version", false, "print the version")
        f.BoolVar(&o.license, "license", false, "print the license and those of the libraries")
        f.BoolVar(&o.readme, "readme", false, "show the README and the documentation it links to; print the README as markdown\n"+
                "when the output is no terminal")
        f.BoolVarP(&o.untracked, "untracked", "u", false, "also show the untracked files in a git diff; u in the view switches them.\n"+
                "Neither is saved")
        f.StringVar(&o.features, "features", "", "delta features to use instead of delta.features: `NAMES` of [delta \"name\"]\n"+
                "sections of your git config, separated by spaces. They only shape the \"delta\" theme,\n"+
                "which is used unless main.toml names another. DELTA_FEATURES, when set, wins; with a\n"+
                "leading + it adds to them.")
        for _, c := range []struct{ name, use, short string }{
                {"log", "log [GIT LOG ARGS]", "browse the commits that git log lists"},
                {"diff", "diff [GIT DIFF ARGS]", "show the files that git diff shows"},
        } {
                root.AddCommand(&cobra.Command{
                        Use:                c.use,
                        Short:              c.short,
                        DisableFlagParsing: true, // all of them are git's
                        RunE: func(cmd *cobra.Command, args []string) error {
                                start(o, c.name, args)
                                return nil
                        },
                })
        }
        root.AddCommand(&cobra.Command{
                Use:   "completion bash|zsh|fish",
                Short: "print the script that completes jdiff's arguments in the shell",
                Long: "Prints the script that completes jdiff's flags and arguments in the shell; after log and diff\n" +
                        "it completes as git does, when git's completion is installed. To use it, e.g.:\n\n" +
                        "  bash: source <(jdiff completion bash)       in ~/.bashrc\n" +
                        "  zsh:  source <(jdiff completion zsh)        in ~/.zshrc, after compinit\n" +
                        "  fish: jdiff completion fish | source        in ~/.config/fish/config.fish\n\n" +
                        "The packages install it, so that this is not needed.",
                ValidArgs: []string{"bash", "zsh", "fish"},
                Args:      cobra.MatchAll(cobra.ExactArgs(1), cobra.OnlyValidArgs),
                RunE: func(cmd *cobra.Command, args []string) error {
                        return printCompletion(cmd.OutOrStdout(), cmd.Root(), args[0])
                },
        })
        // A usage line for each command, jdiff's flags before log and diff, and
        // no "jdiff [command] --help": the help of log and diff is git's.
        usage := strings.Replace(root.UsageTemplate(), "\n  {{.CommandPath}} [command]{{end}}",
                "{{range .Commands}}{{if .IsAvailableCommand}}\n  {{$.CommandPath}} {{if .DisableFlagParsing}}[flags] {{end}}{{.Use}}"+
                        "{{end}}{{end}}{{end}}", 1)
        usage = strings.Replace(usage, "{{if .HasAvailableSubCommands}}\n\nUse \"{{.CommandPath}} [command] --help\" "+
                "for more information about a command.{{end}}", "", 1)
        root.SetUsageTemplate(usage)
        root.SetHelpCommand(&cobra.Command{Hidden: true}) // --help does it
        root.CompletionOptions.DisableDefaultCmd = true   // the one above
        return root
}

// shellParts holds what printCompletion adds to cobra's scripts, git.bash,
// git.zsh and git.fish: after log and diff, git's completion takes over.
//
//go:embed shell
var shellParts embed.FS

// printCompletion writes the completion script of root for shell to w:
// cobra's, then its part of shellParts. zsh calls a completion function by
// the name of its file, _jdiff, so cobra's _jdiff is renamed for the one of
// shellParts to call; jdiff stays bound to _jdiff.
func printCompletion(w io.Writer, root *cobra.Command, shell string) error {
        var b bytes.Buffer
        var err error
        switch shell {
        case "bash":
                err = root.GenBashCompletionV2(&b, true)
        case "zsh":
                err = root.GenZshCompletion(&b)
        case "fish":
                err = root.GenFishCompletion(&b, true)
        }
        if err != nil {
                return err
        }
        script := b.String()
        if shell == "zsh" {
                script = strings.ReplaceAll(script, "_jdiff", "_jdiff_cobra")
                script = strings.Replace(script, "compdef _jdiff_cobra jdiff", "compdef _jdiff jdiff", 1)
        }
        part, err := shellParts.ReadFile("shell/git." + shell)
        if err != nil {
                return err
        }
        _, err = io.WriteString(w, script+"\n"+string(part))
        return err
}

// start shows what mode asks for: the README, git log or git diff with the
// git arguments args, or else the two files or directories of args.
func start(o options, mode string, args []string) {
        if mode == "readme" && !term.IsTerminal(os.Stdout.Fd()) {
                src, err := docs.ReadFile(docPages[0])
                if err != nil {
                        fatal(err)
                }
                fmt.Print(withoutBadges(string(src)))
                return
        }

        dir, err := config.Dir()
        if err != nil {
                dir = "" // no home: defaults only
        }
        cfg, err := config.Load(dir, theme.ReadGitConfig(), os.Getenv("DELTA_FEATURES"), o.features)
        if err != nil {
                fatal(err)
        }
        repo, err := gitsrc.Repo{}.TopLevel()
        if err != nil {
                repo = "" // not in a repository: marks with absolute paths
        }
        if o.untracked {
                cfg.Settings.Untracked = true
        }
        cfg.LoadMarks(repo)

        if mode == "readme" {
                run(ui.WithSizeGuard(cfg, ui.WithSearch(cfg, ui.WithHelp(cfg, ui.NewReadme(cfg, docs, docPages, 0)))))
                return
        }
        var m tea.Model
        switch mode {
        case "log":
                m, err = loadLog(cfg, args)
        case "diff":
                m, err = loadDiff(cfg, args)
        default:
                m, err = load(cfg, args[0], args[1])
        }
        if err != nil {
                fatal(err)
        }
        // d replaces whatever is shown by the files of git diff, which runs in
        // the current directory.
        run(ui.WithSizeGuard(cfg, ui.NewApp(cfg, m, gitDiff).WithAutoReload(gitStatus, gitsrc.Repo{}.WatchPaths, gitsrc.Repo{}.WorkDirs)))
}

// run runs the program that shows page. 16 color mode: colors are drawn as
// ANSI colors, so the terminal palette applies. Terminals without color
// support stay without.
func run(page tea.Model) {
        var opts []tea.ProgramOption
        if colorprofile.Detect(os.Stdout, os.Environ()) > colorprofile.ANSI {
                opts = append(opts, tea.WithColorProfile(colorprofile.ANSI))
        }
        end, err := tea.NewProgram(page, opts...).Run()
        // git commands still running in the background are of no use now: they
        // are killed, which takes a moment.
        if s, ok := end.(interface{ Stop() }); ok {
                s.Stop()
                gitsrc.Wait(time.Second)
        }
        if err != nil {
                fatal(err)
        }
}

// load opens the file list for two directories, as passed by
// git difftool --dir-diff, and the diff view for two files; both reload
// files that change on disk.
func load(cfg *config.Config, oldPath, newPath string) (tea.Model, error) {
        oldInfo, errOld := os.Stat(oldPath)
        newInfo, errNew := os.Stat(newPath)
        if errOld == nil && errNew == nil && oldInfo.IsDir() && newInfo.IsDir() {
                files, err := source.LoadDirs(oldPath, newPath)
                if err != nil {
                        return nil, err
                }
                relist := func(context.Context) ([]source.File, error) { return source.LoadDirs(oldPath, newPath) }
                return ui.NewDiff(cfg, files).WithRelist(relist), nil
        }

        // Path names the file in messages; the panes show the paths as given.
        f := source.File{Path: filepath.Base(newPath), OldFile: oldPath, NewFile: newPath, InOld: true, InNew: true}
        var err error
        if f.Old, f.OldStamp, err = source.Read(oldPath); err != nil {
                return nil, fmt.Errorf("%s: %w", oldPath, err)
        }
        if f.New, f.NewStamp, err = source.Read(newPath); err != nil {
                return nil, fmt.Errorf("%s: %w", newPath, err)
        }
        return ui.NewWatchedFileDiff(cfg, f), nil
}

// loadLog opens the commit list for the commits git log selects with args.
func loadLog(cfg *config.Config, args []string) (tea.Model, error) {
        repo := gitsrc.Repo{}
        commits, err := repo.Log(args)
        if err != nil {
                return nil, err
        }
        if len(commits) == 0 {
                return nil, fmt.Errorf("no commits")
        }
        relist := func(ctx context.Context) (string, []gitsrc.Commit, error) {
                r := repo.WithContext(ctx)
                commits, err := r.Log(args)
                return r.Branch(), commits, err
        }
        files := func(ctx context.Context, c gitsrc.Commit) ([]source.File, error) {
                return repo.WithContext(ctx).Files(c)
        }
        counts := func(ctx context.Context, hashes []string) (map[string]gitsrc.LineCounts, error) {
                return repo.WithContext(ctx).Counts(hashes)
        }
        return ui.NewLog(cfg, repo.Branch(), commits, gitsrc.Reversed(args), files, counts).WithRelist(relist), nil
}

// loadDiff opens the file list for the files git diff compares with
// args; files of the working tree are reloaded when they change on disk.
func loadDiff(cfg *config.Config, args []string) (tea.Model, error) {
        files, err := gitDiff(context.Background(), args, cfg.Settings.Untracked)
        if err != nil {
                return nil, err
        }
        return ui.NewDiff(cfg, files).WithGitDiff(args, gitDiff), nil
}

// gitDiff runs git diff with args, with the untracked files when untracked
// is set; see ui.DiffFunc.
func gitDiff(ctx context.Context, args []string, untracked bool) ([]source.File, error) {
        repo := gitsrc.Repo{}.WithContext(ctx)
        if untracked {
                return repo.DiffUntracked(args)
        }
        return repo.Diff(args)
}

// gitStatus returns the state of the repository for auto reload, see
// gitsrc.Repo.Status.
func gitStatus(ctx context.Context) (string, error) { return gitsrc.Repo{}.WithContext(ctx).Status() }

func fatal(err error) {
        fmt.Fprintln(os.Stderr, "jdiff:", err)
        os.Exit(1)
}

// withoutBadges drops the lines of badges from a page of the documentation:
// images to load are of no use in a terminal.
func withoutBadges(readme string) string {
        var b strings.Builder
        for _, line := range strings.SplitAfter(readme, "\n") {
                if !strings.HasPrefix(line, "[![") {
                        b.WriteString(line)
                }
        }
        return b.String()
}

// printLicense prints jdiff's license and those of the modules linked into it.
func printLicense() {
        fmt.Print(license, "\njdiff is built with these libraries, under their licenses:\n\n", thirdParty)
}

func resolveVersion() string {
        bi, ok := debug.ReadBuildInfo()
        if !ok {
                return "dev"
        }
        var rev, dirty string
        for _, s := range bi.Settings {
                switch s.Key {
                case "vcs.revision":
                        rev = s.Value
                case "vcs.modified":
                        if s.Value == "true" {
                                dirty = "-dirty"
                        }
                }
        }
        if rev == "" {
                return "dev"
        }
        // Main.Version is the tag, or a pseudo-version containing the revision.
        v := strings.TrimSuffix(bi.Main.Version, "+dirty")
        if v == "" || v == "(devel)" || strings.Contains(v, rev[:min(len(rev), 12)]) {
                v = rev[:min(len(rev), 7)]
        }
        return v + dirty
}

//go:build linux

package watch

import (
        "bytes"
        "encoding/binary"
        "fmt"
        "os"
        "sync"

        "golang.org/x/sys/unix"
)

// mask selects the events that change a file's content or replace or
// remove it, and the move of a watched directory; touching its times or mode
// does not.
const mask = unix.IN_MODIFY | unix.IN_CLOSE_WRITE | unix.IN_CREATE | unix.IN_DELETE |
        unix.IN_MOVED_FROM | unix.IN_MOVED_TO | unix.IN_MOVE_SELF | unix.IN_ONLYDIR | unix.IN_EXCL_UNLINK

// inotify is the backend of Linux. All watches share one inotify instance,
// which takes one file descriptor. Its descriptor is used through Control,
// so that close cannot close it meanwhile.
type inotify struct {
        file *os.File
        mu   sync.Mutex
        live map[int32]bool // the watches not removed nor ended
        ch   chan event
        done chan struct{}
        once sync.Once
}

func newBackend() (backend, error) {
        fd, err := unix.InotifyInit1(unix.IN_NONBLOCK | unix.IN_CLOEXEC)
        if err != nil {
                return nil, fmt.Errorf("inotify: %w", err)
        }
        // Non-blocking, the file is read through Go's poller, so that close
        // ends a pending read.
        b := &inotify{file: os.NewFile(uintptr(fd), "inotify"), live: map[int32]bool{},
                ch: make(chan event), done: make(chan struct{})}
        go b.read()
        return b, nil
}

func (b *inotify) add(dir string) (int, error) {
        var wd int
        var err error
        if cerr := b.control(func(fd int) { wd, err = unix.InotifyAddWatch(fd, dir, mask) }); cerr != nil {
                return 0, cerr
        }
        if err != nil {
                return 0, fmt.Errorf("inotify: %s: %w", dir, err)
        }
        b.mu.Lock()
        b.live[int32(wd)] = true
        b.mu.Unlock()
        return wd, nil
}

func (b *inotify) remove(id int) {
        b.mu.Lock()
        live := b.live[int32(id)]
        delete(b.live, int32(id))
        b.mu.Unlock()
        if live {
                b.control(func(fd int) { unix.InotifyRmWatch(fd, uint32(id)) })
        }
}

func (b *inotify) events() <-chan event { return b.ch }

func (b *inotify) close() error {
        err := os.ErrClosed
        b.once.Do(func() {
                close(b.done)
                err = b.file.Close()
        })
        return err
}

// control runs f with the file's descriptor.
func (b *inotify) control(f func(fd int)) error {
        conn, err := b.file.SyscallConn()
        if err != nil {
                return err
        }
        return conn.Control(func(fd uintptr) { f(int(fd)) })
}

// read sends the events the kernel reports, until the file is closed. A
// watch removed is dropped, the end the kernel reports for it too. A
// directory moved away ends its watch, which would follow it.
func (b *inotify) read() {
        defer close(b.ch)
        buf := make([]byte, 64*1024)
        for {
                n, err := b.file.Read(buf)
                if err != nil {
                        return
                }
                for off := 0; off+unix.SizeofInotifyEvent <= n; {
                        wd := int32(binary.NativeEndian.Uint32(buf[off:]))
                        evMask := binary.NativeEndian.Uint32(buf[off+4:])
                        nameLen := int(binary.NativeEndian.Uint32(buf[off+12:]))
                        name := bytes.TrimRight(buf[off+unix.SizeofInotifyEvent:off+unix.SizeofInotifyEvent+nameLen], "\x00")
                        off += unix.SizeofInotifyEvent + nameLen
                        ev, ok := b.event(wd, evMask, string(name))
                        if !ok {
                                continue
                        }
                        select {
                        case b.ch <- ev:
                        case <-b.done:
                                return
                        }
                }
        }
}

// event returns the event of watch wd, with evMask and name, and whether
// there is one.
func (b *inotify) event(wd int32, evMask uint32, name string) (event, bool) {
        if evMask&unix.IN_Q_OVERFLOW != 0 {
                return event{overflow: true}, true
        }
        b.mu.Lock()
        live := b.live[wd]
        ended := live && evMask&(unix.IN_IGNORED|unix.IN_MOVE_SELF) != 0
        if ended {
                delete(b.live, wd)
        }
        b.mu.Unlock()
        switch {
        case !live:
                return event{}, false
        case ended:
                if evMask&unix.IN_MOVE_SELF != 0 {
                        b.control(func(fd int) { unix.InotifyRmWatch(fd, uint32(wd)) })
                }
                return event{id: int(wd)}, true
        case name == "":
                return event{}, false
        }
        return event{id: int(wd), name: name}, true
}

// Package watch reports changes to files as the system sees them, instead
// of checking them over and over. It watches the directories the files are
// in, not the files: editors often save by writing a new file and renaming
// it over the old one, which a watch on the old file would not see, and only
// a directory sees a file appear. Only these directories are watched, not
// their subdirectories.
//
// A Watcher watches the directories of pinned files always, and of the others
// at most a number. When more are wanted, the most recently used ones are
// watched, see Watched, and the callers check the others themselves. A
// directory is used when something in it changes and when Touch says so;
// before either, at the time it or its newest file was modified.
//
// What a system offers is its backend. Only Linux (inotify) has one; elsewhere
// New fails with ErrUnsupported and the callers check the files themselves.
// Another system needs only a backend of its own (backend_<os>.go).
package watch

import (
        "cmp"
        "errors"
        "io/fs"
        "maps"
        "os"
        "path/filepath"
        "slices"
        "sync"
        "time"
)

// ErrUnsupported is returned where the system cannot report changes.
var ErrUnsupported = errors.New("not supported on this system")

// settle is how long changes are collected after the first one, so that
// the events of one save come as one batch.
const settle = 50 * time.Millisecond

// backend watches single directories, without their subdirectories. Its
// methods may be called from any goroutine; add and remove must not wait for
// the events to be taken.
type backend interface {
        // add starts watching dir and returns the id of its events. A missing
        // dir is an error matching fs.ErrNotExist.
        add(dir string) (int, error)
        // remove stops watching id: no event of it comes after.
        remove(id int)
        // events returns the events, closed after close or when the system
        // stops reporting.
        events() <-chan event
        close() error
}

// event is a change of the entry name of the directory watched as id. With
// name "", the directory itself is gone or moved away, and its watch ended.
// With overflow, the system lost events; id is not set.
type event struct {
        id       int
        name     string
        overflow bool
}

// Watcher reports changes to files and directories. Its methods may be called
// from any goroutine.
type Watcher struct {
        b backend

        mu     sync.Mutex
        limit  int                  // the most directories watched
        files  map[string][]string  // SetFiles, see watched
        pinned map[string][]string  // Pin, see watched
        whole  map[string]bool      // SetDirs: all their entries count
        used   map[string]time.Time // the directories wanted, but pinned ones, and when they were used
        ids    map[int]string       // watch id -> directory
        dirIDs map[string]int       // watched directory -> watch id
        now    func() time.Time     // the time of a use, time.Now but in tests

        raw     chan string
        changes chan []string
        done    chan struct{}
        once    sync.Once
}

// New returns a watcher of at most limit directories, watching none yet. It
// fails when the system cannot report changes.
func New(limit int) (*Watcher, error) {
        b, err := newBackend()
        if err != nil {
                return nil, err
        }
        return newWatcher(b, limit), nil
}

// newWatcher returns a watcher of at most limit directories with b.
func newWatcher(b backend, limit int) *Watcher {
        w := &Watcher{b: b, limit: limit, used: map[string]time.Time{}, ids: map[int]string{}, dirIDs: map[string]int{}, now: time.Now,
                raw: make(chan string), changes: make(chan []string), done: make(chan struct{})}
        go w.read()
        go w.batch()
        return w
}

// SetFiles watches the files at paths, in place of those set before. A
// change of one is reported by its path as given; other entries of their
// directories are not reported. It returns the first error of the system,
// other than for a missing directory, which is left out; the directories
// that could be watched are.
func (w *Watcher) SetFiles(paths []string) error {
        names := watched(paths)
        modified := newest(names)
        w.mu.Lock()
        defer w.mu.Unlock()
        w.files = names
        w.want(modified)
        return w.apply()
}

// Pin watches the files at paths as SetFiles does, in place of those pinned
// before, but always: their directories do not count towards the limit.
func (w *Watcher) Pin(paths []string) error {
        names := watched(paths)
        w.mu.Lock()
        defer w.mu.Unlock()
        w.pinned = names
        return w.apply()
}

// SetDirs watches the directories of dirs, in place of those set before, with
// the time each was last modified. A change of any entry is reported by the
// directory joined with its name. It returns errors as SetFiles does.
func (w *Watcher) SetDirs(dirs map[string]time.Time) error {
        cleaned := map[string]time.Time{}
        for d, t := range dirs {
                cleaned[filepath.Clean(d)] = t
        }
        w.mu.Lock()
        defer w.mu.Unlock()
        w.whole = map[string]bool{}
        for d := range cleaned {
                w.whole[d] = true
        }
        w.want(cleaned)
        return w.apply()
}

// Touch counts the directories of the files at paths, those of symlink
// targets too, as used now: the most recent ones are watched.
func (w *Watcher) Touch(paths []string) error {
        ds := dirs(watched(paths))
        w.mu.Lock()
        defer w.mu.Unlock()
        now := w.now()
        for _, d := range ds {
                if _, ok := w.used[d]; ok {
                        w.used[d] = now
                }
        }
        return w.apply()
}

// SetLimit sets the most directories watched besides those of pinned files;
// with 0 only those are.
func (w *Watcher) SetLimit(limit int) error {
        w.mu.Lock()
        defer w.mu.Unlock()
        w.limit = limit
        return w.apply()
}

// Watched reports whether a change of the file at path is reported now: its
// directory is watched, and so is that of its target when it is a symlink.
func (w *Watcher) Watched(path string) bool {
        names := watched([]string{path})
        w.mu.Lock()
        defer w.mu.Unlock()
        for name := range names {
                if _, ok := w.dirIDs[filepath.Dir(name)]; !ok {
                        return false
                }
        }
        return true
}

// Changes returns the channel of changed paths, in sorted batches. It is
// closed when the watcher is, and when the system stops reporting.
func (w *Watcher) Changes() <-chan []string { return w.changes }

// Close stops watching.
func (w *Watcher) Close() error {
        err := os.ErrClosed
        w.once.Do(func() {
                close(w.done)
                err = w.b.close()
        })
        return err
}

// want adds the directories of modified, at the time given, to those wanted
// that are not yet.
func (w *Watcher) want(modified map[string]time.Time) {
        for d, t := range modified {
                if _, ok := w.used[d]; !ok {
                        w.used[d] = t
                }
        }
}

// apply watches the pinned directories and the most recently used others, as
// many as the limit allows, and no others: those no longer wanted are dropped.
// A missing directory takes no place. It returns the first error of the
// system other than for a missing directory. w.mu is held.
func (w *Watcher) apply() error {
        wanted := map[string]bool{}
        for d := range w.whole {
                wanted[d] = true
        }
        for _, d := range dirs(w.files) {
                wanted[d] = true
        }
        maps.DeleteFunc(w.used, func(d string, _ time.Time) bool { return !wanted[d] })
        pins := dirs(w.pinned)
        order := slices.Clone(pins)
        others := slices.Collect(maps.Keys(w.used))
        slices.SortFunc(others, func(a, b string) int {
                if c := w.used[b].Compare(w.used[a]); c != 0 {
                        return c
                }
                return cmp.Compare(a, b)
        })
        for _, d := range others {
                if !slices.Contains(order, d) {
                        order = append(order, d)
                }
        }
        // The watches beyond the limit go first, so that the system's limit
        // is not passed on the way.
        keep := order[:min(len(order), len(pins)+max(w.limit, 0))]
        for d, id := range w.dirIDs {
                if !slices.Contains(keep, d) {
                        w.b.remove(id)
                        delete(w.ids, id)
                        delete(w.dirIDs, d)
                }
        }
        var first error
        n := 0 // the others watched
        for _, d := range order {
                pinned := slices.Contains(pins, d)
                if !pinned && n >= w.limit {
                        break
                }
                if _, ok := w.dirIDs[d]; ok {
                        if !pinned {
                                n++
                        }
                        continue
                }
                id, err := w.b.add(d)
                if err != nil {
                        if !errors.Is(err, fs.ErrNotExist) && first == nil {
                                first = err
                        }
                        continue
                }
                w.ids[id], w.dirIDs[d] = d, id
                if !pinned {
                        n++
                }
        }
        return first
}

// read hands the paths of the backend's events to batch, until the watcher
// is closed or the backend ends.
func (w *Watcher) read() {
        defer close(w.raw)
        for ev := range w.b.events() {
                for _, p := range w.paths(ev) {
                        select {
                        case w.raw <- p:
                        case <-w.done:
                                return
                        }
                }
        }
}

// paths returns the paths ev reports. A change counts as use of its
// directory. When events were lost, all paths count as changed. When a
// watched directory is gone or moved away, as when a checkout removes and
// makes it again, its paths count as changed, and the directory at its path
// is watched again when there is one: else it is not watched, which Watched
// tells.
func (w *Watcher) paths(ev event) []string {
        w.mu.Lock()
        defer w.mu.Unlock()
        if ev.overflow {
                var out []string
                for _, names := range []map[string][]string{w.files, w.pinned} {
                        for _, ps := range names {
                                out = append(out, ps...)
                        }
                }
                return append(out, slices.Collect(maps.Keys(w.whole))...)
        }
        dir, ok := w.ids[ev.id]
        if !ok {
                return nil // removed meanwhile
        }
        if ev.name == "" {
                delete(w.ids, ev.id)
                delete(w.dirIDs, dir)
                var out []string
                for _, names := range []map[string][]string{w.files, w.pinned} {
                        for p, ps := range names {
                                if filepath.Dir(p) == dir {
                                        out = append(out, ps...)
                                }
                        }
                }
                if w.whole[dir] {
                        out = append(out, dir)
                }
                w.apply() // what it cannot watch, Watched tells
                return out
        }
        if _, ok := w.used[dir]; ok {
                w.used[dir] = w.now()
        }
        p := filepath.Join(dir, ev.name)
        out := append(slices.Clone(w.files[p]), w.pinned[p]...)
        if w.whole[dir] {
                out = append(out, p)
        }
        return out
}

// batch collects the paths from raw for settle after the first one and
// sends them on as one batch, until raw is closed; the batch collected
// then is still sent.
func (w *Watcher) batch() {
        defer close(w.changes)
        for open := true; open; {
                p, ok := <-w.raw
                if !ok {
                        return
                }
                set := map[string]bool{p: true}
                timer := time.NewTimer(settle)
        collect:
                for {
                        select {
                        case p, ok := <-w.raw:
                                if !ok {
                                        timer.Stop()
                                        open = false
                                        break collect
                                }
                                set[p] = true
                        case <-timer.C:
                                break collect
                        }
                }
                select {
                case w.changes <- slices.Sorted(maps.Keys(set)):
                case <-w.done:
                        return
                }
        }
}

// watched maps each path the system names to the paths it was given as: a
// path stands for itself and, when it is a symlink, for its target, which
// is what git difftool --dir-diff puts in place of working tree files.
func watched(paths []string) map[string][]string {
        names := map[string][]string{}
        for _, p := range paths {
                p = filepath.Clean(p)
                names[p] = append(names[p], p)
                if target, err := filepath.EvalSymlinks(p); err == nil && target != p {
                        names[target] = append(names[target], p)
                }
        }
        return names
}

// dirs returns the directories of the paths of names, sorted.
func dirs(names map[string][]string) []string {
        var out []string
        for p := range names {
                out = append(out, filepath.Dir(p))
        }
        slices.Sort(out)
        return slices.Compact(out)
}

// newest returns the directories of the paths of names with the time their
// newest file there was modified; a file that cannot be read counts as never.
func newest(names map[string][]string) map[string]time.Time {
        out := map[string]time.Time{}
        for p := range names {
                d := filepath.Dir(p)
                t := out[d]
                if info, err := os.Stat(p); err == nil && info.ModTime().After(t) {
                        t = info.ModTime()
                }
                out[d] = t
        }
        return out
}
